Al-Tirmidhī narrated this and graded it authentic.

Yet authentic reports also establish the use of oaths such as “By Allah’s might,” “By Allah’s life,” and similar expressions. Muslims unanimously agree that these do not constitute the prohibited practice of swearing by anything other than Allah.

No Muslim disputes seeking relief in the sense of asking the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, for what befits his office. Whoever disputes this meaning is either an unbeliever—if he denies something whose denial constitutes disbelief—or mistaken and misguided. As for seeking relief in the sense that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, denied, this too must be denied. Whoever attributes to anyone other than Allah what belongs to Allah alone is likewise an unbeliever if the proof whose rejection constitutes disbelief has been established against him.

In this connection, Abū Yazīd al-Bistāmī said:

“A created being seeking relief from another created being is like a drowning person seeking relief from another drowning person.”

Shaykh Abū ‘Abd Allāh al-Qurashī, well known in Egypt, likewise said:

“A created being seeking relief from another created being is like a prisoner seeking relief from another prisoner.”

Among the supplications of Moses, peace be upon him, are the words:

“O Allah, to You belongs all praise; to You complaints are brought. You are the One whose help is sought, from You relief is sought, and upon You we rely. There is no power nor strength except through You.”

Since this is the meaning understood from seeking relief when the expression is used without qualification, and since this meaning belongs exclusively to Allah, it is correct to deny it, without qualification, of anyone other than Him. Thus, no imam of the Muslims is known to have permitted seeking relief from anyone other than Allah in an unrestricted sense, or to have objected to anyone who denied seeking relief from anyone other than Allah in that unrestricted sense.

---

## Page 115

Likewise, seeking relief includes a form that is appropriate only to Allah, as indicated by His words:

“You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help.”

For only Allah can provide help in worship in its absolute sense. A created being may, however, be asked for help with what lies within its power. The same applies to seeking support. Allah Most High says:

“And if they seek your help in religion, you must help them.”

Support in its absolute sense consists in creating the means by which the enemy is overcome, and only Allah has the power to do this.

Whoever opposes what is established by the Quran and Sunnah is either an unbeliever, a transgressor, or a sinner—unless he is a believer who exercises independent judgment but errs, in which case he is rewarded for his effort and forgiven for his error. The same applies if he has not received the knowledge by which the proof would be established against him, for Allah says:

“We never punish until We have sent a messenger.”

But if the proof established by the Quran and Sunnah has been established against him and he opposes it, he is punished accordingly, either by execution or by a lesser punishment. And Allah knows best.

---

## Page 116

Shaykh al-Islām also said:

Section

Allah called the deities they worshipped besides Him intercessors, just as He called them partners in several passages. He said in Surah Yūnus:

“They worship besides Allah what neither harms nor benefits them, and say, ‘These are our intercessors with Allah.’ Say, ‘Do you inform Allah of something He does not know in the heavens or on earth?’ Glory be to Him! Exalted is He above what they associate with Him.”

He also said:

“Or have they taken intercessors besides Allah? Say, ‘Even though they possess nothing and have no understanding?’”

“Say, ‘All intercession belongs to Allah.’”

“On the Day the Hour comes, the evildoers will despair.”

“None of those they associated with Allah will be intercessors for them.”

He mentioned both associating partners with Him and intercession in His words:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim to be gods besides Allah. They do not possess even an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth, nor do they have any share in either, nor does He have any helper among them. Intercession with Him benefits none except those for whom He gives permission.’”

These are the four grounds on which they might rely. The first is ownership of anything, however small. The second is their having a share in any part of dominion. Thus, there is neither ownership, nor partnership, nor assistance by which one could become a rival to Him.

When these three are ruled out, intercession remains; He therefore made it dependent on His will.

---

## Page 117

He also said:

“How many angels there are in the heavens whose intercession avails nothing!”

He also said, in this verse and the one that follows:

“Say, ‘Call upon those you claim to be gods besides Him. They have no power to remove harm from you or to change it.’”

He said concerning their taking them as a means of drawing near to Allah:

“We worship them only so that they may bring us nearer to Allah.”

He also said:

“Why, then, did those they had taken as gods besides Allah, as a means of drawing near to Him, not help them? Rather, they vanished from them. That was their falsehood and what they used to fabricate.”

---

## Page 118

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah have mercy on him—also said:

Section: On the Intercession Denied in the Quran

Examples include the words of Allah Most High:

“And fear a Day when no soul will avail another in any way, no intercession will be accepted from it, and no ransom will be taken from it.”

And His words:

“No ransom will be accepted from it, nor will intercession benefit it.”

And His words:

“Before a Day comes when there will be no bargaining, friendship, or intercession.”

And His words:

“So we have no intercessors,
nor any close friend.”

And His words:

“The wrongdoers will have neither a close friend nor an intercessor whose plea is heeded.”

And His words:

“On the Day its fulfilment comes, those who had forgotten it before will say, ‘The messengers of our Lord certainly came with the truth. Have we any intercessors who will intercede for us? Or could we be sent back, so that we might act differently from the way we used to act?’”

There are other passages like these. The Khawārij and the Muʿtazilah cited many of them as evidence against intercession for those who commit major sins. They denied that intercession could be made for anyone deserving punishment, or that anyone who entered the Fire could leave it. They did not, however, deny intercession for those entitled to reward, whereby their reward would be increased.

The position of the early generations of the Muslim community, its leading scholars, and all the adherents of Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamāʿah is to affirm intercession for those who commit major sins and to hold that anyone with an atom’s weight of faith in his heart will be brought out of the Fire.

Furthermore, the widely transmitted hadiths from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, concerning intercession describe those gathered for judgment seeking intercession so that judgment may be passed between them. Among them are believers and disbelievers, and this involves

---

## Page 119

a form of intercession for disbelievers.

Moreover, it is reported in the Sahīh that al-‘Abbās ibn ‘Abd al-Muttalib said: “O Messenger of Allah, have you benefited Abū Tālib in any way? He used to protect you and become angry on your behalf.” He replied: “Yes. He is in a shallow pool of fire. Were it not for me, he would be in the lowest depth of the Fire.”

‘Abd Allah ibn al-Hārith reported: “I heard al-‘Abbās say, ‘I said: O Messenger of Allah, Abū Tālib used to protect and support you. Has that benefited him?’ He replied: ‘Yes. I found him in the depths of the Fire and brought him out into a shallow pool.’”

Abū Sa‘īd al-Khudrī, may Allah be pleased with him, reported that when Abū Tālib, the uncle of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was mentioned in his presence, he said:

“Perhaps my intercession will benefit him on the Day of Resurrection, so that he will be placed in a shallow pool of fire reaching his ankles, which will cause his brain to boil.”

This is an authentic, explicit text establishing his intercession for some disbelievers so that their punishment may be lightened—indeed, so that one may receive the lightest punishment of all the inhabitants of the Fire. As is also reported in the Sahīh, Ibn ‘Abbās narrated that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Abū Tālib receives the lightest punishment of all the inhabitants of the Fire. He wears a pair of sandals that cause his brain to boil.”

Abū Sa‘īd al-Khudrī reported that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The one who receives the least punishment among the inhabitants of the Fire wears a pair of sandals of fire, and his brain boils from the heat of his sandals.”

Al-Nu‘mān ibn Bashīr said: “I heard the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, say:

‘The one who receives the lightest punishment among the inhabitants of the Fire on the Day of Resurrection is a man beneath the arches of whose feet two burning coals are placed, causing his brain to boil.’”

He also reported that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The one who receives the lightest punishment among the inhabitants of the Fire has two sandals and two straps of fire, which cause his brain to boil as a cauldron boils. He thinks that no one suffers more severely than he does, yet his punishment is the lightest of them all.”

---

## Page 120

This second question weakens the answer of those who interpret the denial of intercession as referring to intercession for disbelievers, and who take the wrongdoers to be the disbelievers. . . (1)

The response is as follows:

The intercession being denied is what people ordinarily understand by intercession when the term is used without qualification: an intercessor approaches another on his own initiative, and that person accepts his intercession. But if that person gives him permission to intercede, and he then intercedes, he is not acting independently. Rather, he is obeying that person—that is, following his direction in interceding. His intercession is accepted, while the entire matter rests with the one who gives the command and to whom the petition is addressed.

The explicit text of the Quran establishes in several verses that no one intercedes with Him except by His permission. As He, the Exalted, says:

“Who is it that can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

And He says:

“Intercession does not benefit with Him except for one to whom He gives permission.”

And He says:

“They do not intercede except for those with whom He is pleased.”

There are other verses to the same effect.

What makes clear that this is the intercession being denied is His statement:

“And warn with it those who fear that they will be gathered before their Lord—with no protector or intercessor apart from Him—so that they may become mindful of Him.”

And He, the Exalted, says:

“Allah is the One who created the heavens and the earth and everything between them in six days, then rose above the Throne. You have no protector or intercessor apart from Him.”

Thus, He states that they have no protector or intercessor apart from Allah.

As for the denial of intercession without His permission, intercession performed with His permission is not apart from Him, just as guardianship exercised with His permission is not apart from Him. As He, the Exalted, says:

“Your guardian is only Allah, His Messenger, and those who believe—those who establish prayer, give zakah, and bow down. Whoever takes Allah, His Messenger, and those who believe as guardians—then indeed, the party of Allah will prevail.”

He also says:

“Or have they taken intercessors apart from Allah? Say, ‘Even though they do not possess

> **Footnotes:**
> (1) Blank in the original.

---

## Page 121

anything and do not reason?’”

“Say, ‘To Allah belongs all intercession. To Him belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth.’”

Thus, He censures those who take intercessors apart from Allah and declares that all intercession belongs to Allah. It is therefore clear that intercession is denied to anyone other than Him, since no one intercedes except by His permission, and such intercession belongs to Him.

He also says:

“They worship apart from Allah what neither harms nor benefits them, and they say, ‘These are our intercessors with Allah.’ Say, ‘Do you inform Allah of something He does not know in the heavens or on the earth?’ Glory be to Him! Exalted is He above what they associate with Him.”

This is further clarified by His denial of friendship on that Day in His statement:

“Before there comes a Day in which there will be no trade, no friendship, and no intercession. And the disbelievers are the wrongdoers.”

It is understood that He denies only friendship as it is ordinarily known, together with its customary benefit—as one friend benefits another in this world. As He says:

“And what will make you know what the Day of Recompense is? Then, what will make you know what the Day of Recompense is? A Day when no soul will have power to do anything for another, and all command that Day will belong to Allah.”

And He says:

“To warn of the Day of Meeting—the Day when they will stand exposed, with nothing about them hidden from Allah. To whom belongs sovereignty today? To Allah, the One, the All-Prevailing.”

He does not deny that there will be friendship in the Hereafter that brings benefit by His permission, for He says:

“Close friends, that Day, will be enemies to one another, except the God-fearing. O My servants, no fear will there be concerning you today, nor will you grieve.”

And the subsequent verses.

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, also said that Allah, the Exalted, says:

“My love is assured for those who love one another for My sake.”

And Allah, the Exalted, says:

“Where are those who love one another for the sake of My majesty? Today I shall shade them in My shade, on a Day when there is no shade but My shade.”

It is therefore clear that the entire matter comes back to truly affirming Allah’s oneness: no one brings benefit or harm except by Allah’s permission; no one other than Allah may be worshipped, nor may anyone’s help be sought apart from Allah. On the Day of Resurrection, it will become apparent to all creation that the entire matter belongs to Allah, and every claimant will disassociate himself from

---

## Page 122

his false claim, so that no one will remain who claims for himself or anyone else a share alongside Allah in His lordship or divinity. This is unlike this world: although there is no Lord or God but Him, others have been taken as lords and gods, and people have made such claims.

In this world, a person intercedes with another, and someone benefits from his intercession even though he has not been given permission to intercede. A close friend may come to someone’s aid, and a person may ransom himself from harm. Thus, people and wealth can bring benefit in this world: people may benefit others either independently or by assisting them—which is intercession—while wealth may benefit them through ransom. Allah has negated all three of these forms.

He, the Exalted, says:

“No soul will avail another in any way, nor will intercession be accepted from it, nor will a ransom be taken from it.”

And He says:

“There will be no trade, no friendship, and no intercession.”

Likewise, He says:

“No father will avail his child, nor will a child avail his father in any way.”

Such, then, is the matter, and Allah knows best. Thus, the intercession that Allah has negated ultimately relates to establishing the two foundations of faith: belief in Allah and in the Last Day—Allah’s oneness and the return to Him. He has paired these in many passages, as in His statement:

“Among the people are those who say, ‘We believe in Allah and in the Last Day.’”

And His statement:

“Those who, when a calamity befalls them, say, ‘Indeed, we belong to Allah, and indeed, to Him we will return.’”

And His statement:

“Your creation and your resurrection are only like that of a single soul.”

And His statement:

“You were lifeless, and He gave you life; then He will cause you to die, then bring you back to life, and then to Him you will be returned.”

And other passages like these.

---

## Page 123

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah sanctify his soul—was asked about two men who debated, one of whom said:

“We must have an intermediary between us and Allah, for we cannot reach Him otherwise.”

He replied:

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of the worlds.

If he means that there must be an intermediary who conveys Allah’s commands to us, then this is true. For people cannot know what Allah loves and is pleased with, what He has commanded and forbidden, the honour He has prepared for His allies, or the punishment He has promised His enemies, except through the messengers whom Allah has sent to His servants. Nor can they otherwise know the most beautiful names and sublime attributes that rightfully belong to Allah Most High—attributes beyond the knowledge of human reason—or other such matters.

Those who believe in the messengers and follow them are the rightly guided, whom Allah draws near to Himself, raises in rank, and honours in this world and the Hereafter. As for those who oppose the messengers, they are accursed, astray and veiled from their Lord.

He, the Exalted, says:

“O children of Adam, if messengers from among you come to you, relating My verses to you, then whoever fears Allah and reforms—no fear will there be concerning them, nor will they grieve.”

“But those who deny Our verses and are too arrogant to accept them—those are the companions of the Fire; they will abide therein forever.”

And He, the Exalted, says:

“If guidance comes to you from Me, then whoever follows My guidance will neither go astray nor suffer.”

“But whoever

---

## Page 124

turns away from My remembrance will have a life of hardship.”

“And We will raise him blind on the Day of Resurrection.”

“He will say, ‘My Lord, why have You raised me blind when I once could see?’”

“He will say, ‘Thus did Our signs come to you, but you forgot them; and thus will you be forgotten today.’”

Ibn ʿAbbās said: Allah has guaranteed that whoever reads the Quran and acts upon its teachings will neither go astray in this world nor suffer in the Hereafter.

He, the Exalted, says of the people of the Fire:

“Whenever a group is cast into it, its keepers will ask them, ‘Did no warner come to you?’”

“They will say, ‘Yes, a warner did come to us, but we denied him and said, “Allah has revealed nothing; you are merely in great error.”’”

And He, the Exalted, says:

“Those who disbelieved will be driven to Hell in groups. When they reach it, its gates will be opened, and its keepers will say to them, ‘Did messengers from among yourselves not come to you, reciting your Lord’s verses to you and warning you of the meeting of this Day of yours?’ They will say, ‘Yes, but the decree of punishment has come into effect against the disbelievers.’”

And He, the Exalted, says:

“We send the messengers only as bearers of glad tidings and as warners. Whoever believes and reforms—no fear will there be concerning them, nor will they grieve.”

“But those who deny Our signs will be afflicted with punishment because of their disobedience.”

And He, the Exalted, says:

“Indeed, We have revealed to you as We revealed to Noah and the prophets after him. We also revealed to Abraham, Ishmael, Isaac, Jacob, the descendants, Jesus, Job, Jonah, Aaron and Solomon; and to David We gave the Psalms.”

“And there are messengers whose stories We have already related to you, and messengers whose stories We have not related to you. And Allah spoke directly to Moses.”

“Messengers bearing glad tidings and warnings, so that people would have no argument against Allah after the coming of the messengers.”

There are many passages like these in the Quran. This is a matter on which all the religious communities—Muslims, Jews and Christians—are agreed: they affirm intermediaries between Allah and His servants, namely the messengers who conveyed on Allah’s behalf

---

## Page 125

His commands and what He has made known.

He, the Exalted, says:

“Allah chooses messengers from among the angels and from among humankind.”

Whoever denies these intermediaries is a disbeliever by the consensus of all the religious communities.

The surahs that Allah revealed in Mecca, such as al-Anʿām, al-Aʿrāf, those beginning with (Alif Lām Rā), (Hā Mīm), and (Tā Sīn), and others like them, contain the foundations of religion, such as belief in Allah, His messengers, and the Last Day. Allah has recounted the stories of the disbelievers who rejected the messengers, describing how He destroyed them and granted victory to His messengers and those who believed.

He, the Exalted, says:

“Our word has already gone forth to Our servants, the messengers:
They will surely be given victory,
And Our forces will surely prevail.”

And He says:

“Indeed, We will support Our messengers and those who believe in the life of this world and on the Day when the witnesses arise.”

These intermediaries are to be obeyed and followed, and their example emulated. As He, the Exalted, says:

“We sent no messenger except to be obeyed by Allah’s permission.”

And He, the Exalted, says:

“Whoever obeys the Messenger has obeyed Allah.”

And He, the Exalted, says:

“Say, ‘If you love Allah, then follow me; Allah will love you.’”

And He says:

“So those who believe in him, honour him, support him, and follow the light sent down with him—it is they who will prosper.”

And He, the Exalted, says:

“Indeed, in the Messenger of Allah you have an excellent example, for whoever hopes in Allah and the Last Day and remembers Allah often.”

But if by an intermediary he means that an intermediary is necessary to bring benefits and ward off harm—for example, someone who serves as an intermediary in providing sustenance, victory, and guidance to people, whom they ask for these things and to whom they look in hope of obtaining them—then this is among the gravest forms of polytheism, on account of which Allah declared the polytheists to be disbelievers. They took protectors and intercessors besides Allah, seeking through them to obtain benefits and avoid harm. Yet intercession is only for those to whom Allah grants permission for it. He even says:

“Allah is the One who created the heavens

---

## Page 126

and the earth and all that is between them in six days, then rose over the Throne. You have no protector or intercessor besides Him. Will you not then take heed?”

He, the Exalted, says:

“And warn with it those who fear being gathered before their Lord: they will have no protector or intercessor besides Him.”

And He says:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim to be gods besides Him. They have no power to remove harm from you or divert it.’”

“Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of drawing near to their Lord, seeking to be nearest to Him. They hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Indeed, your Lord’s punishment is something to beware of.”

And He says:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim to be gods besides Allah. They do not possess even an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth, nor do they have any share in either, nor does He have any helper among them.’”

“Intercession before Him benefits none except those for whom He has given permission.”

Some of the early generations said: There were people who invoked the Messiah, Ezra, and the angels. Allah therefore made clear to them that the angels and prophets had no power to remove harm from them or divert it, and that they themselves sought to draw near to Allah, hoped for His mercy, and feared His punishment.

He, the Exalted, says:

“It is not for any human being to whom Allah has given the Scripture, judgment, and prophethood to then say to people, ‘Be my worshippers instead of Allah’s.’ Rather, he would say, ‘Be people devoted to the Lord through your teaching of the Scripture and your study of it.’”

“Nor would he command you to take the angels and prophets as lords. Would he command you to disbelieve after you have become Muslims?”

Thus He, glorified be He, made clear that taking the angels and prophets as lords is disbelief.

Whoever makes the angels and prophets intermediaries, invoking them, relying upon them, and asking them to bring benefits and ward off harm—for example, asking them to forgive sins, guide hearts, relieve distress, and meet needs—is a disbeliever by the consensus of the Muslims.

He, the Exalted, has said:

“They say, ‘The Most Merciful has taken a child.’ Glory be to Him! Rather, they are honoured servants.”

---

## Page 127

“They do not speak before He does, and they act at His command.”

“He knows what is before them and what is behind them. They intercede only for those whom He approves, and they stand in awe of Him.”

“Whoever among them says, ‘I am a god besides Him,’ We will repay with Hell. Thus do We repay the wrongdoers.”

He, the Exalted, says:

“The Messiah would never disdain to be a servant of Allah, nor would the angels brought near to Him. Whoever disdains His worship and is arrogant—He will gather them all to Himself.”

He, the Exalted, says:

“They say, ‘The Most Merciful has taken a child.’”

“You have certainly uttered something monstrous.”

“The heavens almost rupture at it, the earth splits apart, and the mountains collapse in ruins,”

“because they attribute a child to the Most Merciful.”

“It is not fitting for the Most Merciful to take a child.”

“There is no one in the heavens or on earth who will not come to the Most Merciful as a servant.”

“He has certainly accounted for them and counted them precisely.”

“Each of them will come to Him alone on the Day of Resurrection.”

He, the Exalted, says:

“They worship besides Allah what can neither harm nor benefit them, and they say, ‘These are our intercessors with Allah.’ Say, ‘Are you informing Allah of something He does not know in the heavens or on earth?’ Glory be to Him! Exalted is He above what they associate with Him.”

He, the Exalted, says:

“How many angels there are in the heavens whose intercession is of no avail except after Allah gives permission for whomever He wills and approves.”

He, the Exalted, says:

“Who can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

And He says:

“If Allah touches you with harm, none can remove it but Him; and if He intends good for you, none can repel His bounty.”

He, the Exalted, says:

“Whatever mercy Allah grants to people, none can withhold; and whatever He withholds, none can release thereafter.”

He, the Exalted, says:

“Say, ‘Have you considered those whom you invoke besides Allah? If Allah intends harm for me, can they remove His harm? Or if He intends mercy for me, can they withhold His mercy?’ Say, ‘Allah is sufficient for me. In Him those who trust place their trust.’”

There are many passages like this in the Quran.

As for those other than the prophets—scholars and religious teachers—whoever regards them as intermediaries between

---

## Page 128

the Messenger and his community—conveying his message to them, teaching them, cultivating their conduct, and serving as examples for them to follow—is correct in this. When these scholars reach a consensus, their consensus is conclusive proof: they do not agree upon misguidance. If they disagree over something, they refer it back to Allah and the Messenger, for no individual among them is absolutely infallible. Rather, some of anyone’s words may be accepted and others rejected, except those of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The scholars are the heirs of the prophets. The prophets did not leave behind dinars or dirhams, but only knowledge. Whoever acquires it has acquired an abundant share.”

But if someone regards them as intermediaries between Allah and His creation—like the chamberlains between a king and his subjects—such that they present the needs of His creation to Allah, and Allah guides and provides for His servants only through their mediation, with people asking them and them asking Allah, then this resembles intermediaries at royal courts. Because of their closeness to the kings, they ask the kings to meet people’s needs. People ask these intermediaries out of courtesy, rather than petitioning the king directly, or because asking the intermediaries is more beneficial to them than asking the king, since the intermediaries are closer to the king than those seeking to have their needs met.

Whoever regards them as intermediaries in this manner is an unbeliever who associates partners with Allah. He must be called upon to repent; if he repents, well and good, but otherwise he is to be put to death. These people liken Allah to others: they have likened the created to the Creator and set up rivals to Allah. The Quran contains more refutations of these people than this fatwa has room to present.

For intermediaries between kings and people serve in one of three ways:

—Either to inform the kings of circumstances among the people of which they are unaware.

---

## Page 129

And whoever says that Allah does not know His servants’ circumstances until some angel, prophet, or other being informs Him of them is an unbeliever. Rather, He—glory be to Him—knows what is secret and what is even more hidden. Nothing in the earth or the heavens is hidden from Him:

“He is the All-Hearing, the All-Seeing.”

He hears the clamour of voices in different languages expressing their diverse needs. Hearing one does not distract Him from hearing another; requests do not confuse Him, nor does He grow weary of those who persist in asking.

The second way is that a king cannot govern his subjects or repel his enemies without helpers to assist him. He therefore needs supporters and helpers because of his weakness and inability. Allah—glory be to Him—has no helper, nor does He need a protector on account of weakness. He, the Most High, says:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim to be gods besides Allah. They possess not an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth, nor do they have any share in either, nor does He have any helper among them.’”

And He, the Most High, says:

“And say, ‘Praise be to Allah, who has never taken a son, who has no partner in His dominion, and who has no protector out of weakness.’ And proclaim His greatness.”

Every means that exists has Him as its Creator, Lord and Sovereign. He has no need of anything besides Himself, while everything besides Him is in need of Him. This is unlike kings, who need their helpers and whose helpers are, in reality, their partners in sovereignty.

Allah, the Most High, has no partner in His dominion. Rather, there is no god but Allah alone, with no partner. To Him belongs all dominion and all praise, and He has power over all things.

The third way is that a king has no desire to benefit his subjects, treat them kindly, or show them mercy unless some external influence moves him. Thus, when the king speaks to someone who advises and honours him, or someone who presumes upon his closeness to him—someone in whom the king places hope and whom he fears—the king’s will is stirred

---

## Page 130

and his resolve to meet his subjects’ needs, either because of the effect upon his heart of the words of an adviser, admonisher or counsellor, or because of the hope or fear aroused by the words of someone who presumes upon his closeness to him.

Allah, the Most High, however, is the Lord and Sovereign of all things. He is more merciful to His servants than a mother is to her child. Everything comes into being only by His will: whatever He wills happens, and whatever He does not will does not happen. When He causes His servants to benefit one another—making one show kindness to another, supplicate for him, have his intercession on his behalf accepted, and so forth—it is He who creates all of this. It is He who creates in the heart of that benefactor, supplicant and intercessor the desire to show kindness, supplicate and intercede. It is impossible for there to be anyone in existence who compels Him to act against His will, teaches Him what He did not know, or is someone on whom the Lord places hope or whom He fears. Thus the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Let none of you say, ‘O Allah, forgive me if You will; O Allah, have mercy on me if You will.’ Rather, let him ask firmly, for no one can compel Him.”

Those who intercede with Him do so only with His permission, as He says:

“Who is it that can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

And He, the Most High, says:

“They do not intercede except for those with whom He is pleased.”

And He, the Most High, says:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim to be gods besides Allah. They possess not an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth, nor do they have any share in either, nor does He have any helper among them.’”

“Intercession with Him is of no benefit except for one to whom He gives permission.”

He thus makes clear that none of those invoked besides Him possesses any dominion, has any share in His dominion, or is His helper, and that their intercession is of no benefit except for one to whom He gives permission. This is unlike kings: someone who intercedes with them may possess dominion of his own, may share in their dominion, or may support and assist them in maintaining their sovereignty, and these people

---

## Page 131

have their intercession accepted by kings without the kings’ permission, as do others. The king accepts their intercession sometimes because he needs them, sometimes because he fears them, and sometimes to repay their kindness and reward them for the favours they have done him. He even accepts the intercession of his child and his wife for these reasons, for he needs both his wife and his child; if they were to turn away from him, he would suffer as a result. He also accepts the intercession of his slave, fearing that, if he refuses it, the slave may disobey him or seek to harm him.

All intercession between people is of this kind: no one accepts another’s intercession except out of hope or fear.

Allah, the Most High, however, places no hope in anyone, fears no one and needs no one. Rather, He is the Self-Sufficient. He, the Most High, says:

“Indeed, to Allah belongs whoever is in the heavens and whoever is on earth. Those who call upon others besides Allah do not follow any true partners of His. They follow nothing but conjecture and do nothing but guess.”

And the passage continues to His words:

“They say, ‘Allah has taken a son.’ Glory be to Him! He is the Self-Sufficient. To Him belongs whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on earth.”

The polytheists take intercessors on the model of the intercession familiar to them. He, the Most High, says:

“They worship besides Allah what can neither harm nor benefit them, and they say, ‘These are our intercessors with Allah.’ Say, ‘Are you informing Allah of something He does not know in the heavens or on earth?’ Glory be to Him! Exalted is He above whatever they associate with Him.”

And He, the Most High, says:

“Why, then, did those whom they had taken as gods besides Allah, as a means of drawing near to Him, not help them? Rather, they vanished from them. Such was their falsehood and what they used to fabricate.”

He also relates that the polytheists said:

“We worship them only so that they may bring us nearer to Allah.”

And He, the Most High, says:

“Nor would he command you to take the angels and the prophets as lords. Would he command you to disbelieve after you have become Muslims?”

And He, the Most High, says:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim to be gods besides Him. They have no power to remove harm from you or to shift it elsewhere.’”

“Those whom they call upon themselves seek a means of drawing near to their Lord

---

## Page 132

—seeking to be the nearest—and hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Indeed, the punishment of your Lord is ever to be feared.”

He thus states that those invoked besides Him have no power to remove harm or shift it elsewhere, and that they hope for His mercy, fear His punishment, and seek to draw near to Him. Thus He—glory be to Him—has denied this of the angels and prophets, except for intercession with His permission. Intercession is supplication.

There is no doubt that created beings benefit from supplicating for one another, and Allah has commanded this. However, the one who supplicates and intercedes may do so only with Allah’s permission. He must not offer any intercession that has been forbidden, such as interceding for polytheists and praying for their forgiveness. He, the Most High, says:

“It is not for the Prophet and those who believe to seek forgiveness for the polytheists, even if they are close relatives, after it has become clear to them that they are destined for the blazing Fire.”

“Abraham’s prayer for his father’s forgiveness was only because of a promise he had made to him. But when it became clear to him that his father was an enemy of Allah, he disassociated himself from him.”

And He, the Most High, says concerning the hypocrites:

“It makes no difference to them whether you ask forgiveness for them or do not ask forgiveness for them: Allah will never forgive them.”

It is established in the authentic hadith collection that Allah forbade His Prophet to seek forgiveness for the polytheists and hypocrites, and declared that He would not forgive them. This is also expressed in His words:

“Indeed, Allah does not forgive associating partners with Him, but He forgives anything less than that for whom He wills.”

And His words:

“Never offer the funeral prayer for any of them who dies, nor stand at his grave. Indeed, they disbelieved in Allah and His Messenger and died in a state of defiant disobedience.”

He, the Most High, also says:

“Call upon your Lord humbly and privately. Indeed, He does not love those who transgress”—that is, in supplication.

One form of transgression in supplication is for a servant to ask for something that the Lord would not do: for example, to ask Him for the stations of the prophets when he is not one of them, or for forgiveness for the polytheists, and the like. Another is to ask Him for something involving disobedience to Allah, such as help in disbelief, wickedness, and disobedience.

---

## Page 133

Thus, the intercessor whom Allah has permitted to intercede does so through supplication that involves no transgression. If any of them were to make an inappropriate request, he would not be allowed to persist in it, for they are protected from being allowed to persist in such requests. As Noah said:

“Indeed, my son is of my family; Your promise is surely true, and You are the most just of judges.”

He, the Most High, said:

“O Noah, he is not of your family. Indeed, his conduct is unrighteous. So do not ask Me about that of which you have no knowledge. I admonish you lest you be among the ignorant.”

“He said, ‘My Lord, I seek refuge in You from asking You about that of which I have no knowledge. Unless You forgive me and have mercy upon me, I will be among the losers.’”

Whenever someone supplicates to Allah—glory be to Him, the Most High—and his intercession is accepted, his supplication and intercession occur only by Allah’s decree, predestination, and will. It is He who answers supplication and accepts intercession; He created both the cause and its effect. Supplication is among the causes ordained by Allah—glory be to Him, the Most High.

Since this is so, placing one’s trust in causes violates Allah’s oneness by associating partners with Him; denying that causes are causes betrays a deficiency in reason; and abandoning causes altogether undermines the revealed law. Rather, the servant must direct his trust, supplication, requests, and longing to Allah—glory be to Him, the Most High. Allah ordains for him whatever causes He wills, including the supplications of His creatures and other means.

It is prescribed that those of higher standing supplicate for those of lower standing, and those of lower standing for those of higher standing. Seeking intercession and supplication from the prophets is exemplified by the Muslims’ seeking the Prophet’s intercession—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—when praying for rain, and asking him to supplicate. Likewise, after him, ʿUmar and the Muslims sought rain through his uncle al-ʿAbbās. On the Day of Resurrection, people will seek intercession from the prophets

---

## Page 134

and Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—who is the foremost of the intercessors and has forms of intercession reserved for him. Yet, despite this, it is established in the two Sahīh collections that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“When you hear the caller to prayer, repeat what he says, then invoke blessings upon me. Whoever invokes blessings upon me once, Allah will bless him ten times. Then ask Allah to grant me al-Wasīlah, for it is a rank in Paradise reserved for only one of Allah’s servants, and I hope to be that servant. Whoever asks Allah to grant me al-Wasīlah will be entitled to my intercession on the Day of Resurrection.”

When ʿUmar intended to perform ʿumrah (the lesser pilgrimage) and bade him farewell, he said to him: “My brother, do not forget me in your supplications.”

Thus, the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—asked his community to supplicate for him. However, this was not a matter of seeking something from them. Rather, his instruction to them was like his instructions concerning all other acts of obedience for which they are rewarded. Moreover, he—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—receives rewards equal to theirs for everything they do, for it is authentically reported that he said:

“Whoever calls to guidance will receive a reward equal to the rewards of those who follow him, without diminishing their rewards in the least. Whoever calls to misguidance will bear a burden of sin equal to the burdens of those who follow him, without diminishing their burdens in the least.”

He is the one who calls the community to every form of guidance, so he receives rewards equal to theirs for everything in which they follow him.

Likewise, when they invoke blessings upon him, Allah blesses each of them ten times, and he receives rewards equal to theirs, in addition to what Allah grants in answer to their supplications for him. Allah has rewarded them for those supplications, while the benefit he receives through them is a blessing bestowed upon him by Allah. It is also established in the Sahīh that he said:

“Whenever a man supplicates for his brother in his absence, Allah appoints an angel over him. Each time he supplicates for his brother, the appointed angel says, ‘Amen, and may you have the same.’”

Another hadith states:

“The supplication answered most swiftly is that of one person for another in his absence.”

---

## Page 135

Thus, supplication for another benefits both the one who supplicates and the one for whom he supplicates, even if the former is of a lower rank than the latter. A believer’s supplication for his brother benefits them both. Whoever says to another, “Supplicate for me,” intending that they both benefit, is cooperating with his brother in righteousness and piety. He has drawn the other person’s attention to something beneficial to them both and encouraged him to do it, while the person asked has done what benefits them both. This is like someone who instructs another to act with righteousness and piety: the one instructed is rewarded for his action, and the one who instructed him receives an equal reward for having encouraged it. This is especially so since some supplications are commanded of the servant, as Allah Most High says:

“And seek forgiveness for your sin and for the believing men and believing women.”

He thus commanded him to seek forgiveness. He also said:

“If, when they wronged themselves, they had come to you and sought Allah’s forgiveness, and the Messenger had sought forgiveness for them, they would have found Allah ever accepting of repentance, Most Merciful.”

Allah—glorified is He—mentioned both their seeking forgiveness and the Messenger’s seeking forgiveness for them, since this was something the Messenger had been commanded to do: He commanded him to seek forgiveness for the believing men and believing women. Allah has not commanded any created being to ask another created being for something that He has not commanded the latter to do. Rather, whatever Allah commands His servant to do, whether as an obligation or a recommendation, constitutes worship of Allah, obedience to Him, a means of drawing near to Him, a benefit to the one who performs it, and a good deed on his part. When he does so, Allah’s kindness and favor toward him are all the greater.

Indeed, the greatest blessing Allah has bestowed upon His servants is His guiding them to faith. Faith consists of speech and action, and it increases through obedience and good deeds. The more good the servant does, the more his faith increases.

This is the true bestowal of favor mentioned in His words:

“The path of those upon whom You have bestowed Your favor,”

and in His words:

“Whoever obeys Allah and the Messenger will be with those upon whom Allah has bestowed His favor.”

Indeed, are worldly blessings without religion counted among His blessings or not? There are two well-known views on this among the scholars of our school and others.

---

## Page 136

The correct conclusion is that they are blessings in one respect, though not complete blessings in another. As for the blessing of religion that one should seek, it consists of what Allah has commanded, whether obligatory or recommended. This is the good that should be sought, by the agreement of the Muslims, and it is the true blessing according to Ahl al-Sunnah, who hold that Allah bestows the blessing of actually doing good. The Qadariyyah, by contrast, hold that He bestows only the capacity to do it—a capacity equally suited to doing good or its opposite.

The point here is that Allah has not commanded any created being to ask another for anything except what benefits the person being asked: an obligatory or recommended act. He—glorified is He—requires nothing else of His servant, so how could He command someone else to ask that servant for anything else? Indeed, He has forbidden a servant to ask another for his wealth except out of necessity.

If the person making the request intends to benefit the person being asked, or to benefit both himself and that person, he is rewarded for it. But if his intention is merely to obtain what he wants, without intending that the person being asked should benefit, then this comes from himself. Allah never commands such a request; rather, He has forbidden it, since it is merely asking something of a created being without intending to benefit him or serve his interests. Allah commands us to worship Him and turn to Him in hope, and He commands us to do good to His servants. Yet this person intends neither: he does not intend to turn to Allah in hope and supplicate to Him, which is prayer, nor does he intend to do good to created beings, which is zakat (almsgiving).

Although a servant may incur no sin through such a request, there is nevertheless a difference between what he is commanded to do and what he is permitted to do. Do you not see that, in the hadith concerning the seventy thousand who enter Paradise without being called to account, he said:

“They do not ask others to perform ruqyah (healing recitations) for them.”

Yet requesting ruqyah is permissible. We have discussed this at length elsewhere.

The point here is that whoever posits intermediaries between Allah and His creation, like those that

---

## Page 137

exist between kings and their subjects is a polytheist. Indeed, this is the religion of the polytheists who worshipped idols. They used to say that these were images of the prophets and the righteous, and means through which they drew near to Allah. This is among the forms of associating partners with Allah for which He condemned the Christians, saying:

“They have taken their rabbis and monks, and the Messiah, son of Mary, as lords besides Allah, though they were commanded only to worship one God. There is no deity except Him. Exalted is He above whatever they associate with Him.”

Allah Most High also says:

“When My servants ask you about Me, I am indeed near. I answer the call of the supplicant when he calls upon Me. So let them respond to Me and believe in Me, that they may be rightly guided.”

That is: let them respond to Me when I call them through My commands and prohibitions, and let them believe in Me, trusting that I will answer their prayers when they ask Me and humbly entreat Me.

Allah Most High says:

“So when you have finished, strive diligently.”

“And direct your longing to your Lord.”

Allah Most High says:

“When harm befalls you at sea, all those you invoke vanish except Him.”

Allah Most High says:

“Who answers the distressed when he calls upon Him, removes harm, and makes you successors on the earth?”

Allah Most High says:

“Everyone in the heavens and the earth asks of Him. Every day He is engaged in a matter.”

Allah has explained this affirmation of His oneness in His Book and closed off all avenues to associating partners with Him, so that no one should fear anyone other than Allah, place hope in anyone besides Him, or rely upon anyone but Him.

Allah Most High says:

“So do not fear people, but fear Me, and do not exchange My signs for a small price.”

“It is only Satan who makes you fear his allies”—that is, he makes you afraid of his allies—“so do not fear them, but fear Me, if you are believers.”

Allah Most High says:

“Have you not seen those who were told, ‘Restrain your hands, establish prayer, and give zakat’? Then, when fighting was prescribed for them, some of them feared people as they feared Allah, or with even greater fear.”

Allah Most High says:

“Allah’s mosques are maintained only by those who believe in Allah and the Last Day and establish prayer

---

## Page 138

and give zakat and fear none but Allah.”

Allah Most High says:

“Whoever obeys Allah and His Messenger, fears Allah, and is mindful of Him—it is they who will be successful.”

He thus makes clear that obedience is due to Allah and His Messenger, whereas fear is due to Allah alone.

Allah Most High says:

“If only they had been content with what Allah and His Messenger gave them and had said, ‘Allah is sufficient for us. Allah will give us of His bounty, and so will His Messenger.’”

Similar to this is His saying:

“Those to whom people said, ‘The people have gathered against you, so fear them,’ but this only increased their faith, and they said, ‘Allah is sufficient for us, and He is the best Disposer of affairs.’”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, firmly established this affirmation of Allah’s oneness among his community and cut off the sources of associating partners with Him. This is the realization of our declaration, “There is no deity except Allah.” For a deity is one whom hearts worship with the utmost love, reverence, veneration, honour, hope, and fear. He even told them:

“Do not say, ‘Whatever Allah wills and Muhammad wills.’ Rather, say, ‘Whatever Allah wills, then Muhammad wills.’”

A man said to him, “Whatever Allah wills and you will.” He replied:

“Have you made me a rival to Allah? Rather, say, ‘Whatever Allah alone wills.’”

He said:

“Whoever wishes to swear an oath should swear by Allah or remain silent.”

He said:

“Whoever swears by other than Allah has associated a partner with Him.”

He said to Ibn ‘Abbās:

“When you ask, ask Allah; and when you seek help, seek Allah’s help. The pen has dried concerning what you will encounter. If all creation strove to benefit you, they could benefit you only with something Allah has decreed for you. And if they strove to harm you, they could harm you only with something Allah has decreed against you.”

He also said:

“Do not praise me excessively as the Christians praised Jesus, son of Mary. I am only a servant, so say, ‘Allah’s servant and Messenger.’”

He said:

“O Allah, do not make my grave an idol that is worshipped.”

He said:

“Do not make my grave a place of recurring gatherings. Send blessings upon me, for your blessings reach me wherever you may be.”

During his illness, he said:

“May Allah curse the Jews and the Christians; they took the graves of

---

## Page 139

their prophets as places of worship.” He was warning against what they had done. ‘Ā’ishah said:

“Were it not for that, his grave would have been left exposed, but he disliked its being made a place of worship.”

This is a broad subject.

Although the believer knows that Allah is the Lord and Sovereign of everything, he does not deny the causes Allah has created. For example, He has made rain a cause of plant growth. Allah Most High says:

“and the rain Allah sends down from the sky, giving life thereby to the earth after its death and dispersing throughout it every kind of creature.”

Likewise, He has made the sun and the moon causes of what He creates through them, and intercession and supplication causes of what He decrees through them. An example is the funeral prayer offered by Muslims for the deceased: it is among the causes through which Allah has mercy on the deceased and for which He rewards those who pray for him.

However, three matters concerning causes must be understood.

First: A particular cause cannot produce the desired result on its own; other causes must accompany it, and even then there are impediments. If Allah does not supply all the necessary causes and remove the impediments, the intended outcome will not occur. Whatever He—glorified be He—wills comes to pass, even if people do not will it; and whatever people will does not come to pass unless Allah wills it.

Second: It is not permissible to believe that something is a cause without knowledge. Anyone who claims that something is a cause without knowledge, or in contradiction to religious law, is in error. An example is someone who thinks that making a vow is a cause of averting affliction and obtaining blessings. It is established in the two Sahīh collections that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, forbade vows and said:

“A vow brings no good; it only serves to extract something from a miser.”

Third: No religious act may be employed as a means unless it is prescribed, for acts of worship must be based on revealed authorization. Thus, a person may not associate partners with Allah by supplicating to anyone other than Him—even if he thinks this is a means of achieving some of his aims—

---

## Page 140

nor may he worship Allah through innovations that contravene religious law—even if he thinks they serve that purpose.

For devils may help a person achieve some of his aims when he associates partners with Allah, and some of a person’s aims may be achieved through disbelief, wickedness, and disobedience. Yet this is not lawful for him, because the resulting harm is greater than the resulting benefit. The Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was sent to secure and perfect benefits, and to eliminate and reduce harms. Thus, in whatever Allah commands, the benefit predominates; and in whatever He forbids, the harm predominates.

These points require more detailed explanation than this sheet can accommodate. Allah knows best.

---

## Page 141

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was also asked:

The questioner said: “Allah hears supplication through Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, for he is the means and the intermediary.”

The Answer

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. If he means that believing in Muhammad, obeying him, and invoking blessings and peace upon him are a means by which a servant’s supplication is accepted and rewarded, then he has spoken the truth.

But if he means that Allah does not answer anyone’s supplication until that person presents it to a created being or adjures Allah by that being, or that the prophets themselves—without belief in them, obedience to them, or their intercession—are a means for supplication to be answered, then he has spoken falsely in this. And Allah knows best.”

---

## Page 142

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah Most High have mercy on him—was also asked:

“Is it permissible to seek a means of drawing near to Allah through the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace?”

He replied:

“Praise be to Allah. Seeking a means through faith in him, love for him, obedience to him, invoking blessings and peace upon him, his supplication and intercession, and similar deeds of his and acts prescribed for servants with regard to him is lawful by agreement of the Muslims. The Companions, may Allah be pleased with them, sought a means through him during his lifetime. After his death, they sought a means through his uncle al-‘Abbās, just as they had sought a means through him.

As for someone saying, ‘O Allah, I seek a means of drawing near to You through him,’ scholars hold two views concerning this, just as they hold two views concerning swearing by him. The majority of the leading scholars, including Mālik, al-Shāfi‘ī, and Abū Hanīfah, hold that it is not permissible to swear by anyone other than Allah, including prophets and angels. Scholars agree that such an oath is not legally binding. This is one of the two views reported from Ahmad. According to the other, an oath sworn by him alone, to the exclusion of others, is legally binding.

For this reason, Ahmad stated in the manual of pilgrimage rites he wrote for his companion al-Marwadhī that one may seek a means through the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in supplication. Other scholars, however, said that this amounts to adjuring Allah by him, and that Allah must not be adjured by any created being. In one of the two views reported from Ahmad, he permitted swearing by him, and therefore permitted seeking a means through him.

The other view reported from him, however, accords with the position of the majority of scholars: that one must not swear by him;

---

## Page 143

and therefore Allah must not be adjured by him, any more than by the other angels and prophets. We know of no one among the early generations or the leading scholars who said that Allah may be adjured by him, just as they did not say that oaths may be sworn by them at all.

For this reason, Abū Muhammad ibn ‘Abd al-Salām issued a ruling that Allah must not be adjured by any angel, prophet, or anyone else. However, when he was told that a hadith concerning adjuring Allah by the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had been reported, he said: ‘If the hadith is authentic, this would apply exclusively to him.’ Yet the hadith in question does not indicate that Allah may be adjured by him.

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:
‘Whoever wishes to swear an oath must swear by Allah; otherwise, let him remain silent.’

He also said:
‘Whoever swears by other than Allah has associated partners with Him.’

Supplication is worship, and worship is founded on revealed prescription and adherence, not on personal desire and innovation. And Allah knows best.”

---

## Page 144

Shaykh al-Islām—may Allah sanctify his soul—also said:

In the name of Allah, the Most Merciful, the Bestower of Mercy.

All praise is due to Allah. We seek His help and forgiveness, and we seek refuge in Allah from the evil within ourselves and from our evil deeds. Whoever Allah guides, none can misguide; and whoever He leaves astray, none can guide. I bear witness that none has the right to be worshipped except Allah alone, without partner. And I bear witness that Muhammad is His servant and Messenger, whom He sent with guidance and the religion of truth to make it prevail over all religion. Allah suffices as a witness.

He sent him before the Hour as a bearer of glad tidings and a warner, as one who calls to Allah by His permission, and as an illuminating lamp. Through him, He guided people out of misguidance, gave them sight after blindness, and led them from error to right guidance. Through him, He opened blind eyes, deaf ears, and veiled hearts. He conveyed the message, fulfilled the trust, sincerely counselled the Muslim community, strove for Allah as he ought to strive, and worshipped his Lord until certainty came to him from his Lord. May Allah bless him and his family and grant him abundant peace.

There is therefore a distinction between truth and falsehood, guidance and misguidance, right guidance and error, the path of the people of Paradise and the path of the people of Hell, and between his allies and his enemies. What is lawful is what Allah and His Messenger have made lawful; what is forbidden is what Allah and His Messenger have forbidden; and religion is what Allah and His Messenger have prescribed.

Allah sent him to both jinn and humankind. Everyone must therefore believe in him and in what he brought, and follow him inwardly and outwardly. Believing in him and following him is the path of Allah and the religion of Allah,

---

## Page 145

and it is worship of Allah, obedience to Allah, the path of Allah’s allies, and the means of drawing near to Him which Allah commanded His servants to seek when He, Most High, said:

“O you who believe, fear Allah and seek the means of drawing near to Him.”

Seeking the means of drawing near to Allah is possible only through faith in Muhammad and following him. Drawing near through faith in him and obedience to him is obligatory upon everyone, inwardly and outwardly, during the lifetime of the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and after his death, in his presence and in his absence. Once the proof has been established against a person, this obligation to draw near through faith in him and obedience to him is not waived under any circumstances or on any grounds. There is no way to attain Allah’s honour and mercy, or to escape the humiliation and punishment He inflicts, except by drawing near through faith in him and obedience to him.

He—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—is the intercessor for all creation and the holder of the Praiseworthy Station, a distinction that the first and the last will wish to share. He is the greatest of all intercessors in rank and the highest in standing before Allah. Allah, Most High, said of Moses:

“He was held in honour before Allah.”

And He said of the Messiah:

“Honoured in this world and the Hereafter.”

Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—has a higher standing than all the prophets and messengers. Yet his intercession and supplication benefit only those for whom the Messenger intercedes and supplicates. Whoever the Messenger supplicates and intercedes for draws near to Allah through his intercession and supplication, just as his Companions used to draw near to Allah through his supplication and intercession, and just as people on the Day of Resurrection will draw near to Allah, Blessed and Most High, through his supplication and intercession. May Allah bless him and his family and grant him abundant peace.

In the Companions’ usage, the term (tawassul, seeking a means of approach) was employed in this sense. Drawing near through his supplication and intercession is beneficial when accompanied by faith in him. Without faith in him, however, the intercession of intercessors will avail disbelievers and hypocrites nothing in the Hereafter.

---

## Page 146

For this reason, he was forbidden to seek forgiveness for his uncle, his father, and other disbelievers. He was also forbidden to seek forgiveness for the hypocrites, and was told:

“It is all the same for them whether you ask forgiveness for them or do not ask forgiveness for them; Allah will never forgive them.”

However, disbelievers differ in their degrees of disbelief, just as believers differ in their degrees of faith. Allah, Most High, said:

“Postponement is only an increase in disbelief.”

Thus, if a disbeliever’s disbelief is less severe because he supported and helped the Prophet, the Prophet’s intercession benefits him by alleviating his punishment, not by removing it altogether. Thus, in Sahīh Muslim, al-‘Abbās ibn ‘Abd al-Muttalib said:

“I asked, ‘O Messenger of Allah, have you benefited Abū Tālib in any way? He used to protect you and become angry on your behalf.’

“He replied, ‘Yes. He is in a shallow expanse of fire. Were it not for me, he would be in the lowest depth of the Fire.’”

Another wording reads:

“‘Abū Tālib used to protect you, support you, and become angry on your behalf. Has that benefited him?’ He replied, ‘Yes. I found him in the depths of the Fire and brought him out into a shallow expanse.’”

It also contains a report from Abū Sa‘īd that, when his uncle Abū Tālib was mentioned in the presence of the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—he said:

“Perhaps my intercession will benefit him on the Day of Resurrection, so that he will be placed in a shallow expanse of the Fire reaching his ankles, from which his brain will boil.”

He also said:

“Abū Tālib will have the lightest punishment of all the inhabitants of the Fire. He will be wearing two sandals of fire, from which his brain will boil.”

Likewise, his supplication benefits them by preventing their punishment from being hastened in this world. Thus, he—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—related the account of a prophet whom his people had beaten and who was saying:

“O Allah, forgive my people, for they do not know.”

It is reported that he supplicated to this effect: “Forgive them, and do not hasten their punishment in this world.” Allah, Most High, said:

“If Allah were to take people to task for what they have earned, He would not leave a single living creature on the earth’s surface. But He grants them respite until an appointed time.”

---

## Page 147

Moreover, he may supplicate for a disbeliever, asking Allah to guide him or grant him provision, and Allah then guides him or provides for him. Thus, he supplicated for the mother of Abū Hurayra, and Allah guided her. He also supplicated for Daws, saying:

“O Allah, guide Daws and bring them to me.”

Allah then guided them. Likewise, Abū Dāwūd reported that he prayed for rain for some polytheists when they asked him to do so. This was an act of kindness towards them, by which he sought to win their hearts, just as he sought to win their hearts by other means.

Muslims agree that he—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—has the greatest standing with Allah of all creation. No created being has greater standing with Allah than he does, nor is any intercession greater than his. Yet the supplications and intercession of the prophets are not equivalent to believing in them and obeying them. Believing in them and obeying them ensures bliss in the Hereafter and deliverance from punishment, without qualification and for everyone who does so. Whoever dies believing in Allah and His Messenger and obeying Allah and His Messenger will certainly be among those destined for bliss. Whoever dies disbelieving in what the Messenger brought will certainly be among the inhabitants of the Fire.

As for intercession and supplication, the benefit people derive from them depends on conditions being met, and there are impediments to that benefit. Interceding for disbelievers to be saved from the Fire and seeking forgiveness for them do not benefit them if they die in disbelief—even if the intercessor has the greatest standing of all intercessors. No intercessor is greater than Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—followed by Abraham, Allah’s intimate friend. Abraham, Allah’s intimate friend, supplicated for his father and sought forgiveness for him, as Allah, Most High, relates that he said:

“Our Lord, forgive me, my parents, and the believers on the Day when the reckoning is established.”

He—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—had wished to seek forgiveness for Abū Tālib, following Abraham’s example. Some Muslims also wished to seek forgiveness for some of their relatives. Allah, Most High, then revealed:

“It is not for the Prophet and those who believe to seek forgiveness for the polytheists, even if they are close relatives, after it has become clear to them that they are inhabitants of the blazing Fire.”

---

## Page 148

Then Allah explained Abraham’s reason, saying:

“Abraham’s prayer for his father’s forgiveness was only because of a promise he had made to him. But when it became clear to him that his father was an enemy of Allah, he disowned him. Indeed, Abraham was tender-hearted and forbearing.”

“Allah would not lead a people astray after guiding them until He had made clear to them what they must avoid.”

It is established in Sahīh al-Bukhārī, on the authority of Abū Hurayra, that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Abraham will meet his father Āzar on the Day of Resurrection, with Āzar’s face covered in grime and dust. Abraham will say to him, ‘Did I not tell you not to disobey me?’ His father will reply, ‘Today I will not disobey you.’ Abraham will then say, ‘My Lord, You promised me that You would not disgrace me on the Day when people are resurrected. What disgrace could be greater than that of my wretched father?’ Allah, Mighty and Majestic, will say, ‘I have forbidden Paradise to the disbelievers.’ Then Abraham will be told, ‘Look beneath your feet.’ He will look and find a befouled male hyena. It will be seized by its legs and thrown into the Fire.”

Thus, because his father died as a polytheist, Abraham’s seeking forgiveness for him did not benefit him, despite Abraham’s immense standing and rank. Allah, Most High, has said to the believers:

“You have an excellent example in Abraham and those with him, when they said to their people, ‘We disown you and whatever you worship besides Allah. We reject you, and enmity and hatred have arisen between us and you forever, until you believe in Allah alone’—except for Abraham’s words to his father, ‘I will surely seek forgiveness for you, but I have no power to protect you from Allah in any way.’ Our Lord, in You we put our trust, to You we turn, and to You is the final return.”

“Our Lord, do not make us a trial for those who disbelieve, and forgive us, our Lord. Indeed, You are the Almighty, the All-Wise.”

Allah, Most High, has therefore commanded the believers to follow the example of Abraham and those who followed him, except in Abraham’s words to his father:

“I will surely seek forgiveness for you.”

For Allah does not forgive associating partners with Him.

The same applies to Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—the foremost of all intercessors. In Sahīh Muslim, Abū Hurayra reports that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“I asked my Lord for permission to seek forgiveness for my mother, but He did not permit me. I asked Him for permission to visit her grave, and He permitted me.”

Another narration relates: “The Prophet

---

## Page 149

visited his mother’s grave and wept, causing those around him to weep. Then he said, ‘I asked my Lord for permission to seek forgiveness for my mother, but He did not permit me. I asked Him for permission to visit her grave, and He permitted me. So visit graves, for they remind you of death.’”

It is established in the Sahīh, on the authority of Anas:

“A man said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, where is my father?’ He replied, ‘In the Fire.’ When the man turned to leave, he called him back and said, ‘My father and your father are in the Fire.’”

It is also established in the Sahīh, on the authority of Abū Hurayra:

“When this verse was revealed: ‘And warn your closest relatives,’ the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—summoned Quraysh, and they gathered. He addressed them both generally and specifically, saying, ‘O descendants of Kaʿb ibn Luʾayy, save yourselves from the Fire. O descendants of Murra ibn Kaʿb, save yourselves from the Fire. O descendants of ʿAbd Shams, save yourselves from the Fire. O descendants of ʿAbd Manāf, save yourselves from the Fire. O descendants of ʿAbd al-Muttalib, save yourselves from the Fire. O Fātima, save yourself from the Fire. I have no power to protect you from Allah in any way. However, you have ties of kinship with me, which I shall duly maintain.’”

Another narration from him states:

“O people of Quraysh, ransom yourselves from Allah’s punishment, for I cannot protect you from Allah in any way. O descendants of ʿAbd al-Muttalib, I cannot protect you from Allah in any way. O ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muttalib, I cannot protect you from Allah in any way. O Safiyya, aunt of the Messenger of Allah, I cannot protect you from Allah in any way. O Fātima, daughter of the Messenger of Allah, ask me for whatever you wish of my wealth; I cannot protect you from Allah in any way.”

ʿĀʾisha reported:

“When ‘And warn your closest relatives’ was revealed, the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—stood up and said, ‘O Fātima, daughter of Muhammad! O Safiyya, daughter of ʿAbd al-Muttalib! I have no power to protect you from Allah in any way. Ask me for whatever you wish of my wealth.’”

Abū Hurayra reported:

“One day, the Messenger of Allah—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—stood among us to deliver an address. He spoke of misappropriation, stressing its enormity and the gravity of the matter. Then he said, ‘Let me not find any of you coming on the Day of Resurrection with a bellowing camel on his neck, saying, “O Messenger of Allah, help me!” And I will say, “I can do nothing for you

---

## Page 150

; I have conveyed the message to you.” Let me not find any of you coming on the Day of Resurrection with a neighing horse on his neck, saying, “O Messenger of Allah, help me!” And I will say, “I can do nothing for you; I have conveyed the message to you.” Let me not find any of you coming on the Day of Resurrection with a bleating sheep on his neck, saying, “O Messenger of Allah, help me!” And I will say, “I can do nothing for you; I have conveyed the message to you.” Let me not find any of you coming on the Day of Resurrection with fluttering scraps on his neck, saying, “O Messenger of Allah, help me!” And I will say, “I can do nothing for you; I have conveyed the message to you.” Let me not find any of you coming on the Day of Resurrection with inanimate property on his neck, saying, “O Messenger of Allah, help me!” And I will say, “I can do nothing for you; I have conveyed the message to you.”’”

Both al-Bukhārī and Muslim recorded this in their Sahīh collections. Muslim added:

“Let me not find any of you coming on the Day of Resurrection with a screaming person on his neck, saying, ‘O Messenger of Allah, help me!’ And I will say, ‘I can do nothing for you; I have conveyed the message to you.’”

Al-Bukhārī also relates from him that the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said:

“Let none of you come on the Day of Resurrection carrying a bleating sheep on his neck, saying, ‘O Muhammad!’ And I will say, ‘I can do nothing for you; I have conveyed the message.’ Let none of you come carrying a bellowing camel on his neck, saying, ‘O Muhammad!’ And I will say, ‘I can do nothing for you; I have conveyed the message.’”

His statement here—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—“I have no power to protect you from Allah in any way,” is like Abraham’s statement to his father:

“I will surely ask forgiveness for you, but I have no power to protect you from Allah in any way.”

As for his intercession and supplication for the believers, these benefit them in their worldly and religious affairs, by the consensus of the Muslims. Likewise, the Muslims agree that his intercession for the believers on the Day of Resurrection increases their reward and raises their ranks, although it has been said that some adherents of religious innovation deny this.

As for his intercession for the sinners of his community, the Companions, those who followed them in righteousness, and all the leading scholars of the Muslims—the four imams and others—agree upon it. Many adherents of religious innovation among the Khārijites, Muʿtazilites, and Zaydīs have denied it. These groups maintained that whoever enters the Fire will not leave it

---

## Page 151

neither through intercession nor by any other means. In their view, there are only those who enter Paradise and never enter the Fire, and those who enter the Fire and never enter Paradise. They hold that no individual can receive both reward and punishment.

The Companions, those who followed them in righteousness, and all the leading scholars, including the four imams and others, affirm what is established by the authentic, mass-transmitted hadith from the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace: Allah will bring some people out of the Fire after punishing them for as long as He wills. He will bring them out through the intercession of Muhammad—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and bring others out through the intercession of others, while bringing some out without intercession.

Those who deny intercession cite the words of the Most High:

“And fear a Day when no soul will avail another in any way, nor will intercession be accepted from it, nor will a ransom be taken from it.”

And His words:

“Nor will a ransom be accepted from it, nor will intercession benefit it.”

And His words:

“Before there comes a Day in which there will be no trade, no friendship, and no intercession.”

And His words:

“The wrongdoers will have neither a close friend nor an intercessor whose plea is heeded.”

And His words:

“So the intercession of intercessors will not benefit them.”

The response of Ahl al-Sunnah is that these passages convey two meanings.

The first is that intercession does not benefit the polytheists, as the Most High says when describing them:

“‘What brought you into Saqar?’
They will say, ‘We were not among those who prayed,
nor did we feed the poor.
We used to indulge in vain discourse with those who indulged in it,
and we used to deny the Day of Recompense,
until certainty came to us.’
So the intercession of intercessors will not benefit them.”

The benefit of the intercessors’ intercession is denied to these people because they were disbelievers.

The second is that these passages deny the intercession affirmed by the polytheists and those who resemble them among the adherents of religious innovation, from both the People of the Book and the Muslims. They suppose that created beings have such standing with Allah that they can intercede with Him without His permission, just as people intercede with one another, so that intercession is accepted by

---

## Page 152

the person to whom the intercessor appeals, because he needs the intercessor and is motivated by hope or fear, just as created beings deal with one another through reciprocal exchange.
