The polytheists used to take angels, prophets, and righteous people as intercessors besides Allah. They made images of them and sought intercession through these images, saying:

“These are Allah’s chosen ones. We therefore seek access to Allah by invoking and worshipping them, so that they may intercede for us, just as people seek access to kings through their close associates, who are nearer to them than others. These associates intercede with kings without the kings’ permission. One of them may even intercede with a king in a matter the king would not himself choose, yet the king feels compelled to accept his intercession out of hope or fear.”

Allah rejected this intercession, saying:

“Who is it that can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

And He said:

“And how many angels there are in the heavens whose intercession avails nothing except after Allah has given permission to whom He wills and is pleased with.”

And He said concerning the angels:

“They say, ‘The Most Merciful has taken a son.’ Glory be to Him! Rather, they are honoured servants.”

“They do not precede Him in speech, and they act by His command.”

“He knows what is before them and what is behind them, and they intercede only for those with whom He is pleased, while they stand in awe of Him.”

And He said:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim besides Allah. They possess not an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth, nor do they have any share in either, nor does He have any helper from among them.’”

“Intercession with Him benefits none except those to whom He gives permission.”

And the Most High said:

“They worship besides Allah that which neither harms nor benefits them, and they say, ‘These are our intercessors with Allah.’ Say, ‘Do you inform Allah of something He does not know in the heavens or on earth?’ Glory be to Him, and exalted is He above what they associate with Him!”

And the Most High said:

“Warn with it those who fear being gathered before their Lord: they will have neither a protector nor an intercessor besides Him, so that they may become mindful of Him.”

And the Most High said:

“Allah is the One who created the heavens and the earth and everything between them in six days, then rose above the Throne. You have neither a protector nor an intercessor besides Him. Will you not then take heed?”

---

## Page 153

And the Most High said:

“Those whom they invoke besides Him have no power of intercession, except those who testify to the truth knowingly.”

And the Most High said:

“You have come to Us alone, just as We created you the first time, leaving behind all that We bestowed upon you. We do not see with you your intercessors whom you claimed were partners with Allah concerning you. All ties between you have been severed, and what you used to claim has deserted you.”

And the Most High said:

“Or have they taken intercessors besides Allah? Say, ‘Even though they possess nothing and have no understanding?’”

“Say, ‘All intercession belongs to Allah. His is the dominion of the heavens and the earth. Then to Him you will be returned.’”

“When Allah alone is mentioned, the hearts of those who do not believe in the Hereafter shrink with aversion. But when those besides Him are mentioned, they rejoice.”

And the Most High said:

“Voices will be humbled before the Most Merciful, and you will hear nothing but a faint sound.”

“On that Day, intercession will benefit none except one to whom the Most Merciful has given permission and whose words He approves.”

And the man mentioned in Surah Yā Sīn said:

“Why should I not worship the One who created me, and to whom you will be returned?”

“Should I take gods besides Him? If the Most Merciful intends harm for me, their intercession will avail me nothing, nor can they rescue me.”

“Then I would surely be in clear error.”

“I have believed in your Lord, so listen to me.”

This, then, is the intercession that the polytheists attributed to angels, prophets, and righteous people, even making images of them and saying: “Seeking intercession through their images is seeking intercession through them.” Likewise, they went to their graves and said: “We seek their intercession after their death so that they may intercede for us with Allah.” They made images of them and worshipped them in this way. Allah and His Messenger rejected this intercession, condemned the polytheists for it, and declared them disbelievers on account of it.

Allah the Most High said concerning Noah’s people:

“They said, ‘Never abandon your gods; never abandon Wadd, Suwāʿ, Yaghūth, Yaʿūq, or Nasr.’”

“They have already led many astray.”

Ibn ʿAbbās and others said: “These were righteous men among Noah’s people. When they died, the people remained at their graves; then they made images of them and worshipped them.” This is well known in works of Quranic commentary and hadith

---

## Page 154

and other works, such as those of al-Bukhārī and others. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, invalidated this intercession, eradicated its roots, and blocked the means leading to it. He even cursed those who took the graves of prophets and righteous people as places of worship in which prayers are offered, even when those praying there do not seek their intercession. He forbade praying towards graves and sent ʿAlī ibn Abī Tālib with instructions to leave no raised grave without leveling it, and no image without obliterating and erasing it. He also cursed image-makers.

Abū al-Hayyāj al-Asadī reported: “ʿAlī ibn Abī Tālib said to me: ‘I shall send you on the same mission on which the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, sent me: leave no image without obliterating it, and no raised grave without leveling it.’ In another wording: ‘And no picture without obliterating it.’”

Recorded by Muslim.

---

## Page 155

Section

The term tawassul (seeking a means of drawing near to Allah) may have three meanings. Two of these are agreed upon among Muslims.

The first is the very foundation of faith and Islam: seeking nearness to Allah through faith in the Prophet and obedience to him. The second is through his supplication and intercession. This too is beneficial: those for whom he supplicates and whose behalf his intercession is accepted may seek nearness to Allah through it, by the agreement of Muslims. Anyone who denies tawassul through him in either of these two senses is an unbeliever and an apostate, who is to be called upon to repent and, if he does not, put to death as an apostate.

However, seeking nearness to Allah through faith in him and obedience to him is the foundation of the religion. This is a matter necessarily known to be part of Islam among scholars and laypeople alike. Thus, the unbelief of anyone who denies this meaning is evident to scholars and laypeople alike. As for his supplication and intercession, and the benefit Muslims derive from them, anyone who denies this is also an unbeliever. Yet this is less evident than the first matter. Anyone who denies it out of ignorance is therefore to be informed of it; if he persists in denying it, he is an apostate.

As for his supplication and intercession in this world, no one among the people of the qiblah (the Muslim community) has denied them. Regarding intercession on the Day of Resurrection, the position of Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamāʿah (the people of the Sunnah and the community)—namely, the Companions, those who follow them in righteousness, and all the imams of the Muslims, including the four imams and others—is that he will have both particular and general intercessions on the Day of Resurrection. His intercession will be accepted for those members of his community who have committed major sins and for whom Allah permits him to intercede. Only believers who affirm Allah’s oneness benefit from his intercession, not the adherents of

---

## Page 156

polytheism. Even if a polytheist loves and reveres him, his intercession will not save that person from the Fire. Only affirming Allah’s oneness and believing in him can save a person from the Fire. Thus, although Abū Tālib and others loved him, they did not acknowledge the oneness of Allah that he proclaimed, so they could not be brought out of the Fire through his intercession or by any other means.

In Sahīh al-Bukhārī, Abū Hurayrah relates:
“I said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, who will be most fortunate in receiving your intercession on the Day of Resurrection?’ He replied, ‘The person most fortunate in receiving my intercession on the Day of Resurrection is the one who says, “There is no god but Allah,” sincerely from his heart.’”

He also relates in Sahīh Muslim that the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:
“Every prophet has a supplication that is answered. Every prophet hastened to offer his supplication, but I have reserved mine as intercession on the Day of Resurrection. Allah willing, it will reach those of my community who die without associating anything with Allah.”

In the Sunan collections, ʿAwf ibn Mālik relates that the Messenger of Allah said:
“Someone came to me from my Lord and gave me the choice between half of my community entering Paradise and intercession. I chose intercession, and it is for those who die without associating anything with Allah.”

In another wording, he said:
“Whoever meets Allah without associating anything with Him will be included in my intercession.”

This principle—affirming Allah’s oneness—is the foundation of the religion. Allah accepts no other religion from the earlier or later generations. With this He sent the messengers and revealed the scriptures, as He, the Most High, says:

“And ask those of Our messengers whom We sent before you: Did We appoint any deities besides the Most Merciful to be worshipped?”

He, the Most High, also says:

“And We sent no messenger before you except that We revealed to him: ‘There is no deity except Me, so worship Me.’”

He, the Most High, also says:

“And We certainly sent into every nation a messenger, saying, ‘Worship Allah and shun false objects of worship.’ Among them were those whom Allah guided, and among them were those upon whom misguidance became inevitable.”

Allah, the Almighty and Majestic, has mentioned that each of the messengers began his call by saying to his people:

“Worship Allah; you have no deity other than Him.”

---

## Page 157

And in the Musnad, Ibn ‘Umar relates that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“I was sent with the sword before the Hour, so that Allah alone would be worshipped, without any partner. My provision was placed beneath the shade of my spear, and humiliation and abasement were imposed upon those who oppose my command. Whoever imitates a people is one of them.”

The polytheists of Quraysh and others—whose polytheism the Quran described, whose blood and property the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, considered lawful to take, whose households he took captive, and whom he declared deserving of the Fire—acknowledged that Allah alone created the heavens and the earth, as He says:

“If you ask them who created the heavens and the earth, they will surely say, ‘Allah.’ Say, ‘All praise belongs to Allah.’ But most of them do not know.”

He also says:

“If you ask them who created the heavens and the earth and subjected the sun and the moon, they will surely say, ‘Allah.’ How, then, are they turned away?”

He also says:

“Say, ‘To whom belong the earth and whoever is in it, if you know?’”

“They will say, ‘To Allah.’ Say, ‘Then will you not take heed?’”

“Say, ‘Who is the Lord of the seven heavens and the Lord of the mighty Throne?’”

“They will say, ‘Allah.’ Say, ‘Then will you not fear Him?’”

“Say, ‘In whose hand is the dominion of all things, who grants protection while none can grant protection against Him, if you know?’”

“They will say, ‘Allah.’ Say, ‘How, then, are you deluded?’”

“Rather, We have brought them the truth, and they are certainly liars.”

“Allah has never taken a son, nor has there ever been any deity alongside Him. Otherwise, each deity would have taken away what it created, and some would have sought supremacy over others. Exalted is Allah above what they attribute to Him!”

The polytheists who set up other deities alongside Him acknowledged that their deities were created beings. Yet they took them as intercessors and sought to draw near to Him by worshipping them, as He, the Most High, says:

“They worship besides Allah what can neither harm nor benefit them, and they say, ‘These are our intercessors with Allah.’ Say, ‘Are you informing Allah of something He does not know in the heavens or on the earth?’ Exalted is He and far above whatever they associate with Him!”

---

## Page 158

And He, the Most High, says:

“The revelation of the Book is from Allah, the Almighty, the All-Wise.”

“Indeed, We have sent down the Book to you in truth, so worship Allah, devoting your religion sincerely to Him.”

“Surely, sincere devotion belongs to Allah alone. Those who take protectors besides Him say, ‘We worship them only so that they may bring us nearer to Allah.’ Indeed, Allah will judge between them concerning their differences. Allah does not guide anyone who is a liar and a persistent disbeliever.”

In their pilgrimage invocation, they would say: “Here I am at Your service. You have no partner except a partner who belongs to You: You own him and all that he owns.” He, the Most High, says:

“He sets forth an example for you from your own lives: Do any of your slaves share as partners in what We have provided you, so that you are equal in it and fear them as you fear one another? Thus do We explain the signs to people who reason.”

“Rather, the wrongdoers follow their desires without knowledge. Who, then, can guide those whom Allah has led astray? They will have no helpers.”

“So turn your face towards the religion, inclining to the truth—the natural disposition upon which Allah created humankind. There is no altering Allah’s creation. That is the upright religion, but most people do not know.”

“Turn to Him in repentance, fear Him, establish prayer, and do not be among the polytheists—”

“those who divided their religion and became sects, each faction rejoicing in what it possesses.”

Through the example He set forth for them, He, glorified be He, made clear that no one He owns should be made His partner. He said:

“Do any of your slaves share as partners in what We have provided you, so that you are equal in it?”

Would any of you fear his slave as you fear one another? If none of you is willing to have his slave as his partner, how, then, can you accept this for yourselves?

This is like their saying that He has daughters. He, the Most High, says:

“They attribute to Allah what they themselves dislike, while their tongues falsely claim that the best awaits them. Without doubt, the Fire awaits them, and they will be abandoned there.”

He, the Most High, also says:

“When one of them is given news of a daughter, his face darkens, and he is filled with suppressed grief.”

“He hides from his people because of the distressing news he has received. Should he keep her in humiliation or bury her in the dust?

---

## Page 159

Evil indeed is their judgment!”

“Those who do not believe in the Hereafter have evil attributes, while Allah has the highest attributes. He is the Almighty, the All-Wise.”

The polytheists whom Allah and His Messenger described as such trace their origins to two groups: the people of Noah and the people of Abraham. The polytheism of Noah’s people began with their remaining at the graves of the righteous. They then made statues of them and subsequently worshipped them. The polytheism of Abraham’s people began with worshipping the stars, the sun, and the moon.

Both groups worship the jinn, for devils may speak to them and help them with various things. They may believe that they are worshipping angels, although in reality they are worshipping only the jinn, since it is the jinn who help them and approve of their polytheism. He, the Most High, says:

“On the Day He gathers them all together, He will say to the angels, ‘Was it you these people used to worship?’”

“They will say, ‘Glory be to You! You are our Protector, not they. Rather, they used to worship the jinn; most of them believed in them.’”

The angels do not help them in their polytheism, either in life or in death, nor do they approve of it. Devils, however, may help them and appear to them in human forms, so that they see them with their own eyes. One of them may say: “I am Abraham. I am the Messiah. I am Muhammad. I am al-Khidr. I am Abū Bakr. I am ‘Umar. I am ‘Uthmān. I am ‘Alī. I am such-and-such a shaykh.”

Some may say of others: “This is such-and-such a prophet,” or, “This is al-Khidr.” Yet all of them are jinn, vouching for one another.

The jinn are like human beings: among them are disbelievers, the morally corrupt, and the disobedient, as well as ignorant worshippers. Some of them love a shaykh, assume his appearance, and say: “I am so-and-so.” This may happen in a wilderness or a desolate place. The jinn then gives that person food and drink, shows him the way, or tells him about events occurring beyond his knowledge, so he supposes

---

## Page 160

that the shaykh himself, whether dead or alive, had done this. He may say: “This is the shaykh’s secret; this is his raqīqa; this is his reality,” or, “This is an angel who has come in his likeness.” In fact, it is a jinn, for angels do not assist in polytheism, falsehood, sin, or aggression.

Allah, the Most High, has said:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim besides Him. They have no power to remove your hardship or transfer it elsewhere.’”

“Those whom they invoke seek a means of access to their Lord, each seeking to be closest to Him. They hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Indeed, your Lord’s punishment is ever to be feared.”

Some of the early generations said that there were people who invoked angels and prophets, such as Ezra and the Messiah. Allah, the Most High, explained that the angels and prophets are servants of Allah, just as those who worship them are servants of Allah. He also explained that they hope for His mercy, fear His punishment, and draw near to Him, as do all His other righteous servants.

The polytheists among these people may say: “We seek their intercession; that is, we ask the angels and prophets to intercede. When we visit the grave of one of them, we ask him to intercede for us. If we make an image of him”—images being either three-dimensional figures or pictures, like those Christians make in their churches—they say, “our purpose in making these images is to remember those they depict and their lives. We address these images, but our intention is to address those they depict, so that they may intercede for us with Allah.”

One of them says: “O my master so-and-so,” or, “O my master George or Peter,” or, “O my tender-hearted lady Mary,” or, “O my master Abraham, the close friend of Allah, or Moses son of ‘Imrān,” or something similar, “intercede for me with your Lord.”

They may address the deceased at his grave: “Ask your Lord on my behalf.” Or they may address a living person who is absent as they would address him if he were alive and present. They recite poems in which one of them says:

“O my master so-and-so, I rely on you; I am under your protection. Intercede for me with Allah. Ask Allah to grant us victory

---

## Page 161

over our enemy. Ask Allah to relieve us of this hardship. I complain to you of such and such, so ask Allah to relieve this distress.”

Or one of them says: “Ask Allah to forgive me.” Some of them interpret the words of Allah, the Most High:

“If, when they wronged themselves, they had come to you and asked Allah for forgiveness, and the Messenger had asked forgiveness for them, they would have found Allah ever accepting of repentance, Merciful.”

They say: “If we ask him to seek forgiveness for us after his death, we are in the same position as those Companions who asked him to seek forgiveness for them.” In doing so, they contradict the consensus of the Companions, those who followed them in righteousness, and all other Muslims. None of them asked the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to intercede for him after his death, nor did they ask him for anything. Nor did any of the imams of the Muslims mention this in their books. It was mentioned only by some later jurists, who recounted a fabricated story attributed to Mālik, may Allah be pleased with him. This story will be related and discussed in detail later, if Allah, the Most High, wills.

These forms of addressing angels, prophets, and righteous people after their death, at their graves and in their absence, and addressing their images, are among the gravest forms of polytheism found among polytheists outside the People of the Book, and among those of the People of the Book and the Muslims who introduce religious innovations—those who have introduced forms of polytheism and worship that Allah, the Most High, has not authorized.

Allah, the Most High, says:

“Or do they have partners who have prescribed for them a religion that Allah has not authorized?”

Invoking angels and prophets after their death and in their absence, making requests of them, seeking their aid and intercession in such circumstances, and setting up their images—with the intention of seeking intercession from them—are religious practices that Allah has not prescribed, for which He has sent no messenger and concerning which He has revealed no scripture. By the consensus of the Muslims, this is neither obligatory nor recommended. None of the Companions or those who followed them in righteousness practised it, nor did any imam of the Muslims command it, even though it is practised by many

---

## Page 162

people devoted to worship and asceticism. They recount stories and dreams concerning these practices, but all of this is from Satan. Some of them compose poems invoking the dead, seeking their intercession and calling upon them for help, or include such appeals in their praise of the prophets and the righteous. None of this is prescribed, obligatory, or recommended, by the consensus of the imams of the Muslims. Whoever performs an act of worship that is neither obligatory nor recommended, believing it to be obligatory or recommended, is misguided and has introduced a bad religious innovation, not a good one, by the consensus of the imams of the religion. For Allah is to be worshipped only through what is obligatory or recommended.

Many people speak of benefits and advantages in these forms of polytheism and argue in their favour on the basis of opinion, personal experience, imitation, dreams, and the like.

These people may be answered in two ways:

The first is through scriptural texts and consensus. The second is through analogy, experience, and reflection, explaining the harm involved in these practices, for their harm outweighs whatever benefit is imagined to lie in them.

As for the first, the answer is:

It is known as an indisputable fact of the religion of Islam, established through mass transmission and the consensus of the early generations of this community and its imams, that these practices are neither obligatory nor recommended.

It is also known that neither the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, nor any prophet before him prescribed that people invoke angels, prophets, or righteous people, or seek their intercession, whether after their deaths or in their absence. Thus, no one should say:

“O angels of Allah, intercede for me with Allah. Ask Allah to grant us victory, provide for us, or guide us.”

---

## Page 163

And likewise, no one should say to a prophet or righteous person who has died:

“O Prophet of Allah, O Messenger of Allah, pray to Allah for me. Ask Allah on my behalf. Ask Allah to forgive me. Ask Allah to forgive me, guide me, grant me victory, or grant me well-being.”

Nor should anyone say: “I complain to you of my sins, my diminished provision, or the enemy’s domination over me,” or: “I complain to you of so-and-so, who has wronged me.” Nor should anyone say: “I am staying with you; I am your guest; I am your neighbour,” or: “You grant protection to whoever seeks it,” or: “You are the best refuge in which to seek shelter.”

Nor should anyone write a paper and hang it at graves, or draw up a written statement declaring that he has sought protection from so-and-so and take that statement to someone who will act upon it, or engage in similar practices performed by those who introduce religious innovations among the People of the Book and the Muslims. Christians do such things in their churches, as do innovators among the Muslims at the graves of prophets and righteous people, or in their absence.

It is an indisputable fact of the religion of Islam, established through mass transmission and the consensus of the Muslims, that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, did not prescribe these practices for his community. Likewise, the prophets before him prescribed none of these things. Indeed, the People of the Book possess no transmitted account from the prophets supporting such practices, just as the Muslims possess no transmitted account from their Prophet supporting them.

None of their Prophet’s Companions, nor those who followed them in righteousness, did this. Nor did any of the imams of the Muslims consider it recommended—neither the four imams nor anyone else. No imam stated, whether in works on the rites of Hajj or elsewhere, that it is recommended for anyone to ask the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, at his grave to intercede for him or pray for his community, or to complain to him of the calamities that have befallen his community in worldly or religious matters.

After his death, his Companions were afflicted with various trials: at times with drought, at times with diminished provision, at times with fear and the strength of the enemy, and at times with sins and acts of disobedience

---

## Page 164

and none of them would go to the grave of the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, or to the grave of Abraham, Allah’s intimate friend, or to the grave of any prophet, and say:

“We complain to you of drought, the strength of the enemy, or the abundance of sins.”

Nor would anyone say: “Ask Allah on our behalf, or on behalf of your community, to provide for them, grant them victory, or forgive them.” Rather, these and similar practices are newly introduced religious innovations that none of the imams of the Muslims considered recommended. By agreement of the imams of the Muslims, they are neither obligatory nor recommended.

Every innovation that is neither obligatory nor recommended is an evil innovation and, by agreement of the Muslims, a form of misguidance. Those who describe certain innovations as good innovations do so only when there is evidence from the revealed law that they are recommended. As for anything that is neither recommended nor obligatory, no Muslim says that it is among the good deeds through which one draws nearer to Allah.

Whoever seeks to draw nearer to Allah through something that is not among the good deeds commanded as either obligatory or recommended is misguided and follows Satan; his path is one of Satan’s paths, as ‘Abd Allah ibn Mas‘ūd reported:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, drew a line for us, then drew other lines to its right and left. He then said: ‘This is Allah’s path, and these are other paths; at the head of each is a devil calling people to it.’ Then he recited: ‘And indeed, this is My straight path, so follow it, and do not follow other paths, for they will separate you from His path.’”

This is a comprehensive principle that everyone who believes in Allah and His Messenger must follow. No one should depart from the established Sunnah and the way of the earliest pioneers among the Muhājirūn and the Ansār, and those who followed them in righteousness, by following someone who opposes the Sunnah and the earlier consensus—especially when no imam of the Muslims supports him in his innovation, nor any scholar qualified to exercise independent reasoning whose judgment in religious matters may be relied upon, nor anyone

---

## Page 165

whose opinion carries weight in matters of consensus and disagreement. His dissent therefore does not invalidate a consensus, nor does consensus depend on his agreement. Even if a scholar qualified to exercise independent reasoning had disputed this, he would have been refuted by the mass-transmitted Sunnah and the agreement of the imams before him. How much more so when the objector is not qualified to exercise independent reasoning and has no evidence from the revealed law, but merely follows someone who speaks about religion without knowledge and disputes concerning Allah without knowledge, guidance, or an enlightening Book.

Indeed, not only did the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, not prescribe this—so it is neither obligatory nor recommended—but he also forbade it and whatever leads to it, just as he forbade taking the graves of prophets and righteous people as places of prayer.

In Sahīh Muslim, Jundub ibn ‘Abd Allah reports that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said five days before his death:

“Those before you used to take graves as places of prayer. Beware! Do not take graves as places of prayer, for I forbid you to do so.”

In the two Sahīh collections, ‘Ā’ishah reports that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said before his death:

“May Allah curse the Jews and Christians: they took the graves of their prophets as places of prayer.”

He was warning against what they had done. ‘Ā’ishah said: “Were it not for that, his grave would have been left exposed, but making it a place of prayer was considered objectionable.”

Taking a location as a place of prayer means setting it aside for the five daily prayers and other prayers, just as mosques are built for that purpose. A place set aside for prayer is intended for worshipping Allah and supplicating to Him, not for supplicating to created beings. Thus he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, forbade taking their graves as places of prayer with the intention of praying there, just as one goes to mosques for prayer—even if the person going there intends only to worship Allah alone—because this is

---

## Page 166

a means—unless they go to the mosque for the sake of the grave’s occupant, to invoke him, to supplicate through him, or to supplicate beside him. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, therefore forbade setting aside this place even for worshipping Allah alone, lest it become a means of associating partners with Allah. An act that leads to harm and has no outweighing benefit is forbidden.

Similarly, he forbade prayer at the three specified times because of the outweighing harm involved: imitating the polytheists in a way that leads to associating partners with Allah. Deliberately choosing those times for prayer brings no outweighing benefit, since voluntary prayers can be offered at other times.

For this reason, scholars disagreed about prayers occasioned by a specific cause. Many permitted them at these times, and this is the stronger of the two scholarly opinions. When a prohibition serves to prevent a means to wrongdoing, an outweighing benefit warrants an exception. Prayers occasioned by a specific cause may need to be performed at these times; if they are not, the opportunity to perform them is lost, together with their benefit. They are therefore permitted because of their outweighing benefit. By contrast, prayers without a specific cause can be performed at another time. Prohibiting them at these times thus entails no loss of an outweighing benefit, while the harm involved warrants their prohibition.

If his prohibition of prayer at these times was intended to block a means to associating partners with Allah, lest it lead to prostrating to the sun, invoking it, and asking it for things—as do those who invoke the sun, moon, and stars and petition them—then it is clear that invoking the sun and prostrating to it are forbidden in themselves, and far more strictly forbidden than the prayer he prohibited lest it lead to invoking the heavenly bodies.

Likewise, when he forbade taking the graves of prophets and righteous people as places of prayer—thereby forbidding

---

## Page 167

deliberately going to them to pray beside them, lest this lead to invoking their occupants and prostrating to them—invoking them and prostrating to them was far more strictly forbidden than taking their graves as places of prayer.

For this reason, visits to the graves of Muslims are of two kinds: prescribed visits and innovated visits. A prescribed visit is one in which the visitor’s purpose is to supplicate for the deceased, just as the purpose of offering the funeral prayer is to supplicate for him. Standing at his grave is thus of the same nature as praying over him. Allah Most High said concerning the hypocrites:

“Never pray over any of them who dies, nor stand at his grave.”

He forbade His Prophet to pray over them or stand at their graves because they disbelieved in Allah and His Messenger and died as disbelievers. Since He forbade both acts for this reason—disbelief—this indicates that the prohibition ceases to apply when that reason is absent. Singling them out for this prohibition also indicates that funeral prayers may be offered for others and that one may stand at their graves. Had this not been prescribed for anyone, they would not have been singled out for the prohibition, nor would their disbelief have been given as its reason.

For this reason, praying over deceased believers and standing at their graves are part of the Sunnah established through mass transmission. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, would pray over deceased Muslims and prescribed this for his community. Whenever a man from his community was buried, he would stand at his grave and say:

“Ask for him to be granted steadfastness, for he is now being questioned.”

This was narrated by Abū Dāwūd and others. He would visit the graves of those buried in al-Baqīʿ and of the martyrs at Uhud. He also taught his Companions that, when visiting graves, each of them should say:

“Peace be upon you, believers and Muslims who inhabit these abodes. We shall, if Allah Most High wills, join you. May Allah have mercy on those of us and of you who have gone before, and on those who come later. We ask Allah for well-being for us and for you. O Allah, do not deprive us of the reward on their account, and do not put us to trial after them.”

And in the Sahīh of

---

## Page 168

Muslim, it is narrated from Abū Hurayrah, may Allah be pleased with him, that Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, went out to the cemetery and said:

“Peace be upon you, believers who inhabit these abodes. We shall, if Allah wills, join you.”

The hadiths concerning this are authentic and well known. The purpose of this visit to the graves of believers is to supplicate for them. This differs from the form of visitation that is also permissible at the graves of disbelievers. As established in Sahīh Muslim and the collections of Abū Dāwūd, al-Nasā’ī and Ibn Mājah, Abū Hurayrah said:

“Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, came to his mother’s grave and wept, causing those around him to weep. He then said, ‘I asked my Lord’s permission to seek forgiveness for her, but He did not grant me permission. Then I asked His permission to visit her grave, and He granted me permission. So visit graves, for they remind you of the Hereafter.’”

This visit, which serves as a reminder of death, is prescribed even if the person buried is a disbeliever. By contrast, a visit intended to supplicate for the deceased is prescribed only for believers.

As for an innovated visit, it is one intended to ask the deceased to fulfil one’s needs, to ask him to supplicate or intercede, or to supplicate at his grave because the visitor supposes that supplications are more readily answered there. Visits made for any of these purposes are innovations: the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, did not prescribe them, nor did the Companions practise them, whether at the Prophet’s grave, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, or at any other grave. They fall within the category of shirk (associating partners with Allah) and the practices that lead to it.

Even if someone intended to pray at the graves of prophets and righteous people without intending to invoke them or to supplicate beside them—for instance, by taking their graves as places of prayer—this would still be unlawful and forbidden, and the person doing so would expose himself to Allah’s anger and curse. As the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Allah’s anger was severe against a people who took the graves of their prophets as places of prayer.”

He also said:

“May Allah curse the Jews and Christians: they took the graves of their prophets as places of prayer.”

He was warning against what they had done. He also said:

“Indeed, those who came before you used to

---

## Page 169

take graves as places of prayer. Beware: do not take graves as places of prayer, for I forbid you to do so.”

If this is forbidden and brings the Lord’s anger and curse, then what of someone who deliberately invokes the deceased, supplicates beside him and through him, and believes that these are means of having supplications answered, obtaining what he seeks and having his needs fulfilled?

This was the initial cause of shirk among Noah’s people and of idol worship among humankind. Ibn ‘Abbās said:

“There were ten generations between Adam and Noah, all of them following Islam. Then shirk arose through the veneration of the graves of their righteous people.”

It has been widely transmitted from Ibn ‘Abbās and others, in Sahīh al-Bukhārī, works of Quranic commentary and accounts of the prophets, concerning Allah’s words:

“And they said, ‘Never leave your gods and never leave Wadd or Suwā‘ or Yaghūth and Ya‘ūq and Nasr.’”

These were righteous men among Noah’s people. When they died, the people remained devotedly at their graves, then made images of them and worshipped them. Ibn ‘Abbās said: “These idols subsequently came to be found among the Arab tribes.”

A group of heretical philosophers who held the world to be eternal introduced yet another aspect of shirk in their discussions of visiting graves. Ibn Sīnā discussed this, as did those who took their ideas from him, such as the author of al-Kutub al-Madnūn bihā (The Withheld Books) and others. They explained intercession according to their own foundational doctrine. They do not acknowledge that Allah created the heavens and the earth in six days, or that He knows particulars, hears the voices of His servants and answers their supplications.

According to their doctrine, the intercession of prophets and righteous people is not what the believers understand it to be: a supplication offered by a righteous man which Allah answers. Likewise, when rain falls following their supplication for rain, they do not consider its cause to be the answering of their supplication. Rather, they claim that what brings about events in the world is the powers of the soul or the movements

---

## Page 170

of the heavenly spheres or natural forces. They say:

When a person loves a righteous man who has died, especially if he visits his grave, his spirit becomes connected to the spirit of the deceased. Whatever emanates upon that disembodied spirit from what they regard as the active intellect or the celestial soul also emanates upon the spirit of the visitor seeking intercession, without Allah knowing anything of this. Indeed, even the spirit whose intercession is sought may be unaware of it. They liken this to the sun: when a mirror faces it, sunlight falls upon the mirror; when another mirror faces the first, light passes from the first mirror to the second. If a wall or water faces that mirror, its reflected light falls upon it. This is their conception of intercession, and this is how, according to them, the visitor benefits.

The forms of disbelief contained in this view are plain to anyone who reflects upon it. There is no doubt that the presence of devils at idols, their speech and their activities are among the causes of humanity’s misguidance. Turning graves into idols was the beginning of shirk. Thus, at graves, some people hear speech, see a figure or witness extraordinary activity that they suppose comes from the deceased, though it may come from jinn and devils. For example, a person may see the grave split open and the deceased emerge, speak to him and embrace him. Such things are witnessed at the graves of prophets and others, but it is only a devil. A devil can assume human forms, and one of them claims to be Prophet So-and-so or Shaykh So-and-so, lying in doing so.

There are very many incidents of this kind, more than can be recounted here. An ignorant person supposes that the figure he saw emerge from the grave and embrace or speak to him was the person buried there—a prophet, a righteous person or someone else. A believer of great stature, however, knows that it is a devil. This becomes clear through several things:

---

## Page 171

The first is to recite the Verse of the Throne sincerely. When he recites it, that figure disappears, sinks into the ground, or hides from view. If he were a righteous man, a king, or a believing jinn, the Verse of the Throne would not harm him; it harms only devils. This is established in the authentic collection through the report of Abū Hurayra, to whom the jinn said:

“Recite the Verse of the Throne when you go to bed. A guardian from Allah will remain over you, and no devil will approach you until morning.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “He told you the truth, though he is a habitual liar.”

Another is to seek refuge in Allah from the devils.

Another is to seek refuge through the supplications prescribed by revelation. Devils would confront the prophets during their lifetimes, seeking to harm them and disrupt their worship. Thus, the jinn came to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, carrying a flame with which they intended to burn him. Gabriel brought him the well-known supplication for protection contained in the hadith related from Abū al-Tayyāh, who said:

“A man asked ʿAbd al-Rahmān ibn Hubaysh—an elderly man who had lived in the Prophet’s time—‘What did the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, do when the devils plotted against him?’

“He replied: ‘They descended upon him from the mountain passes and valleys. Among them was a devil carrying a flame with which he intended to burn the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.’

“He said: ‘The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was frightened. Gabriel, peace be upon him, came to him and said, “O Muhammad, say…” He asked, “What should I say?” Gabriel said, “Say: I seek refuge in the perfect words of Allah, which neither the righteous nor the wicked can overstep, from the evil of what He has created, brought forth, and brought into being; from the evil of what descends from heaven and what ascends into it; from the evil of what emerges from the earth and what descends into it; from the evil of the trials of night and day; and from the evil of every night visitor who comes, except one who comes with good, O Most Merciful.”’

“He said: ‘Their fires were extinguished, and Allah, Mighty and Majestic, defeated them.’”

---

## Page 172

It is also established in the two Sahīh collections that Abū Hurayra said:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: ‘Last night, a cunning, wicked jinn came to attack me and interrupt my prayer. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, gave me power over him, and I throttled him. I wanted to seize him and tie him to a pillar in the mosque so that you could see him in the morning. Then I remembered the words of Solomon, peace be upon him: “My Lord, forgive me and grant me a kingdom that will belong to no one after me.” So Allah Most High drove him away, humiliated.’”

Ā’isha reported:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was praying when Satan came to him. He seized him, threw him down, and strangled him. The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: ‘I felt the coolness of his tongue against my hand. Had it not been for Solomon’s supplication, he would have remained bound until morning for people to see.’”

Al-Nasā’ī recorded this report, and its chain of transmission meets al-Bukhārī’s criteria, as Abū Abd Allāh al-Maqdisī stated in his selected collection, which is superior to al-Hākim’s Sahīh.

Abū Sa‘īd al-Khudrī reported:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was performing the dawn prayer, with Abū Sa‘īd behind him, when his recitation became confused. When he finished his prayer, he said: ‘If only you had seen me with Iblīs! I reached out with my hand and kept strangling him until I felt the coolness of his saliva between these two fingers of mine—the thumb and the finger next to it. Had it not been for the supplication of my brother Solomon, he would have remained tied to one of the mosque’s pillars until morning, with the children of Medina playing with him. Whoever can prevent anyone from coming between him and the direction of prayer should do so.’”

Imam Ahmad related it in his Musnad, and Abū Dāwūd in his Sunan.

In Sahīh Muslim, Abū al-Dardā’ said:

“The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, stood up to pray. We heard him say, ‘I seek refuge in Allah from you.’ Then he said, ‘I curse you with Allah’s curse,’ three times, and stretched out his hand as though reaching for something. When he finished his prayer, we said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, we heard you say something during the prayer that we had never heard you say before, and we saw you stretch out your hand.’ He said, ‘The enemy of Allah

---

## Page 173

, Iblīs, came with a flame of fire to thrust into my face. I said, “I seek refuge in Allah from you,” three times. Then I said, “I curse you with Allah’s complete curse,” and he drew back. I then wanted to seize him. Had it not been for the supplication of our brother Solomon, he would have remained bound until morning, with the children of Medina playing with him.’”

If devils come to the prophets, peace and blessings be upon them, to harm them and disrupt their worship, and Allah Most High repels them through the supplication, remembrance, worship, and physical struggle with which He strengthens the prophets, then what of those who are below the prophets in rank?

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, subdued the devils among humans and jinn through the various forms of knowledge and action with which Allah Most High strengthened him. Among the greatest of these are prayer and jihad. Most of the Prophet’s hadiths concern prayer and jihad. Whoever follows the prophets will receive Allah’s help through the same means by which He helped the prophets.

As for someone who introduces a religion that they did not prescribe, abandoning their command to worship Allah alone, without partner, and to follow His Prophet in what he prescribed for his community, while introducing excessive veneration of the prophets and the righteous and associating them with Allah in worship—such a person is manipulated by the devils. Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, he has no authority over those who believe and put their trust in their Lord.”

“His authority is only over those who take him as an ally and those who, through him, associate others with Allah.”

And Allah Most High says:

“Indeed, you have no authority over My servants, except those among the misguided who follow you.”

And (another means is for the person who sees such an apparition to call upon his Lord, Blessed and Most High, to clarify the matter for him).

And (another means is to say to that figure, “Are you so-and-so?” and adjure him with solemn oaths, reciting over him the Quranic passages that repel devils, along with other means that harm them).

This is like the way many worshippers see the Kaaba circling around them and see a mighty throne

---

## Page 174

with an immense figure upon it, and see figures ascending and descending, whom they suppose to be angels. They imagine that the figure on the throne is Allah—exalted and holy is He—whereas it is a devil.

This has happened to more than one person. Some were protected by Allah and recognized that it was a devil, such as Shaykh Abd al-Qādir in his well-known account. He said:

“Once, while engaged in worship, I saw a mighty throne with a light upon it. It said to me, ‘O Abd al-Qādir, I am your Lord, and I have made lawful for you what I have forbidden to others.’”

He said:

“I replied, ‘Are you Allah, besides whom there is no deity? Begone, enemy of Allah!’”

He said:

“That light then broke apart and became darkness. It said, ‘O Abd al-Qādir, you have escaped me through your understanding of your religion, your knowledge, and your encounters during your spiritual states. I have led seventy men astray through this very deception.’”

He was asked, “How did you know that it was a devil?” He replied:

“Because it said to me, ‘I have made lawful for you what I have forbidden to others.’ I knew that the law of Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, cannot be abrogated or changed. Moreover, it said, ‘I am your Lord,’ but could not say, ‘I am Allah; there is no deity but Me.’”

Others believed that what they saw was Allah, and they and their companions came to believe that they were seeing Allah Most High while awake, basing this belief on what they had witnessed. They are truthful in what they report, but they did not know that it was a devil.

This has frequently happened among groups of ignorant worshippers. One of them imagines that he sees Allah Most High with his own eyes in this world, for many of them have seen something they thought was Allah, when it was only a devil. Many have also seen someone they supposed to be a prophet, a righteous man, or al-Khidr, when it was a devil.

It is established in the authentic hadith collection that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “Whoever sees me in

---

## Page 175

a dream has truly seen me, for Satan cannot take my form.”

This concerns seeing him in a dream, since dreams may be true or may come from Satan. Allah has therefore prevented Satan from assuming his form in dreams. As for wakefulness, no one sees him with his own eyes in this world. Whoever supposes that the figure he sees is the deceased person has been misled by his own ignorance. This is why nothing of this kind happened to any of the Companions or those who followed them faithfully.

Some who have seen such a thing—or believed someone who claimed to have seen it—have concluded that a single person can be in two places at the same time, thus contradicting plain reason. Some say, “This is the raqīqa of the person seen,” or, “This is his spiritual form,” or, “This is his essence taking shape.” They do not realize that it is a jinn that has assumed his appearance. Others suppose that it is an angel, although angels differ from jinn in many respects. Among the jinn are disbelievers, evildoers, and the ignorant; among them are also believers who follow Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him abundant peace. Thus many who do not realize that these beings are jinn and devils regard them as angels.

Likewise, those who invoke the stars and other idols may have a spirit descend upon them, which they describe as the spiritual form of the stars. Some suppose that it is an angel, whereas it is only one of the jinn and devils who lead those who associate partners with Allah astray.

The devils ally themselves with those who do what they love: associating partners with Allah, wickedness, and disobedience. Sometimes they tell such a person about certain hidden matters so that he may disclose them to others. At other times, they harm someone he wishes to harm, by killing him, making him ill, or the like.

---

## Page 176

The devils ally themselves with those who do what they love: associating partners with Allah, wickedness, and disobedience. Sometimes they tell such a person about certain hidden matters so that he may disclose them to others. At other times, they harm someone he wishes to harm, by killing him, making him ill, or the like. Sometimes they bring him whichever person he desires. Sometimes they steal people’s property for him—money, food, clothing, and other things—and he believes these to be miraculous gifts granted to Allah’s allies, when they are merely stolen goods. Sometimes they carry him through the air to a distant place.

Among them are people whom they take to Mecca on the afternoon of the Day of Arafah and then bring back. Such a person believes this to be a miraculous gift, although he has not performed the pilgrimage as Muslims do: he has neither entered ihrām (ritual consecration), nor recited the talbiya (pilgrimage invocation), nor circumambulated the House, nor traversed the distance between al-Safā and al-Marwa. It is well known that this is among the gravest forms of misguidance.

Some go to Mecca to circumambulate the House without undertaking an ‘umra (lesser pilgrimage) in accordance with Islamic law, and do not enter ihrām when they come abreast of the mīqāt (prescribed boundary for entering ihrām). It is well known that anyone intending to perform pilgrimage rites in Mecca may not pass beyond the mīqāt without entering ihrām. Even if he intends to go there for trade, to visit a relative, or to seek knowledge, he is likewise instructed to enter ihrām at the mīqāt. Is this obligatory or recommended? Scholars hold two well-known opinions on the matter.

This is a broad subject. It includes sorcery and soothsaying, and it has been discussed at length elsewhere. The idol-worshipping polytheists, and those Christians and innovators within this community who resemble them, have so many accounts of such things that describing them would take a long time. No one habitually invokes a dead person and seeks his aid—whether a prophet or someone else—without having heard accounts of such things that have contributed to his misguidance.

Likewise, those who invoke them in their absence and seek their aid see someone who has their appearance, or whom they believe to have their appearance. He says, “I am so-and-so,” speaks to them, and fulfils some of their needs. They then suppose that the deceased person whose aid they sought is the one who spoke to them and fulfilled their request, whereas it is merely one of the jinn and devils.

---

## Page 177

Some of them say, “He is an angel.” Yet angels do not assist polytheists; these are only devils who have led them astray from Allah’s path. At places where partners are associated with Allah, there are incidents and accounts known to those who are there and those who have experienced them, too numerous to describe at length.

Those steeped in ignorance fall into two groups regarding these matters: one denies them altogether, while the other regards them as miraculous gifts granted to Allah’s allies. The first group says, “These are merely imaginings in their minds, with no existence in the external world.” When they repeat this to one group after another, some have already seen these things and witnessed their actual occurrence, or received numerous convergent reports of their occurrence from eyewitnesses whose truthfulness is beyond doubt. Such denials become one of the strongest reasons why these polytheists and innovators persist in their ways, having witnessed these things or learned of them through truthful reports.

Then, whenever these very deniers witness any of these things, they submit to the person through whom they occur, obey him, and believe him to be one of Allah’s allies. They do so despite knowing that he does not fulfil the duties Allah has prescribed—not even the five daily prayers—and does not refrain from what Allah has forbidden, whether indecencies or injustice. Indeed, he may be among those furthest removed from the faith and piety with which Allah describes His allies in His words, exalted is He:

“Unquestionably, the allies of Allah—there will be no fear concerning them, nor will they grieve.”

“Those who believed and were mindful of Allah.”

Thus they see someone who is among those furthest removed from faith and piety receiving disclosures of hidden matters

---

## Page 178

and performing extraordinary feats—things they regard as miraculous gifts granted to Allah’s pious allies. Some of them consequently abandon Islam and turn back on their heels, believing that someone who does not pray, does not even believe in the messengers, and indeed insults and disparages them, is among the greatest of Allah’s pious allies. Others remain bewildered, wavering, doubtful, and suspicious, with one foot directed towards disbelief and the other towards Islam. They may even be closer to disbelief than to faith.

This happens because they take as evidence of being Allah’s allies things that do not establish that status. Disbelievers, polytheists, sorcerers, and soothsayers have devils with them who accomplish through them many times more than this. Allah, exalted is He, says:

“Shall I inform you upon whom the devils descend?”

“They descend upon every sinful liar.”

These people inevitably engage in falsehood and violate the revealed law. Their sin and falsehood are commensurate with their departure from the commands and prohibitions of Allah with which He sent His Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Those satanic manifestations result from their misguidance, polytheism, innovation, ignorance, and disbelief, and are evidence and signs of these things. Yet the ignorant, misguided person supposes that they result from their faith and their being allies of Allah, exalted is He, and that they are signs and evidence of their faith and their being allies of Allah, glorified is He.

This is because he has no criterion by which to distinguish the allies of the Most Merciful from the allies of Satan, as we have discussed in our treatment of “The Distinction between the Allies of the Most Merciful and the Allies of Satan.” He does not realize that these manifestations, which he takes as evidence of being Allah’s allies, occur among disbelievers—both polytheists and the People of the Book—to a greater extent than among those who profess Islam. Evidence necessarily entails what it establishes, is exclusive to it, and cannot exist without it. Thus, if

---

## Page 179

such manifestations occur among disbelievers, polytheists, and the People of the Book, they do not necessarily entail faith, let alone being an ally of Allah, nor are they exclusive to either. They therefore cannot serve as evidence of either. Allah’s allies are the believers who fear Him, and their miraculous gifts are the fruit of their faith and piety, not of polytheism, innovation, or wickedness. The greatest of Allah’s allies use these miraculous gifts only to support the religion or to meet a need of the Muslims. Those who follow a moderate course may use them for permissible purposes. But whoever uses them to assist him in sinful acts wrongs himself and transgresses the limits set by his Lord, even if those gifts arose from faith and piety.

If someone fights the enemy and acquires spoils, then spends them in obedience to Satan, that wealth becomes a source of ruin for him, even though he acquired it through a righteous deed. How much worse, then, when extraordinary feats arise from disbelief, wickedness, and disobedience, and lead to further disbelief, wickedness, and disobedience! This is why the leaders of these people acknowledge that most of them die outside Islam. A fuller discussion of these matters belongs elsewhere.

The point here is that among the greatest causes of the polytheists’ misguidance is what they see or hear at idols: information about something hidden from them, something that brings about the fulfillment of a need, or the like. When one of them sees a grave split open and an impressive-looking sheikh emerge from it, embrace him, or speak to him, he supposes that this is the prophet or sheikh buried there. Yet the grave has not split open; Satan merely made it appear so to him

---

## Page 180

just as he makes one of them imagine that a wall has split open and a human figure has emerged from it. In reality, it is Satan appearing to him in human form and making him think that he emerged from the wall.

Among these people are those who say to the figure they saw emerge from the grave: “We do not remain in our graves. Rather, as soon as one of us is buried, he leaves his grave and walks among the people.” Others see the deceased walking in his own funeral procession and taking them by the hand. There are other such manifestations, familiar to those acquainted with them.

The misguided either deny these manifestations or suppose them to be miraculous gifts granted to Allah’s allies. They imagine that the figure is the prophet or righteous man himself, or an angel in his likeness. Sometimes they say, “This is his spiritual presence, his raqīqa, his inner secret, his likeness, or his spirit embodied.” Someone may even see that figure in two places and suppose that a single body can occupy two places at the same time, not realizing that the being which has assumed his likeness is not that human being.

These and similar occurrences show that those who invoke prophets and righteous people after their deaths, whether at their graves or elsewhere, are among the polytheists who invoke others besides Allah—like those who invoke the heavenly bodies and those who take angels and prophets as lords. Allah Most High says:

“It is not for a human being to whom Allah has given the Scripture, wisdom, and prophethood to then say to the people, ‘Be servants to me rather than Allah.’ Rather, ‘Be devoted scholars of the Lord because of your teaching of the Scripture and your study of it.’”

“Nor would he command you to take the angels and prophets as lords. Would he command you to disbelieve after you have become Muslims?”

Allah Most High also says: “Say, ‘Invoke those whom you claim besides Him; they have no power to

---

## Page 181

remove harm from you or shift it elsewhere.’”

“Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of drawing near to their Lord, striving to be closest to Him. They hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Indeed, your Lord’s punishment is ever to be feared.”

Allah Most High also says:

“Say, ‘Invoke those whom you claim besides Allah. They do not possess even an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth. They have no share in either, nor does He have any helper among them.’”

“Nor is intercession of any avail with Him except for one to whom He gives permission.”

There are many passages like these in the Quran, forbidding the invocation of anyone other than Allah—whether angels, prophets, or anyone else—for this constitutes polytheism or is a means leading to it. This differs from asking one of them during his lifetime to supplicate or intercede, which does not lead to that. None of the prophets or righteous people was worshipped during his lifetime in his presence, since he would forbid anyone who did so. By contrast, invoking them after their deaths is a means leading to associating them with Allah in worship. Likewise, invoking them in their absence is a means leading to polytheism.

If someone sees a prophet or an angel and says to him, “Supplicate for me,” this does not lead to associating him with Allah in worship. Invoking him in his absence, however, does lead to that, as has indeed happened. Someone who is absent or dead cannot forbid those who associate him with Allah in worship. Rather, when hearts become attached to his supplication and intercession, this leads to associating him with Allah in worship: a person then invokes him and seeks out the site of his grave or image, or something else of that kind. This is what the polytheists have fallen into, as have those who resemble them among the People of the Book and religious innovators among Muslims.

It is well known that the angels supplicate for the believers and seek forgiveness for them, as Allah Most High says:

“Those who bear the Throne and those around it glorify their Lord with praise, believe in Him, and seek forgiveness

---

## Page 182

for those who believe: ‘Our Lord, You encompass all things in mercy and knowledge, so forgive those who repent and follow Your way, and protect them from the punishment of Hell.’”

“Our Lord, admit them to the Gardens of everlasting residence which You have promised them, along with the righteous among their parents, spouses, and descendants. Indeed, You are the Almighty, the All-Wise.”

“And protect them from evil deeds. Whoever You protect from evil deeds on that Day, You have indeed shown him mercy. That is the supreme triumph.”

Allah Most High also says:

“The heavens almost break apart above them, while the angels glorify their Lord with praise and seek forgiveness for those on earth. Indeed, Allah is the All-Forgiving, the Most Merciful.”

“As for those who take protectors besides Him, Allah watches over them, and you are not a guardian over them.”

Thus, the angels seek forgiveness for the believers without anyone asking them. Likewise, reports that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, or other prophets and righteous people supplicate and intercede for the upright members of his community fall into this same category: they do what Allah has permitted them to do without anyone asking them.

Since invoking the angels is not prescribed, neither is invoking prophets and righteous people who have died, nor asking them to supplicate and intercede—even if they are invoked and intercede. This is for two reasons:

First, whatever Allah has commanded them to do in this regard, they do even without being asked; and whatever they have not been commanded to do, they do not do even if asked. There is therefore no benefit in asking them.

Second, invoking them and asking them to intercede in these circumstances leads to associating them with Allah in worship. It therefore entails this harm. Even supposing it had some benefit, this harm would outweigh it—how much more so when it has no benefit at all! This differs from asking them during their lifetime and in their presence, for there is no harm

---

## Page 183

in this, for they forbid associating them with Allah in worship. Indeed, it has a benefit: they are rewarded and recompensed for benefiting all creation at that time, since they are still in the abode of deeds and religious obligations. Their intercession in the Hereafter manifests the honor Allah bestows upon them on the Day of Resurrection.

As a general rule, asking other people to meet worldly needs that they are under no obligation to fulfill is neither obligatory nor recommended for the person asking. Rather, what is commanded is to ask Allah Most High, turn one’s hopes to Him, and rely upon Him.

Asking other people is, in principle, forbidden, but it has been permitted out of necessity. Refraining from it in reliance upon Allah is better. Allah Most High says:

“So when you have finished your duties, devote yourself to worship.”

“And to your Lord direct your longing.”

That is, turn your hopes to Allah, not to anyone else. Allah Most High also says:

“If only they had been satisfied with what Allah and His Messenger gave them and had said, ‘Allah is sufficient for us. Allah will give us of His bounty, and so will His Messenger. Indeed, to Allah we turn our hopes.’”

Thus, He ascribed giving to Allah and His Messenger, in keeping with His words:

“Whatever the Messenger gives you, take it; and whatever he forbids you, refrain from it.”

He therefore commanded them to please Allah and His Messenger.

As for sufficiency, however, He commanded them to say, “Allah is sufficient for us,” not, “Allah and His Messenger are sufficient for us.” He also commanded them to say:

“Indeed, to Allah we turn our hopes.”

He did not command them to say, “To Allah and His Messenger we turn our hopes.” Thus, one’s hopes are to be directed to Allah alone, as He says in another verse:

“And whoever obeys Allah and His Messenger, fears Allah, and remains mindful of Him—it is they who will triumph.”

Thus, He made obedience due to Allah and His Messenger, while fear and piety are directed to Allah alone.

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to Ibn ‘Abbās:

“Young boy, I shall teach you a few words: Be mindful of Allah, and He will protect you. Be mindful of Allah, and you will find Him before you. Draw near to Allah in times of ease, and He will care for you in times of hardship. When you ask, ask Allah; and when you seek help, seek help from Allah. The pen has dried concerning what

---

## Page 184

you will encounter. Even if all creation strove to harm you, they could harm you only with something Allah had decreed for you. If you can act for Allah’s sake with contentment and certainty, then do so. If you cannot, there is much good in patiently enduring what you dislike.”

This hadith is well known and widely circulated, though it is sometimes narrated in an abridged form. His words,

“When you ask, ask Allah; and when you seek help, seek help from Allah,”

are among the most authentically transmitted sayings from him.

Ahmad’s Musnad relates that when Abū Bakr al-Siddīq’s whip fell from his hand, he would not ask anyone to hand it to him. He would say, “My dear friend instructed me not to ask people for anything.”

Sahīh Muslim relates from ‘Awf ibn Mālik that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, took a pledge from a group of his Companions and quietly confided an instruction to them: “Do not ask people for anything.” ‘Awf said, “I have seen some of those men drop their whips without asking anyone to hand them back.”

The two Sahīh collections relate that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Seventy thousand of my community will enter Paradise without being brought to account.” He also said, “They are those who do not seek healing recitations from others, do not resort to cauterization, do not take omens, and put their trust in their Lord.”

He praised these people for not seeking healing recitations—that is, they do not ask anyone to perform such recitations for them. Healing recitation is a form of supplication, so they do not ask anyone to do this for them. The wording “nor do they perform healing recitations” has also been transmitted, but it is an error: performing such recitations for others and for oneself is a good deed. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, used to perform healing recitations for himself and for others, but he did not ask others to perform them for him. His recitations for himself and for others were a form of supplication for himself and for others, and this is commanded. All the prophets asked Allah and supplicated to Him, as Allah mentions in the accounts of Adam, Abraham, Moses, and others.

---

## Page 185

As for the report that when Abraham, Allah’s intimate friend, was placed in the catapult, Gabriel said to him, “Ask,” and he replied, “His knowledge of my condition suffices me without my asking,” it has no known chain of transmission and is false. Rather, the report established in the Sahīh from Ibn ‘Abbās gives the words:

“Allah is sufficient for me, and He is the best Disposer of affairs.”

Ibn ‘Abbās said: “Abraham said these words when he was cast into the fire, and Muhammad said them when:

‘People said to them, “The people have gathered against you, so fear them.”’”

It has also been reported that Gabriel asked, “Do you have any need?” Abraham replied, “From you, no.” Imām Ahmad and others have mentioned this. As for Abraham’s asking his Lord, mighty and majestic, this is mentioned in several places in the Quran. How, then, could he say, “His knowledge of my condition suffices me without my asking”?

Allah knows all things, yet He has commanded His servants to worship Him, put their trust in Him, and ask Him. For He, glorified be He, has made these acts means through which He brings about their results: rewarding those who worship Him and answering those who ask Him. He, glorified be He, knows things as they truly are. His knowledge that one person is in need or another has sinned does not preclude His commanding the sinner to repent and seek forgiveness, or the person in need to supplicate and employ other means through which his need is met. Likewise, He commands a person to worship and obey Him, thereby attaining the honor He bestows.

At certain times, however, a servant may be commanded to do something better than supplication, as related in the hadith:

“Whoever is so occupied with remembering Me that he does not ask Me, I give him the best of what I give those who ask.”

In al-Tirmidhī, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is reported to have said:

“Whoever is so occupied with reciting the Quran that he does not remember Me or ask Me, I give him the best of what I give those who ask.”

Al-Tirmidhī said: “This is a good, singular hadith.”

The best physical act of worship is prayer. It includes recitation, remembrance, and supplication, and each

---

## Page 186

one is prescribed in its proper place. While standing, after the opening supplication, one recites the Quran. During bowing and prostration, one is forbidden to recite the Quran and is commanded to glorify Allah and remember Him. At the end of the prayer, one is commanded to supplicate, just as the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, used to supplicate at the end of the prayer and instruct others to do so. Supplication during prostration is good and prescribed. It is also permissible to supplicate while standing and bowing, although recitation and remembrance are generally superior. The point is that a servant’s asking his Lord in the prescribed manner is good and commanded.

Allah’s intimate friend and others did ask Him. Allah Most High relates his words:

“Our Lord, I have settled some of my descendants in an uncultivated valley near Your Sacred House, our Lord, so that they may establish prayer. So make hearts among the people incline toward them and provide them with fruits, so that they may be grateful.”

“Our Lord, You know what we conceal and what we reveal. Nothing on earth or in heaven is hidden from Allah.”

“Praise be to Allah, who has granted me Ishmael and Isaac in old age. Indeed, my Lord hears supplication.”

“My Lord, make me one who establishes prayer, and also some of my descendants. Our Lord, accept my supplication.”

“Our Lord, forgive me, my parents, and the believers on the Day when the reckoning takes place.”

Allah Most High also says:

“And when Abraham and Ishmael were raising the foundations of the House, they said, ‘Our Lord, accept this from us. Indeed, You are the All-Hearing, the All-Knowing.’”

“Our Lord, make us both submissive to You, and from our descendants a nation submissive to You. Show us our rites and accept our repentance. Indeed, You are the Accepter of repentance, the Most Merciful.”

“Our Lord, send among them a messenger from themselves who will recite Your verses to them, teach them the Book and wisdom, and purify them. Indeed, You are the Almighty, the All-Wise.”

Likewise, a Muslim’s supplication for his brother is good and prescribed. It is established in the Sahīh, on the authority of Abū al-Dardā’, that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“No man supplicates

---

## Page 187

for his brother in his absence without Allah appointing an angel for him. Whenever he offers a supplication for his brother, the appointed angel says, ‘Āmīn, and may you have the same.’” That is, the same as what you asked for your brother.

As for asking another person to meet one’s own needs or to supplicate for one, this has not been commanded. Asking for knowledge, however, is different: Allah has commanded it, as in His words, Most High:

“So ask the people of knowledge if you do not know.”

Allah Most High also says:

“So if you are in doubt about what We have revealed to you, ask those who have been reading the Scripture before you.”

And He says:

“Ask those of Our messengers whom We sent before you: Have We appointed any gods besides the Most Merciful to be worshipped?”

This is because knowledge must be shared. Whoever is asked about something he knows and conceals it will be bridled by Allah with a bridle of fire on the Day of Resurrection. Knowledge grows through teaching; it does not diminish through teaching as wealth diminishes when it is given away. For this reason, it is likened to a lamp.

Likewise, whoever has a right to something held by another, whether specific property or a debt—such as property entrusted for safekeeping or capital entrusted under a profit-sharing arrangement—may request it from the person holding it. The same applies to fayʾ (public revenues) and other jointly held funds whose distribution is administered by the ruler. A person may claim his rightful share from him, just as he may claim his entitlement from an endowment, an inheritance, or a bequest, because whoever has control of such property must deliver what is due to the person entitled to it.

This category also includes requesting maintenance from someone obliged to provide it, and a traveller requesting hospitality from someone obliged to offer it, just as Moses and al-Khidr asked the people of the town for food. Likewise, a creditor may demand repayment from the person who owes him.

Each party to a contract may ask the other to fulfil what is due to him:

---

## Page 188

the seller asks for the price, and the buyer asks for the item sold. This category includes the words of Allah Most High:

“And fear Allah, in whose name you appeal to one another, and honour the ties of kinship.”

Some requests are not commanded, although the person being asked is commanded to respond. Allah Most High says:

“And do not rebuke the one who asks.”

And He says:

“And those in whose wealth there is a known share for the beggar and the deprived.”

And He says:

“So eat from them and feed the needy and those who come seeking help.”

This also includes the hadith:

“One of you asks me for something, then leaves carrying it under his arm as fire.”

And his words:

“Put an end to this man’s importuning me.”

A request may be prohibited or discouraged, even though the person being asked is commanded to grant it. Giving to those who asked was part of the perfection of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. For him, this was a virtue and a praiseworthy quality, and was obligatory or recommended, even when the requester’s act of asking was itself forbidden. For this reason, it has never been known that Abū Bakr al-Siddīq or other leading Companions asked him for anything of this kind, or asked him to supplicate for them personally. They did, however, ask him to supplicate for the Muslims, as in the following report:
