“During one of his military expeditions, when they sought his permission to slaughter some of their riding animals, ‘Umar advised him, saying: ‘O Messenger of Allah, what will become of us if we meet the enemy tomorrow on foot and hungry? But if you see fit, summon the people with what remains of their provisions, gather them together, and then ask Allah to bless them. Allah will bless us through your supplication.’

In another version: ‘Allah will bring us relief through your supplication.’”

It was only some of the Muslims who asked him for such supplications: the blind man asked him to supplicate to Allah to restore his sight; Umm Sulaym asked him to supplicate to Allah for his servant Anas; and Abū Hurayrah asked him to supplicate to Allah to make him and his mother beloved to His believing servants. There were other such examples.

As for Abū Bakr al-Siddīq, Allah said of him and those like him:

“But the most righteous will be spared it,
who gives his wealth

---

## Page 189

to purify himself,
with no favour owed to anyone to be repaid,
but seeking only the Face of his Lord Most High.
And he will surely be pleased.”

It is established in the authentic hadith collections that he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Of all people, Abū Bakr has been the most generous to us with his companionship and his wealth. Were I to take anyone on earth as an intimate friend, I would take Abū Bakr as an intimate friend.”

Thus, none of the Companions surpassed al-Siddīq in what he offered of himself and his wealth. Abū Bakr did this seeking the Face of his Lord Most High, without seeking any recompense from a created being. Allah Most High therefore said:

“But the most righteous will be spared it,
who gives his wealth to purify himself,
with no favour owed to anyone to be repaid,
but seeking only the Face of his Lord Most High.
And he will surely be pleased.”

Al-Siddīq owed no one a favour requiring repayment, for his earnings and wealth made him independent of everyone. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had bestowed upon al-Siddīq and others the blessing of faith and knowledge. That blessing cannot be repaid, for the Messenger’s reward for it rests with Allah, as He Most High said:

“And I ask you for no reward for it. My reward is only with the Lord of the worlds.”

As for ‘Alī, Zayd and others, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had bestowed upon them favours that could be repaid. Zayd had been his slave, and he freed him. Allah Most High said:

“And when you said to the one whom Allah had favoured and whom you had favoured, ‘Keep your wife.’”

‘Alī was among the dependants of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, because of a drought that had afflicted the people of Mecca. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and al-‘Abbās wished to lighten Abū Tālib’s burden of supporting his dependants. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, therefore took ‘Alī into his household, and al-‘Abbās took Ja‘far into his. This is discussed at length elsewhere.

The point here is that al-Siddīq was the most generous of all people with his companionship and his wealth to the best of

---

## Page 190

creation, the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, because he spent his wealth in Allah’s cause, as when he purchased those who were being tortured.

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had no personal need of either Abū Bakr or anyone else. Indeed, when Abū Bakr said to him during the journey of migration, “I have two riding camels; take one of them,” the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, replied, “For payment.”

He was thus the finest friend of the finest prophet. Part of his excellence was that he did whatever he did solely to seek the Face of his Lord Most High, seeking no recompense from any created being—neither angels, nor prophets, nor anyone else.

Asking to be prayed for is also a form of seeking recompense. Allah Most High said of those whom He praised:

“We feed you only to seek the Face of Allah; we desire from you neither recompense nor thanks.”

Supplication is a form of recompense, as stated in the hadith:

“Whoever does you a kindness, repay him. If you cannot find anything with which to repay him, then pray for him until you know that you have repaid him.”

Whenever ‘Ā’ishah sent charity to some people, she would tell the messenger: “Listen to the prayers they offer for us, so that we may offer the same prayers for them, and our reward may remain with Allah.”

One of the early generations said: “If a beggar says to you, ‘May Allah bless you,’ then say, ‘And may Allah bless you.’”

Whoever does good to created beings—whether the recipient is a prophet, a righteous man, a king, or a wealthy person—is commanded to do so sincerely for Allah, seeking His Face, without seeking any recompense, supplication, or anything else from a created being, whether a prophet, a righteous man, or an angel. Allah has commanded all His servants to worship Him, devoting their religion sincerely to Him. This is the religion of Islam with which Allah sent the earliest and the last of His messengers

---

## Page 191

and so He accepts no other religion from anyone. Allah Most High said:

“Whoever seeks a religion other than Islam, it will never be accepted from him, and in the Hereafter he will be among the losers.”

Noah, Abraham, Moses, the Messiah, and all the followers of the prophets—peace be upon them—followed Islam. Noah said:

“And I have been commanded to be among the Muslims.”

Allah said concerning Abraham:

“Who would turn away from the religion of Abraham except one who makes a fool of himself? We certainly chose him in this world, and in the Hereafter he will surely be among the righteous.”

“When his Lord said to him, ‘Submit,’ he said, ‘I have submitted to the Lord of all worlds.’”

“Abraham enjoined this upon his sons, as did Jacob: ‘My sons, Allah has chosen this religion for you, so do not die except as Muslims.’”

“Moses said, ‘My people, if you have believed in Allah, then place your trust in Him, if you are Muslims.’”

The magicians said:

“Our Lord, pour patience upon us and cause us to die as Muslims.”

Joseph said:

“Cause me to die as a Muslim and join me with the righteous.”

Allah Most High said:

“Indeed, We sent down the Torah, containing guidance and light. The prophets who submitted to Allah judged by it for the Jews.”

He said concerning the disciples:

“And when I inspired the disciples to believe in Me and My messenger, they said, ‘We believe, so bear witness that we are Muslims.’”

The religion of Islam rests upon two foundations: that we worship Allah alone, without any partner, and that we worship Him according to the religion He has prescribed. This consists of what the messengers were commanded to observe, whether as an obligation or a recommendation. Thus, in every age, He is worshipped according to what He commanded in that age.

When the law of the Torah was authoritative, those who acted according to it were Muslims; the same applied to the law of the Gospel. Likewise, in the early days of Islam, when the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, prayed facing Jerusalem, his prayer in that direction was part of Islam. When he was commanded to face the Kaaba, praying toward it was part of Islam, whereas turning away from it toward the Rock was a departure from the religion of

---

## Page 192

Islam. Thus, anyone who, after Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was sent, does not worship Allah according to what He has prescribed of obligatory and recommended acts is not a Muslim. All obligatory and recommended acts must be devoted sincerely to Allah, the Lord of all worlds, as He Most High said:

“Those who were given the Scripture did not become divided until after the clear evidence had come to them.”

“They were commanded only to worship Allah with sincere devotion to Him, upright in faith, to establish prayer, and to give zakat. That is the upright religion.”

He Most High also said:

“The revelation of the Book is from Allah, the Almighty, the All-Wise.”

“Indeed, We have sent down the Book to you in truth, so worship Allah with sincere devotion to Him.”

“Indeed, sincere devotion belongs to Allah alone.”

Everything a Muslim does among obligatory and recommended acts of devotion—such as believing in Allah and His Messenger, performing bodily and financial acts of worship, loving Allah and His Messenger, and showing kindness to Allah’s servants by helping them and giving them wealth—he is commanded to do sincerely for Allah, the Lord of all worlds, without seeking any recompense from a created being: neither a supplication nor anything else. These are acts for which it is not permissible to seek recompense, whether a supplication or anything else.

As for asking a created being for anything else, this is not obligatory, nor even recommended except in certain cases; and the person being asked is commanded to give before the request is made. If the believers are not commanded to ask created beings, then this applies all the more to the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, for his standing is far greater, and Allah makes him far less dependent on anyone else.

For asking created beings entails three harms: the harm of dependence upon other than Allah, which is a form of associating partners with Him; and the harm of causing distress to the person asked, which is a form of wronging others.

---

## Page 193

and it involves abasement before other than Allah, which is a form of wronging oneself. Thus, it encompasses all three kinds of wrongdoing, and Allah has kept His Messenger free of all of them.

When he commands the community to supplicate for him, this is an instruction to do what benefits them, just as he commands them to perform all other obligatory and recommended acts. Although he benefits from their supplications for him, he also benefits from the acts of worship and righteous deeds that he commands them to perform. It is authentically reported that he said:

“Whoever calls others to guidance will receive a reward equal to the rewards of those who follow him, without their rewards being diminished in any way.”

Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is the one who calls his community to the good deeds they perform. For whatever they do, he receives a reward equal to theirs, without their rewards being diminished in any way. This is why dedicating the reward of their deeds to him was not part of the worship of the early generations: he receives a reward equal to theirs without such dedication, and without their rewards being diminished in any way.

The same does not apply to parents, for a parent does not receive a reward equal to that of the child for everything the child does. Rather, the parent benefits from the child’s supplications and similar acts whose benefit reaches the father, as he said in the authentic hadith:

“When the son of Adam dies, his deeds come to an end except for three: ongoing charity, knowledge from which others benefit, and a righteous child who supplicates for him.”

Thus, when the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, asks his community to supplicate for him, he does so by way of instruction and encouragement, not as a personal appeal.

This includes his command that we invoke blessings and peace upon him. Allah has commanded this in the Quran, saying:

“Send blessings upon him and greet him with peace.”

The hadiths reported from him concerning invoking blessings and peace upon him are well known.

---

## Page 194

This also includes his command to ask Allah to grant him al-wasīlah (a rank in Paradise), eminence, and the Praiseworthy Station. It is established in Sahīh Muslim, on the authority of ‘Abd Allāh ibn ‘Amr, that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“When you hear the caller to prayer, repeat what he says, then invoke blessings upon me, for whoever invokes blessings upon me once, Allah will bless him ten times. Then ask Allah to grant me al-wasīlah, for it is a rank in Paradise reserved for only one of Allah’s servants, and I hope to be that servant. Whoever asks Allah to grant me al-wasīlah will be entitled to my intercession on the Day of Resurrection.”

In Sahīh al-Bukhārī, it is reported on the authority of Jābir that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever says upon hearing the call to prayer, ‘O Allah, Lord of this perfect call and the prayer to be established, grant Muhammad al-wasīlah, eminence, and the lofty rank, and raise him to the Praiseworthy Station which You have promised him. Indeed, You never fail in Your promise,’ will be entitled to my intercession on the Day of Resurrection.”

Thus, he encouraged Muslims to ask Allah to grant him al-wasīlah and explained that whoever asks for it on his behalf will be entitled to his intercession on the Day of Resurrection. Likewise, whoever invokes blessings upon him once, Allah will bless him ten times, for the reward corresponds to the deed.

Another example is the hadith narrated by Ahmad, Abū Dāwūd, al-Tirmidhī—who graded it authentic—and Ibn Mājah:

“‘Umar ibn al-Khattāb asked the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, for permission to perform ‘umrah (the lesser pilgrimage). He gave him permission, then said, ‘My brother, do not forget us in your supplications.’”

The Prophet’s request that ‘Umar supplicate for him is like his request that he invoke blessings and peace upon him and ask Allah to grant him al-wasīlah and the lofty rank. It is also like his request that he perform all other righteous deeds: his purpose is to benefit the person he addresses and to show him kindness. He himself, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, also benefits from teaching them what is good and commanding them to do it, as well as from the righteous deeds they perform and their supplications for him.

---

## Page 195

Another example is the statement of a man who said:

“I frequently invoke blessings upon you. How much of my supplication should I devote to invoking blessings upon you?”
He replied, “As much as you wish.”
The man asked, “A quarter?”
He replied, “As much as you wish, but if you increase it, that is better for you.”
The man asked, “Half?”
He replied, “As much as you wish, but if you increase it, that is better for you.”
The man asked, “Two-thirds?”
He replied, “As much as you wish, but if you increase it, that is better for you.”
The man said, “I shall devote all my supplication to invoking blessings upon you.”
He replied, “Then your concerns will be taken care of, and your sins will be forgiven.”

This was narrated by Ahmad in his Musnad, al-Tirmidhī, and others. It has been discussed at length in Answers to the Baghdad Questions. This man had a supplication that he would regularly make. If he replaced it with invoking blessings upon the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, Allah would take care of whatever concerned him in this world and the Hereafter. For whenever he invokes blessings upon him once, Allah blesses him ten times. If he were to supplicate for individual believers, the angels would say, “Amen, and may you receive the same.” His supplication for the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is all the more deserving of that response.

Whoever says to another person, “Supplicate for me—or for us,” intending that the person asked should benefit from supplicating, that he himself should also benefit from asking him to do so, and that the person should carry out what he has been asked to do—just as he would ask him to perform any other good deed—is following the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and taking him as his example. This is not a less preferable form of asking.

But if his sole intention is to have his own need met, without intending to benefit the other person or show him kindness, then he is not following the Messenger or taking him as his example in this matter. Rather, this is a less preferable form of asking: it is better to leave it aside and turn to Allah and His Messenger than to turn to a created being and ask him.

All of this concerns permissible, religiously sanctioned requests made of the living.

---

## Page 196

As for asking the dead, this is not religiously sanctioned: it is neither obligatory nor recommended, nor even permissible. None of the Companions or those who followed them in righteousness ever did this, nor did any of the early generations of the Muslim community recommend it. This is because it entails harm that outweighs any benefit, with no benefit that outweighs the harm. The Sharia prescribes only what brings unalloyed benefit or benefit that outweighs harm. This practice offers no such outweighing benefit; rather, it entails either pure harm or harm that outweighs its benefit, and neither is religiously sanctioned.

It has thus become clear that the Prophet’s asking others to supplicate, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, falls under showing kindness to people, which is either obligatory or recommended. Likewise, his commands to offer funeral prayers, visit the graves of believers, greet them, and supplicate for them fall under showing kindness to the dead, which is either obligatory or recommended.

Allah Most High commanded Muslims to perform prayer and give zakāh. Prayer is the right owed to the True One in this world and the Hereafter, while zakāh is the right owed to created beings. The Messenger commanded people to fulfil the rights of Allah and the rights of His servants by worshipping Allah without associating anything with Him. Worshipping Him includes showing kindness to people wherever Allah, glorified be He, has commanded it, as in offering funeral prayers and visiting the graves of believers.

But Satan gained mastery over his followers and made their purpose in these acts the association of partners with the Creator and the harming of created beings. For if their sole purpose in visiting the graves of prophets and righteous people is to make requests of them or to make requests at their graves, or if they do not intend to greet them or supplicate for them, as is intended in funeral prayers, then by doing so they are associating partners with Allah, causing harm, and wronging those whom they ask. They are also wronging themselves, thus combining all three kinds of wrongdoing.

---

## Page 197

Thus, what Allah and His Messenger have prescribed entails affirming Allah’s oneness, justice, kindness, sincerity, and the well-being of His servants in this life and the Hereafter. By contrast, innovative acts of worship that Allah and His Messenger have not prescribed entail associating partners with Allah, wrongdoing, harm, and the corruption of His servants in this life and the Hereafter.

Allah Most High commanded the believers to worship Him and show kindness to His servants, as He said:

“Worship Allah and associate nothing with Him, and be good to parents and relatives.”

This is a command to cultivate noble character. He, glorified be He, loves noble character and dislikes base character.

It has been reported that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“I was sent only to perfect noble character.”

Al-Hākim narrated this in his Sahīh. It is also established in the Sahīh that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“The upper hand is better than the lower hand.”

He also said:

“The upper hand is the giving hand, and the lower hand is the begging hand.”

This too is established from him in the Sahīh.

What comparison is there between showing kindness to Allah’s servants and troubling them with requests and begging? What comparison is there between affirming the Creator’s oneness—by turning to Him with longing, placing one’s hope in Him, relying upon Him, and loving Him—and associating partners with Him by turning to a created being with longing, placing one’s hope in that being, relying upon it, and loving it as one loves Allah? And what comparison is there between a servant’s well-being through servitude to Allah, humbling himself before Him, and being in need of Him, and his corruption through servitude to a created being, humbling himself before it, and being in need of it?

The Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, therefore commanded those three virtuous, praiseworthy kinds of conduct that set right the affairs of those who practise them in this world and the Hereafter. He forbade the three kinds that corrupt the affairs of those who practise them.

---

## Page 198

But Satan commands the opposite of what the Messenger commands. Allah Most High said:

“Did I not command you, O children of Adam, not to worship Satan? Indeed, he is a clear enemy to you.”

“And to worship Me? This is a straight path.”

“He has already led great multitudes of you astray. Did you not use your reason?”

Allah Most High also said:

“Indeed, you have no authority over My servants, except those who follow you among the misguided.”

And He said:

“When you recite the Quran, seek refuge in Allah from Satan, the accursed.”

“Indeed, he has no authority over those who believe and put their trust in their Lord.”

“His authority is only over those who take him as an ally and those who associate partners with Allah.”

And He said:

“Whoever turns away from the Reminder of the Most Merciful, We appoint for him a devil who becomes his companion.”

“The devils surely turn them away from the path, while they think they are rightly guided.”

The Reminder of the Most Merciful is the Reminder that Allah revealed to His Messenger, concerning which He said:

“Indeed, it is We who sent down the Reminder, and We will surely preserve it.”

Allah Most High also said:

“When guidance comes to you from Me, whoever follows My guidance will neither go astray nor suffer misery.”

“But whoever turns away from My Reminder will have a life of hardship, and We will raise him blind on the Day of Resurrection.”

“He will say, ‘My Lord, why have You raised me blind when I used to see?’”

“He will say, ‘Thus it is: Our signs came to you, but you forgot them; and so today you will be forgotten.’”

Allah Most High also said:

“Alif Lām Mīm Sād.”

“This is a Book revealed to you—so let there be no distress in your heart because of it—that you may warn through it, and as a reminder to the believers.”

“Follow what has been revealed to you from your Lord, and do not follow other protectors besides Him. How little you take heed!”

Allah Most High also said:

“This is a Book We have revealed to you so that you may bring people out of darkness into light, by their Lord’s permission, to the path of the Almighty, the Praiseworthy.”

“Allah, to whom belongs whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on earth. And woe to the disbelievers from a severe punishment!”

And Allah Most High said:

“And thus We have revealed to you a spirit by Our command. You did not know what the Book was, nor what faith was, but We made it a light by which We guide

---

## Page 199

whomever We will of Our servants. And indeed, you guide to a straight path.”

“The path of Allah, to whom belongs whatever is in the heavens and whatever is on earth. Surely, to Allah all matters return.”

The straight path is what Allah sent His Messenger Muhammad, peace and blessings be upon him, to teach: doing what he commanded, avoiding what he forbade, and believing what he reported. There is no path to Allah except this. It is the way of Allah’s God-fearing allies, His successful party, and His victorious army. Everything contrary to this belongs to the paths of those given to error and misguidance. Allah Most High declared His Prophet free of both, saying:

“By the star when it descends.”

“Your companion has neither strayed nor erred.”

“Nor does he speak from desire.”

“It is nothing but revelation conveyed to him.”

Allah, glorified be He, has commanded us to say in our prayers:

“Guide us to the straight path.”

“The path of those You have blessed, not of those who have incurred Your wrath, nor of those who have gone astray.”

Al-Tirmidhī and others narrated from Adī ibn Hātim that the Prophet, peace and blessings be upon him, said:

“The Jews have incurred wrath, and the Christians have gone astray.”

Al-Tirmidhī said: “This is an authentic hadith.”

Sufyān ibn Uyaynah said:

“They used to say, ‘Whoever among our scholars becomes corrupt bears a resemblance to the Jews, and whoever among our worshippers becomes corrupt bears a resemblance to the Christians.’”

Several of the early Muslims used to say:

“Beware of the trial posed by the wicked scholar and the ignorant worshipper, for they are a trial to all who are put to the test.”

Whoever knows the truth but does not act upon it resembles the Jews, concerning whom Allah said:

“Do you command people to be righteous and forget yourselves, while you recite the Scripture? Will you not then use your reason?”

---

## Page 200

And whoever worships Allah without knowledge, going instead to extremes and associating partners with Him, resembles the Christians, concerning whom Allah said:

“O People of the Scripture, do not go to extremes in your religion beyond the truth, and do not follow the desires of a people who went astray before, led many astray, and strayed from the right way.”

The first belongs to those who err, and the second to those who go astray. For error consists in following one’s desires, while misguidance is the absence of guidance. Allah Most High said:

“And recite to them the account of the one to whom We gave Our signs, but who cast them off. Satan then pursued him, and he became one of those who erred.”

“And had We willed, We could have raised him through them, but he clung to the earth and followed his own desires. His likeness is that of a dog: if you chase it, it pants; and if you leave it alone, it pants. Such is the likeness of the people who deny Our signs. So relate these accounts, that they may reflect.”

Allah Most High also said:

“I will turn away from My signs those who are unjustly arrogant on earth. Even if they see every sign, they will not believe in it. If they see the way of sound guidance, they will not take it as their way; but if they see the way of error, they will take it as their way. That is because they denied Our signs and were heedless of them.”

Whoever combines misguidance and error bears a resemblance to both groups. We ask Allah to guide us and all our brothers to the path of those He has blessed—the prophets, those steadfast in truth, the martyrs, and the righteous. What excellent companions they are!

---

## Page 201

Section

With this understood, it is now clear that the terms “wasīlah” (a means of drawing near) and “tawassul” (seeking a means of drawing near) involve ambiguity and uncertainty. Their meanings must be understood, and each must be given its due. One must understand what the Quran and Sunnah say concerning these terms and what they mean, as well as what the Companions said and did in this regard and what that meant. One must also recognize the usages and meanings introduced by those who brought about innovations. Much of people’s confusion in this matter stems from insufficiently defined expressions and the use of the same words for different meanings, to the point that most of them cannot give a clear, decisive account of the matter.

The term wasīlah occurs in the Quran in the words of Allah Most High:

“O you who believe, fear Allah and seek a means of drawing near to Him.”

And in His words:

“Say: Call upon those whom you claim to be gods besides Him. They have no power to remove harm from you or to transfer it elsewhere.”

“Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of drawing near to their Lord, striving to be closest to Him. They hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Indeed, your Lord’s punishment is to be feared.”

The means of drawing near that Allah has commanded people to seek, and which He has stated that His angels and prophets seek, consists of the obligatory and recommended deeds through which one draws near to Him. Thus, this means of drawing near, which

---

## Page 202

Allah has commanded the believers to seek encompasses every obligatory and recommended deed. Whatever is neither obligatory nor recommended is not included, whether it is forbidden, disliked, or permissible. Obligatory and recommended deeds are those prescribed by the Messenger, who commanded them either as obligations or as recommendations. Their foundation is faith in what the Messenger brought. Thus, the essence of the means of drawing near that Allah has commanded His creation to seek is to draw near to Him by following what the Messenger brought. No one has any means of drawing near to Allah other than this.

Second is the term “wasīlah” as it occurs in authentic hadiths, such as the words of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“Ask Allah to grant me al-wasīlah, for it is a rank in Paradise reserved for only one of Allah’s servants, and I hope to be that servant. Whoever asks Allah to grant me al-wasīlah will be entitled to my intercession on the Day of Resurrection.”

And his words:

“Whoever says upon hearing the call to prayer, ‘O Allah, Lord of this perfect call and the prayer to be established, grant Muhammad al-wasīlah and eminence, and raise him to the praised station You have promised him. Indeed, You never break Your promise,’ will be entitled to intercession.”

This wasīlah is exclusively for the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. We have been commanded to ask Allah to grant him this wasīlah. He stated that it can belong to only one of Allah’s servants, and he hopes to be that servant. We have been commanded to ask for this wasīlah for the Messenger, and he stated that whoever asks for it for him will be entitled to intercession on the Day of Resurrection. This is because the reward corresponds to the deed: since they supplicated for the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, they became deserving of his supplication for them. Intercession is a form of supplication, just as he said that whoever invokes blessings upon him once, Allah will send blessings upon that person ten times in return.

---

## Page 203

As for seeking nearness to Allah through the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and turning to Allah through him, the Companions used these expressions to mean seeking nearness through his supplication and intercession. In the usage of many later scholars, however, seeking nearness through him means adjuring Allah by him and petitioning Allah by him, just as they adjure Allah by other prophets, righteous people, and those they believe to be righteous.

Thus, the expression “seeking nearness through him” has two meanings that are valid by the agreement of the Muslims, and a third meaning for which no precedent has been transmitted in the Sunnah. As for the first two meanings, which are valid by the agreement of the scholars:

- The first is the very foundation of faith and Islam: seeking nearness to Allah through faith in the Prophet and obedience to him.
- The second is through his supplication and intercession, as explained above.

Both are permissible by the consensus of the Muslims. An example of this is the statement of ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb:

“O Allah, whenever we suffered drought, we would seek nearness to You through our Prophet, and You would send us rain. Now we seek nearness to You through our Prophet’s uncle, so send us rain.”

That is, through his supplication and intercession. Likewise, Allah Most High says:

“Seek the means of drawing near to Him.”

This means drawing near to Him through obedience to Him; and obedience to His Messenger is obedience to Him. Allah Most High says:

“Whoever obeys the Messenger has indeed obeyed Allah.”

This first form of seeking nearness is the foundation of the religion, and no Muslim denies it. As for seeking nearness through his supplication and intercession—as ʿUmar said—he sought nearness through the Prophet’s supplication, not through his person. This is why they turned from seeking nearness through him to seeking nearness through his uncle al-ʿAbbās. Had this meant seeking nearness through his person, doing so would have been preferable to seeking nearness through al-ʿAbbās. So when they turned from seeking nearness through him to seeking nearness

---

## Page 204

through al-'Abbās, it became clear that what had been done during his lifetime was no longer possible after his death. This differs from seeking nearness through faith in him and obedience to him, which remains prescribed at all times.

Thus, the expression “seeking nearness” is used in three senses:

- First, seeking nearness through obedience to him. This is obligatory, and faith cannot be complete without it.
- Second, seeking nearness through his supplication and intercession. This occurred during his lifetime, and on the Day of Resurrection people will seek nearness through his intercession.
- Third, seeking nearness through him in the sense of adjuring Allah by his person and petitioning Allah by his person. This is what the Companions did not practise when seeking rain or in similar circumstances, either during his lifetime or after his death, whether at his grave or elsewhere. Nor is this known in any of the supplications commonly used among them. Something of this kind is transmitted only in weak reports traced back to the Prophet or attributed to Companions, or from someone whose statements do not constitute authoritative proof, as we shall explain, if Allah Most High wills.

This is what Abū Hanīfa and his disciples declared impermissible and prohibited, saying: “Allah is not to be petitioned by any created being, nor should anyone say: ‘I ask You by the right of Your prophets.’”

Abū al-Husayn al-Qudūrī said in his extensive work on jurisprudence entitled Commentary on al-Karkhī, in the chapter on disapproved acts:

This has also been mentioned by several of Abū Hanīfa’s disciples.

Bishr ibn al-Walīd said: Abū Yūsuf related to us that Abū Hanīfa said:

No one should supplicate Allah except by Him. I disapprove of saying “By the seats of might in Your Throne” or “By the right of

---

## Page 205

Your creation”.

This is also the position of Abū Yūsuf. Abū Yūsuf said:

“The expression ‘By the seat of might in His Throne’ refers to Allah, so I do not disapprove of it. But I disapprove of saying, ‘By the right of so-and-so,’ or ‘By the right of Your prophets and messengers,’ or ‘By the right of the Sacred House and the Sacred Monument.’”

Al-Qudūrī said:

“Petitioning Him by His creation is impermissible, because created beings have no right over the Creator. It is therefore impermissible by agreement.”

This statement of Abū Hanīfa and his disciples—that Allah is not to be petitioned by any created being—has two meanings.

The first accords with the position of all the other imams who prohibit swearing by a created being. For if adjuring a created being by another created being is prohibited, then prohibiting the adjuration of the Creator by a created being is all the more warranted and appropriate. This differs from His own swearing—glory be to Him—by His creation, as in:

“By the night when it covers, and by the day when it appears”; 
“By the sun and its brightness”; 
“By those who extract with violence”; 
“By those lined up in rows.”

His swearing by His creation includes mention of His signs, which attest to His power, wisdom, and oneness, and thus makes such oaths fitting. Created beings are different: their swearing by created things constitutes associating partners with their Creator. Thus, it is reported in the Sunan collections that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever swears by anything other than Allah has associated partners with Him.”

Al-Tirmidhī and others graded this report authentic. Another wording states:

“Has committed disbelief.”

Al-Hākim graded this authentic. It is also authentically reported from him in the two Sahīh collections that he said:

“Whoever wishes to swear an oath should swear by Allah or remain silent.”

He also said:

“Do not swear by your fathers, for Allah forbids you to swear by your fathers.”

The two Sahīh collections also report that he said:

“Whoever swears by al-Lāt and al-‘Uzzā should say: ‘There is no god but Allah.’”

---

## Page 206

Muslims agree that whoever swears by revered created things, or by anything he considers sacred—such as the Throne, the Footstool, the Kaaba, the Sacred Mosque, al-Aqsā Mosque, the Mosque of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, the angels, the righteous, kings, the swords of those fighting in Allah’s cause, the graves of prophets and righteous people, the customary oaths of pellet-shooters, the ceremonial trousers of the chivalric brotherhoods, or anything else of this kind—his oath is not legally binding, and no expiation is due for swearing by such things. Swearing by created things is forbidden according to the majority of scholars. This is the position of Abū Hanīfa and one of the two opinions in the schools of al-Shāfi‘ī and Ahmad. Consensus among the Companions on this matter has also been reported.

It has also been said that such oaths are merely discouraged, rather than forbidden. The first view, however, is more correct. Indeed, ‘Abd Allāh ibn Mas‘ūd, ‘Abd Allāh ibn ‘Abbās, and ‘Abd Allāh ibn ‘Umar said:

“I would rather swear falsely by Allah than swear truthfully by anything other than Allah.”

This is because swearing by anything other than Allah constitutes associating partners with Him, and associating partners with Him is more grievous than lying.

The only disagreement we know of concerns swearing by the prophets. Two positions are reported from Ahmad regarding swearing by the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. According to the first, such an oath is not legally binding, which accords with the position of the majority, including Mālik, Abū Hanīfa, and al-Shāfi‘ī. According to the second, it is legally binding. A group of scholars in Ahmad’s school adopted this position, including al-Qādī and his followers; Ibn al-Mundhir also agreed with them. Most of these scholars confined the disagreement to swearing specifically by the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, whereas Ibn ‘Aqīl extended this ruling to all the other prophets. The view that expiation is obligatory for swearing by a created being, even a prophet, is exceedingly weak and contrary to the foundational principles and scriptural texts.

---

## Page 207

Thus, invoking a created being in an oath addressed to Allah—and petitioning Him by that being when this amounts to an oath—belongs to the same category. As for petitioning by a created being when the preposition bi- indicates a cause rather than an oath—and there is a difference between the two:

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was commanded to honor oaths. It is established in the two Sahīh collections that he said:

“Among Allah’s servants are those who, if they swore an oath calling upon Allah, He would fulfill it for them.”

He said this when Anas ibn al-Nadr asked, “Will you break al-Rubayyi‘’s front tooth?” He declared, “No, by Him who sent you with the truth, you shall not break her tooth!” The Prophet replied, “O Anas, Allah’s Book prescribes equal retribution.” The people were then satisfied and pardoned her, whereupon the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said, “Among Allah’s servants are those who, if they swore an oath calling upon Allah, He would fulfill it for them.”

He also said:

“Many a disheveled, dust-covered person, turned away from people’s doors, would have his oath fulfilled by Allah if he swore an oath calling upon Him.”

Muslim and others narrated this. He also said:

“Shall I tell you who the people of Paradise are? Every weak and lowly person who, if he swore an oath calling upon Allah, would have it fulfilled by Him. Shall I tell you who the people of Hell are? Every harsh, overbearing, arrogant person.”

This is in the two Sahīh collections, as is the hadith of Anas ibn al-Nadr; the other is narrated by Muslim alone of the two. It has also been reported that, when saying, “Among Allah’s servants are those who, if they swore an oath calling upon Allah, He would fulfill it for them,” he added, “Among them is al-Barā’ ibn Mālik.”

Whenever fighting between the Muslims and the unbelievers grew fierce, they would say, “O Barā’, swear an oath calling upon your Lord!” He would do so, and the unbelievers would be routed.

When they were at a bridge in al-Sūs, they said, “O Barā’, swear an oath calling upon your Lord!” He said, “O Lord, I adjure You to grant us victory over them and make me the first martyr.” Allah fulfilled his oath: the enemy was routed, and al-Barā’ ibn Mālik was martyred that day.

He was the brother of Anas ibn Mālik. He killed a hundred men in single combat, apart from those in whose killing he shared. On the day of the battle against Musaylima, he was lifted upon a shield and thrown into the garden, where he opened the gate.

---

## Page 208

Swearing by Him to adjure another person means swearing that the other person shall certainly do such-and-such. If that person causes the oath to be broken by failing to fulfill it, then, according to the great majority of jurists, the expiation is due from the person who swore, not the person adjured. For example, if someone swears that his slave, child, or friend shall do something, and that person does not do it, the expiation is due from the one whose oath has been broken.

As for saying:

“I ask you by Allah to do such-and-such,”

this is a request, not an oath. The hadith states:

“Give to whoever asks you by Allah.”

No expiation is due from the person making such a request if it is not granted. All created beings ask Allah, believers and unbelievers alike, and Allah may answer the supplications of unbelievers. Unbelievers ask Allah for provision, and He provides for them and gives them water. When distress strikes them at sea, all those they invoke vanish from them except Him. Yet when He delivers them safely to land, they turn away; man is ever ungrateful. Those who swear an oath calling upon Allah and have Him fulfill it, however, are particular individuals.

Examples of petitioning Allah include saying:

“I ask You by the fact that all praise belongs to You. You are Allah, the Bestower, the Originator of the heavens and the earth, O Possessor of Majesty and Honor.”

“I ask You by the fact that You are Allah, the One, the Eternal Refuge, who neither begets nor is born, and to whom none is equal.”

“I ask You by every name that belongs to You, by which You have named Yourself, which You have revealed in Your Book, which You have taught to any of Your creation, or which You have kept to Yourself in the knowledge of the unseen that is with You.”

This is petitioning Allah Most High by His names and attributes, not swearing an oath calling upon Him. His acts are entailed by His names and attributes: His forgiveness and mercy are entailed by His names, the All-Forgiving and the Most Merciful, and His pardon by His name, the Pardoner. Hence, when “‘Ā’ishah said to the Prophet

---

## Page 209

—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—‘If I encounter the Night of Decree, what should I say?’ he replied, ‘Say: “O Allah, You are the Pardoner and You love to pardon, so pardon me.”’”

His guidance and direction are entailed by His name, the Guide. In the report transmitted from Ahmad ibn Hanbal, he instructed a man to say:

“O Guide of the bewildered, guide me to the path of the truthful and make me one of Your righteous servants.”

All the good that Allah does for His servant is entailed by His name, the Lord. Hence, one says in supplication, “O Lord, O Lord,” as Adam said:

“Our Lord, we have wronged ourselves, and if You do not forgive us and have mercy upon us, we will surely be among the losers.”

Noah said:

“My Lord, I seek refuge in You from asking You for that of which I have no knowledge. Unless You forgive me and have mercy upon me, I will be among the losers.”

Abraham said:

“Our Lord, I have settled some of my descendants in a valley without cultivation.”

The other prophets likewise addressed Him in this way.

Mālik, Ibn Abī ‘Imrān—one of Abū Hanīfah’s followers—and others disliked a supplicant saying, “O my Master, O my Master.” They said: “Say as the prophets said: ‘My Lord, my Lord.’”

His name, “the Ever-Living, the Sustainer of all,” encompasses the fundamental meanings of the divine names and attributes, as has been explained at length elsewhere. Hence, the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—would invoke this name when supplicating earnestly.

Thus, when someone is petitioned by invoking something—with the preposition bi (“by”) indicating causation—the petition invokes a cause that entails the realization of what is requested. When someone says:

“I ask You by the fact that all praise belongs to You. You are Allah, the Bestower, the Originator of the heavens and the earth,”

His being worthy of praise, the Bestower, and the Originator of the heavens and the earth entails His bestowing favor upon His supplicating servant.

---

## Page 210

and His being worthy of praise entails that He do what merits praise, while the servant’s praise of Him is a cause of his supplication being answered. Hence, the worshipper is instructed to say:

“Allah hears whoever praises Him.”

That is, Allah answers the supplication of whoever praises Him. Hearing here means answering and accepting, as in the Prophet’s words—may Allah bless him and grant him peace:

“I seek refuge in You from knowledge that does not benefit, a heart that is not humble, a soul that is never satisfied, and a supplication that is not heard.”

That is, a supplication that is not answered. Another example is what Abraham, Allah’s intimate friend, said at the end of his supplication:

“Indeed, my Lord is the Hearer of supplication.”

Likewise, Allah Most High says:

“And among you are those who listen eagerly to them.”

And He says:

“And among the Jews are those who listen eagerly to falsehood, who listen eagerly to other people who have not come to you.”

That is, they accept falsehood and accept what they hear from other people who have not come to you. Hence, the worshipper is instructed to supplicate after praising Allah, following the tashahhud (testimony of faith), which includes praise of Allah, glorified is He.

The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—saw a man praying and supplicating without having praised his Lord or invoked blessings upon His Prophet. He said, “This man has been hasty.” He then called him and said:

“When one of you prays, let him begin by praising and extolling Allah, then invoke blessings upon the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—and then supplicate for whatever he wishes.”

This was recorded by Abū Dāwūd and al-Tirmidhī, who graded it authentic.

‘Abdullāh ibn Mas‘ūd said:

“I was praying while the Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—was present, with Abū Bakr and ‘Umar beside him. When I sat down, I began by extolling Allah, then invoked blessings upon His Prophet, then supplicated for myself. The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—said, ‘Ask, and you will be granted it.’”

This was narrated by al-Tirmidhī, who graded it good.

Thus, the word “hearing” may mean perceiving sound, or understanding its meaning as well

---

## Page 211

, or accepting and responding, together with understanding.

Allah Most High says:

“Had Allah known any good in them, He would have made them hear.”

Then He says, “And if He had made them hear”—while they remained in their present condition, they would not have accepted the truth—then: “They would have turned away, refusing.” Thus, He condemns them for not understanding the Quran and for being unwilling to act upon it even if they understood it.

When someone says to another, “I ask you by Allah,” he is appealing to that person’s faith in Allah. This is a reason to grant the request of someone who makes such an appeal, for Allah, glorified is He, loves kindness towards His creation. This is especially so when the request is to refrain from wrongdoing, for He commands justice and forbids wrongdoing. His command is the greatest incentive to act: no cause has a stronger claim to bring about its effect than the command of Allah Most High.

A hadith narrated by Ahmad in his Musnad and by Ibn Mājah, from ‘Atiyyah al-‘Awfī, from Abū Sa‘īd al-Khudrī, states:

“The Prophet—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—taught anyone going out to prayer to say in his supplication: ‘And I ask You by the right that those who ask You have upon You, and by the right of this walk of mine. For I have not gone out in insolence or arrogance, nor to show off or seek a reputation. Rather, I have gone out to avoid Your wrath and seek Your pleasure.’”

If this is authentic, then the right that those who ask Him have upon Him is that He answer them, and the right that those who worship Him have is that He reward them. This is a right He has made binding upon Himself for their sake, just as He is asked by virtue of faith and righteous deeds, which He has made a means for supplication to be answered. As Allah Most High says:

“And He answers those who believe and do righteous deeds, and grants them more of His bounty.”

Likewise, He is asked by virtue of His promise, because His promise entails the fulfilment of what He has promised. An example is the believers’ words:

---

## Page 212

“Our Lord, we have heard a caller calling to faith, ‘Believe in your Lord,’ and we have believed. Our Lord, forgive us our sins, remove from us our misdeeds, and let us die among the righteous.”

And His words:

“Indeed, a group of My servants used to say, ‘Our Lord, we have believed, so forgive us and have mercy on us, for You are the best of those who show mercy.’”

“But you made them a target of mockery until they caused you to forget to remember Me.”

Similar to this is the Prophet’s earnest appeal—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—on the day of Badr, when he said:

“O Allah, fulfil for me what You have promised me.”

Likewise, the Torah relates that Allah Most High became angry with the Children of Israel, whereupon Moses began beseeching his Lord and recalling what He had promised Abraham. Thus, he appealed to Him by virtue of His earlier promise to Abraham.

An example of asking by virtue of righteous deeds is the supplication of the three men who took shelter in a cave. Each of them appealed by virtue of a great deed he had performed sincerely for Allah, because such a deed is among those Allah loves and is pleased with—a love that entails answering the one who performed it. One appealed by virtue of his dutifulness towards his parents, another by virtue of his complete chastity, and the third by virtue of his trustworthiness and kindness.

Similarly, Ibn Mas‘ūd used to say in the hours before dawn:

“O Allah, You commanded me and I obeyed You; You called me and I answered You. Now it is the hour before dawn, so forgive me.”

Another example is the report that Ibn ‘Umar used to say on al-Safā:

“O Allah, You have said—and Your word is true—‘Call upon Me; I will answer you,’ and You never break Your promise.”

The narrator then mentioned the well-known supplication that Ibn ‘Umar used to recite on al-Safā. Thus, it has become clear that a person’s words, “I ask You by such-and-such,” fall into two categories, for the preposition bi (“by”) may be used

---

## Page 213

to express an oath or to indicate a cause. Thus, it may mean adjuring Allah by that thing, or asking Him by virtue of it.

As for the first, adjuring a created being by created things is impermissible; how, then, could one adjure the Creator by them?

As for the second—asking by virtue of someone held in honour, such as asking by virtue of the prophets’ rights—there is disagreement concerning it. It has already been related that Abū Hanīfa and his companions considered this impermissible, while others permit it. We therefore say:

When someone asks Allah Most High, saying,

“I ask You by virtue of the right of so-and-so among the angels, prophets, righteous people or others, or by virtue of so-and-so’s standing or honour,”

this implies that these individuals have standing before Allah, and this is true.

They indeed possess a station, standing and honour before Allah that entail His raising their ranks, magnifying their worth and accepting their intercession if He grants it, although He, glorified be He, has said:

“Who is it that can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

It also entails that whoever follows them and takes them as examples in matters in which following their example is prescribed will attain happiness, as will whoever obeys the commands they conveyed from Allah. But their rank and standing alone do not entail that a person’s supplication will be answered if he asks Allah by virtue of them, such that he should ask Allah on that basis. Rather, their standing benefits him when he follows them and obeys them in what they were commanded to convey from Allah, or follows their example in the practices they prescribed for the believers. It also benefits him if they supplicate for him and their intercession on his behalf is accepted.

But if they neither supplicate nor intercede for him, and he himself has provided no cause that would entail an answer, then he is not seeking intercession through their standing, nor does his asking by virtue of their standing benefit him before Allah. Rather, he will have asked

---

## Page 214

by virtue of something unrelated to him that provides no grounds for benefiting him.

If a man were to say to a person of great authority:

“I ask you by virtue of so-and-so’s obedience to you, your love for him on account of that obedience, and the standing he enjoys before you because of his obedience to you,”

he would be asking by virtue of something unrelated to himself, with which he has no connection. Likewise, Allah’s kindness towards those drawn near to Him, His love for them and His elevation of their standing, together with their worship of Him and obedience to Him, provide no grounds for answering the supplication of someone who asks by virtue of them. Rather, grounds for answering his supplication arise either from something on his part—his obedience to them—or from something on their part—their intercession for him. If neither is present, there are no such grounds.

Indeed, if he were to ask Allah by virtue of his faith in Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, his love for him, his obedience to him and his following him, he would be asking by virtue of a powerful cause for his supplication to be answered. Indeed, this is the greatest of all causes and means. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, explained that his intercession in the Hereafter benefits those who affirm Allah’s oneness, not those who associate partners with Him, and that those who supplicate for him to be granted al-wasīla (the station in Paradise) are entitled to it. As related in an authentic hadith collection, he said:

“When you hear the caller to prayer, repeat what he says, then invoke blessings upon me. Whoever invokes blessings upon me once, Allah will bless him ten times. Then ask Allah to grant me al-wasīla, for it is a rank in Paradise befitting only one of Allah’s servants, and I hope to be that servant. Whoever asks Allah to grant me al-wasīla will be entitled to my intercession on the Day of Resurrection.”

It is also related in an authentic hadith collection:

“Abū Hurayra said to him, ‘Who will be most blessed by your intercession on the Day of Resurrection?’ He replied, ‘Whoever says, “There is no god but Allah,” sincerely from his heart.’”

Thus, he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, explained that those most entitled to his intercession on the Day of Resurrection are those whose affirmation of Allah’s oneness and sincerity are greatest. For affirming Allah’s oneness is the sum and substance of religion. Allah does not forgive associating partners with Him, but He forgives anything less than that for whomever He wills. No one can intercede with Him, glorified be He, except by His permission. When Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is permitted to intercede, his Lord sets a limit for him, and he admits them into Paradise. This is according to

---

## Page 215

the affirmation of Allah’s oneness and the faith present in their hearts. He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, also stated that whoever asks Allah to grant him al-wasīla will be entitled to his intercession on the Day of Resurrection. He thus explained that his intercession is attained by following him in the affirmation of Allah’s oneness and the faith he brought, and by offering the supplication he prescribed for us to make on his behalf.

As for asking by virtue of someone’s right, this rests on two questions: first, what right that person has with Allah; and second, whether we may ask Allah by virtue of that right, as we ask by virtue of someone’s standing and sanctity.

Regarding the first, some people say that a creature has a right against the Creator which can be known through reason. They compare the creature to the Creator, as some of the Mu'tazila and others do.

Others say that a creature has no right against the Creator under any circumstances, but that what He will do is known on the basis of His promise and His declarations. This is the position of some followers of Jahm and al-Ash'arī, and others who claim adherence to the Sunnah.

Still others say that Allah has prescribed mercy for Himself and imposed upon Himself a right owed to His believing servants, just as He has forbidden injustice for Himself. No creature has imposed this upon Him, nor is He to be compared to His creatures. Rather, by virtue of His mercy, wisdom and justice, He has prescribed mercy for Himself and forbidden injustice for Himself. As He says in the authentic divine hadith:

“O My servants, I have forbidden injustice for Myself and have made it forbidden among you, so do not wrong one another.”

And He, Most High, says:

“Your Lord has prescribed mercy for Himself.”

And He, Most High, says:

“And it was incumbent upon Us to support the believers.”

In the two authentic hadith collections, Mu'ādh relates that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said: “O Mu'ādh, do you know what right Allah has over

---

## Page 216

His servants?” I replied, “Allah and His Messenger know best.” He said, “His right over them is that they worship Him and associate nothing with Him. O Mu'ādh, do you know what right His servants have over Allah if they do that?” I replied, “Allah and His Messenger know best.” He said, “Their right over Him is that He not punish them.”

According to this view, His prophets and righteous servants have a right over Him, glorified is He, which He has imposed upon Himself and declared. According to the second view, they are entitled to what He has declared will occur, even if there is no cause that necessitates it.

Thus, the statement that a creature has no right against the Creator by virtue of which He may be asked—as it is related that Allah, Most High, said to David, “What right do your forefathers have against Me?”—is correct if what is meant is that a creature has no right against Him established by drawing an analogy or comparison with His creatures, in the way that one creature owes a right to another. This is the sort of right that ignorant worshippers imagine they have against Allah, glorified is He, on account of their worship.

Minds steeped in ignorance imagine that, through worship and knowledge, a person acquires a right against Allah of the same kind that one creature acquires against another. This is like those who serve their kings and masters, bringing them benefit and protecting them from harm, and then demand compensation and reward for doing so. When one of them encounters coldness or disregard from his master, he says to him, “Did I not do such-and-such?” He holds over him the favours he has done for him; even if he does not say this aloud, he harbours it within himself.

To imagine anything of this kind in relation to Allah, Most High, stems from human ignorance and injustice. For this reason, He, glorified is He, has explained that the benefit of a person's deeds returns to that person, and that Allah has no need of His creation. As He, Most High, says:

“If you do good, you do so for yourselves; and if you do evil, you do so against yourselves.”

And He, Most High, says:

“Whoever does righteousness, it is for his own soul; and whoever does evil, it is against it. And your Lord is never unjust to His servants.”

And He, Most High, says:

“If you disbelieve

---

## Page 217

then Allah has no need of you. He does not approve of disbelief for His servants, but if you are grateful, He approves of it for you.”

And He, Most High, says:

“Whoever is grateful is grateful only for his own benefit; and whoever is ungrateful—surely my Lord is Self-Sufficient, Generous.”

And He, Most High, says in the account of Moses, peace be upon him:

“If you are grateful, I will surely give you more; but if you are ungrateful, My punishment is indeed severe.”

“And Moses said, ‘If you disbelieve—you and everyone on earth together—Allah is indeed Self-Sufficient, Praiseworthy.’”

And He, Most High, says:

“Do not let those who rush into disbelief grieve you. They will not harm Allah in the least.”

And He, Most High, says:

“Pilgrimage to the House is a duty people owe to Allah—those who are able to make their way to it. And whoever disbelieves—Allah has no need of the worlds.”

He, glorified is He, has explained that He bestows His favour by enabling good deeds. He, Most High, says:

“They regard their acceptance of Islam as a favour to you. Say, ‘Do not regard your acceptance of Islam as a favour to me. Rather, Allah has bestowed His favour upon you by guiding you to faith, if you are truthful.’”

And He, Most High, says:

“Know that the Messenger of Allah is among you. If he were to obey you in many matters, you would fall into hardship. But Allah has endeared faith to you and made it beautiful in your hearts, and has made disbelief, wickedness, and disobedience hateful to you. Those are the rightly guided.”

“As a bounty and blessing from Allah. And Allah is All-Knowing, All-Wise.”

And in the authentic hadith conveying Allah’s words:

“O My servants, you can never harm Me, nor can you ever benefit Me. O My servants, you sin by night and by day, and I forgive all sins and do not mind doing so. So seek My forgiveness, and I will forgive you. O My servants, if the first and last of you, the humans and jinn among you, were all as wicked at heart as the most wicked man among you, that would not diminish My dominion in the least. O My servants, if the first and last of you, the humans and jinn among you, were all as pious at heart as the most pious man among you, that would not increase My dominion in the least. O My servants, if the first and last of you, the humans and jinn among you, were to stand together on one plain and ask of Me, and I gave each person among them

---

## Page 218

what he asked for, that would diminish what I possess no more than a needle diminishes the sea when dipped into it.”

There are differences between the Creator, Most High, and created beings that are evident to anyone with even the slightest insight.

One is that the Lord, Most High, is inherently independent of all besides Him, and it is impossible for Him to need anyone else in any respect. Kings and slave owners, however, necessarily depend on others.

Another is that although the Lord, Most High, loves and approves of righteous deeds and rejoices at the repentance of those who repent, He Himself creates and facilitates all this. Thus, what He loves and approves of comes about only through His power and will. This is clear according to the doctrine of Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamā‘ah, who acknowledge that Allah bestows faith upon His servants, unlike the Qadariyyah. A created being, however, may obtain what he loves through someone else’s action.

Another is that the Lord, Most High, commands His servants to do what benefits them and forbids them from doing what harms them. As Qatādah said:

“Allah did not command His servants to do what He commanded because He needed them, nor does He forbid them from doing what He forbade out of stinginess towards them. Rather, He commanded them to do what benefits them and forbade them from doing what harms them.”

This contrasts with a created being who commands another to do what he himself needs and forbids him from things out of stinginess towards him.

This, too, is clear according to the doctrine of the early generations and Ahl al-Sunnah, who affirm His wisdom and mercy and say:

“He commands His servants only to do good that benefits them, and forbids them only from evil that harms them.”

This is contrary to the Mujabbirah, who say:

“He may command them to do what harms them and forbid them from doing what benefits them.”

And (another difference is that He, glorified is He, bestows His favour by sending the messengers and revealing the scriptures. He also bestows ability, the senses, and the other means through which knowledge and righteous action are attained. He guides His servants, and there is no power or strength except through Him.

For this reason, the people of Paradise say:

“Praise be to Allah, who has guided us

---

## Page 219

to this; and we would never have been guided had Allah not guided us. The messengers of our Lord certainly came with the truth.” No created being is capable of any of this.

And (another difference is that His blessings upon His servants are too numerous to count. Even if worship were repayment for these blessings, it would not suffice to give thanks for even a few of them. How, then, could it do so when worship itself is also one of His blessings?

And (another difference is that His servants continually fall short and need His pardon and forgiveness. No one will enter Paradise by virtue of his deeds, and everyone has sins for which he needs Allah’s forgiveness:

“If Allah were to take people to task for what they have earned, He would not leave a single living creature on the earth.”

The Prophet’s statement, may Allah bless him and grant him peace,

“None of you will enter Paradise by virtue of his deeds,”

does not contradict the statement of the Most High:

“As a reward for what they used to do.”

The negation uses the preposition bā’ to denote recompense and exchange, as when one says, “I sold this for that.” The affirmation uses the same preposition to denote causation. Deeds are not equivalent to the reward, even though they are a cause of receiving it. Thus, anyone who thinks that he has fulfilled all his obligations and has no need of the Lord’s forgiveness and pardon is astray. As authentically reported, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“No one will enter Paradise by virtue of his deeds.” They asked, “Not even you, Messenger of Allah?” He replied, “Not even me, unless Allah envelops me in mercy and bounty from Him.” A variant reads, “in His forgiveness.”

Another example is the hadith recorded in the Sunan collections, in which the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“If Allah were to punish the inhabitants of His heavens and His earth, He would punish them without wronging them. And if He were to have mercy on them, His mercy towards them would be better than their deeds.”

The hadith continues.

If someone says, “Rather, a created being has a right over Allah,” this is correct if he means the right announced by

---

## Page 220

Allah as something that will be fulfilled, for Allah is truthful and never breaks His promise. He Himself has made it binding upon Himself through His wisdom, bounty, and mercy. When someone entitled to this right invokes it in asking Allah Most High, he is asking Allah to fulfil His promise, or asking Him by means of the causes to which Allah has linked their effects, such as righteous deeds. This is appropriate. But when someone who is not entitled to this right asks Him by invoking that person’s right, it is as though he were asking Him by invoking that person’s standing. This is an appeal based on something unrelated to the supplicant; he has not invoked a cause relevant to having his supplication answered.

As for asking Allah by His names and attributes, which entail His granting His servants guidance, provision, and support, these are the greatest means by which to ask Allah Most High.

Thus, the disputant’s statement,

“Allah should not be asked by invoking the right of the prophets, for created beings have no right over the Creator,”

cannot be accepted. The previously cited hadith of Mu‘ādh is authentically established in the two Sahīh collections, and the Most High says:

“Your Lord has prescribed mercy for Himself.”

“And it was incumbent upon Us to help the believers.”

The disputant should therefore be told: this discussion concerns two distinct matters. The first is the right that the servants have over Allah; the second is asking Him by invoking that right.

As for the first, there is no doubt that Allah Most High has promised to reward those who obey Him and to answer those who ask Him. He is truthful and never breaks His promise. Allah Most High says:

“Allah’s promise is true. And who is more truthful than Allah in speech?”

“It is Allah’s promise. Allah does not break His promise, but most people do not know.”

“So never think that Allah will fail to keep His promise to His messengers.”

This, then, is something that must come to pass

---

## Page 221

by virtue of His promise, as all Muslims agree.

They have disagreed, however, over whether He is under any obligation apart from that, holding three views, as discussed earlier. One view is that no one has any binding right over Him apart from that. Another is that certain duties are binding upon Him and certain acts forbidden to Him, by analogy with His servants. A third is that He Himself has imposed obligations upon Himself and forbidden things for Himself: thus, whatever He has made binding upon Himself is binding upon Him, and whatever He has forbidden Himself is forbidden to Him, as established in the Sahīh through the hadith of Abū Dharr cited earlier.

All Muslims agree that injustice is impossible for Him, but they disagree over the nature of the injustice that cannot occur. One view is that it consists of what is impossible, and that anything He can possibly do cannot be injustice, because injustice means either disposing of another’s property or disobeying a command that one is obliged to obey, both of which are impossible for Him. Another view is that whatever constitutes injustice when done by His servants also constitutes injustice when done by Him. A third view is that injustice means putting something where it does not belong. He, glorified be He, does not wrong people in any way. The Most High says:

“But whoever does righteous deeds while being a believer will fear neither injustice nor deprivation.”

The Quranic commentators explained that injustice means having another person’s sins charged against him and being punished for sins he did not commit, while deprivation means having his good deeds diminished.

The Most High also says:

“Indeed, Allah does not wrong anyone even by an atom’s weight. If there is a good deed, He multiplies it and grants from Himself a great reward.”

“We did not wrong them, but they wronged themselves.”

As for the second matter, it may be said: whatever Allah and His Messenger have made clear to be a right that the servants have over Allah is

---

## Page 222

a right; however, the question concerns invoking it in supplication. It may be said: if the right invoked is a reason for the supplication to be answered, then invoking it is appropriate, as with the right due to those who worship Him and supplicate to Him.

But if the supplicant says, “By the right of so-and-so,” then even if those people have a right before Allah that He should not punish them, should honour them with His reward, and should raise their ranks—as He has promised them and made binding upon Himself—their entitlement to such honour from Allah does not constitute a reason for granting this supplicant’s request. That person earned what he earned through the faith and obedience that Allah enabled him to attain, whereas this supplicant has not earned what that person earned. Thus, Allah’s honouring that person does not constitute a reason requiring this supplicant’s prayer to be answered.

If he says, “The reason is that person’s intercession and supplication,” this is true if that person has interceded and supplicated for him. But if he has neither interceded nor supplicated for him, no such reason exists.

If he says, “The reason is my love for him, my faith in him, and my allegiance to him,” this is a means sanctioned by the revealed law. It amounts to asking Allah and approaching Him through the supplicant’s faith, his love for Allah and His Messenger, and his obedience to Allah and His Messenger. However, one must distinguish between loving for Allah’s sake and loving alongside Allah. Whoever loves a created being as he loves the Creator has made that being a rival to Allah; such love harms him and does not benefit him. But whoever loves Allah Most High more than anything else, and loves His prophets and righteous servants for His sake, finds his love for Allah Most High to be the most beneficial of all things. The distinction between these two kinds of love is of the utmost importance.

If it is said: If using faith in him, love for him, and obedience to him as a means takes two forms—sometimes one uses this as a means to attain His reward and Paradise (and this is the greatest of means, and sometimes one uses this as a means

---

## Page 223

in supplication, as in the examples you have mentioned—then a person’s words, “I ask You by Your Prophet Muhammad,” should be understood to mean: “I ask You through my faith in him and my love for him, and I draw near to You through my faith in him, my love for him, and the like.” You have already stated that this is permissible without dispute.

The reply is: Whoever intends this meaning is correct, without dispute. If the statements of those among the early generations who sought to draw near to Allah through the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, after his death—as reported from some of the Companions and Successors, Imam Ahmad, and others—are understood in this sense, that is a sound interpretation. There would then be no dispute over the matter. However, many ordinary people use this expression without intending this meaning; it is these people whom those who objected were criticizing. Likewise, when the Companions spoke of drawing near to Allah through him, they meant through his supplication and intercession, and this is permissible without dispute. Yet most people in our time do not intend this meaning when they use this expression.

If it is said: A person may say to another, “By the right of kinship,” the reply is: Kinship imposes upon a person an obligation towards his relative, as Allah Most High says:

“And fear Allah, in whose name you ask one another, and respect the ties of kinship.”

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Kinship is a bond from the Most Merciful. Whoever maintains it, Allah maintains ties with him; and whoever severs it, Allah cuts him off.”

He also said:

“When Allah created kinship, it clung to the two sides of the Most Merciful’s waist and said, ‘This is the position of one seeking refuge in You from being severed.’ He said, ‘Are you not content that I should maintain ties with whoever maintains you and cut off whoever severs you?’ It replied, ‘Yes, I am content.’”

And he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Allah Most High says: ‘I am the Most Merciful. I created kinship and derived its name from My name. Whoever maintains it, I maintain ties with him; and whoever severs it, I cut him off utterly.’”

---

## Page 224

It has also been reported that whenever his nephew asked him for something by the right of his father, Jaʿfar, ʿAlī would give it to him because of the right Jaʿfar had over him. The right owed to a relative remains after his death, as in the hadith:

“A man said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, is there anything I can still do to honour my parents after their death?’ He replied, ‘Yes: supplicating for them, asking forgiveness for them, fulfilling their promises after they are gone, and maintaining the ties of kinship that you have only through them.’”

Another hadith, narrated by Ibn ʿUmar, states:

“Among the finest acts of filial devotion is for a man to maintain ties with those his father loved after his father has departed.”

Thus, maintaining ties with a deceased person’s relatives and friends after his death is part of fully honouring him.

The statement of Abū Hanīfah, his companions, and other scholars—that it is not permissible to ask Allah Most High by a created being, whether by the right of the prophets or otherwise—encompasses two things, as explained above.

(The first) is adjuring Allah, glorified and exalted is He, by that created being. This is prohibited according to the great majority of scholars, as explained above, just as adjuring Allah by the Kaʿbah or the sacred sites is prohibited by scholarly consensus.

(The second) is asking Him by that created being. Some people permit this, and reports to this effect have been transmitted from some of the early generations. It also occurs in many people’s supplications. However, everything narrated from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, concerning this is weak—indeed, fabricated.

There is no established hadith from him that might be thought to support their position except the hadith of the blind man, whom he taught to say:

“I ask You and turn to You through Your Prophet Muhammad, the Prophet of mercy.”

Yet the hadith of the blind man provides no evidence for their position, for it explicitly states that he sought to draw near to Allah only through the supplication and intercession of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and he

---

## Page 225

asked the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to supplicate for him. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had instructed him to say:

“O Allah, accept his intercession for me.”

Thus Allah restored his sight when the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, supplicated for him. This was counted among the miraculous signs of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. If other blind people, for whom the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had not supplicated, were to seek to draw near to Allah by asking Him by the Prophet, their situation would not be like his.

The well-known supplication for rain made by the Commander of the Faithful, ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb, among the Emigrants and the Helpers, and his words:

“O Allah, when we suffered drought, we used to seek to draw near to You through our Prophet, and You would grant us rain. Now we seek to draw near to You through our Prophet’s uncle,”
