“Although previously they used to pray for victory against those who disbelieved.”

He said: “They sought support, saying: ‘We shall help Muhammad against them.’ Yet they were not as they claimed; they were lying.”

It was also narrated from Ma'mar, from Qatāda, concerning the words of Allah Most High:

“Although previously they used to pray for victory against those who disbelieved.”

He said: “They used to say: ‘A Prophet will come.’ ‘But when there came to them that which they recognized, they disbelieved in it.’”

He also narrated, through his chain of transmission, from Ibn Ishāq:

“Muhammad ibn Abī Muhammad narrated to us, saying: ‘Ikrima—or Sa'īd ibn Jubayr—informed me, from Ibn Abbās, that the Jews used to seek victory over al-Aws and al-Khazraj through Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, before his mission began. But when Allah sent him from among the Arabs, they disbelieved in him and denied what they had previously said about him.

“Mu'ādh ibn Jabal, Bishr ibn al-Barā' ibn Ma'rūr, and Dāwūd ibn Salama said to them: ‘O Jews, fear Allah and embrace Islam. You used to seek victory over us through Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, while we were polytheists. You told us that he would be sent and described his characteristics to us.’

“Salām ibn Mushkim, a member of Banū al-Nadīr, replied: ‘He has brought us nothing that we recognize, and he is not the one we used to tell you about.’ So Allah Most High revealed concerning this:

‘And when there came to them a Book from Allah confirming that which was with them—although previously they used to pray for victory against those who disbelieved—but when there came to them that which they recognized, they disbelieved in it; so the curse of Allah is upon the disbelievers.’”

He also narrated, through his chain of transmission, from al-Rabī' ibn Anas, from Abū al-Āliya, who said:

“The Jews used to seek victory through Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, over the Arab polytheists, saying: ‘O Allah, send this Prophet whom we find mentioned in our scriptures, so that we may torment the polytheists and kill them.’ But when Allah sent Muhammad

---

## Page 301

and saw that he was not from among them, they disbelieved in him out of envy of the Arabs, although they knew that he was Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Allah then said:

‘But when there came to them that which they recognized, they disbelieved in it; so the curse of Allah is upon the disbelievers.’”

As for the hadith narrated from Abd al-Malik ibn Hārūn ibn Antara, from his father, from Sa'īd ibn Jubayr, from Ibn Abbās, who said:

“The Jews of Khaybar used to fight with Sukayna, and whenever they met in battle, the Jews were defeated. They therefore sought refuge in this supplication: ‘O Allah, we ask You by the right of Muhammad, the unlettered Prophet whom You promised to bring forth for us at the end of time, to grant us victory over them.’ Whenever they offered this supplication, they defeated Ghatafān. But when the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, was sent, they disbelieved in him. Allah Most High then revealed:

‘Although previously they used to pray for victory against those who disbelieved—but when there came to them that which they recognized, they disbelieved in it.’”

Al-Hākim narrated this hadith in his Mustadrak and said: “Necessity compelled me to include it.” This is among the things for which scholars criticized him, for Abd al-Malik ibn Hārūn is among the weakest narrators. Scholars of hadith narrators regard his reports as abandoned; indeed, they regard him as a liar. What Yahyā ibn Ma'īn and other leading scholars said about him has already been mentioned.

I say:

This hadith is among them, as is the other hadith he narrates from Abū Bakr, as mentioned earlier. Further evidence of this is that, by agreement of the scholars of Quranic exegesis and the Prophet’s biography, the words of Allah Most High:

“Although previously they used to pray for victory against those who disbelieved”

were revealed specifically concerning the Jews who originally lived near Medina, such as Banū Qaynuqā', Qurayza, and al-Nadīr. They were the ones who formed alliances with al-Aws and al-Khazraj, and with whom the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, concluded a treaty when he arrived in Medina. Then, when they broke the treaty, he fought them

---

## Page 302

—first fighting Banū Qaynuqā', then al-Nadīr—concerning whom Surah al-Hashr was revealed—and then Qurayza in the year of the Battle of the Trench. How, then, can it be said that this verse was revealed concerning the Jews of Khaybar, as al-Bayhaqī says? This account comes from an ignorant liar who did not even know how to lie properly. Further evidence of this is his mention that the Jews defeated Ghatafān when they offered this supplication. No one other than this liar has transmitted such an account. Had it actually happened, truthful narrators would have had ample reason to report it.

It should also be understood that, even if such wording implied petitioning Allah Most High by invoking him and adjuring Allah by him, a report of this kind could not be relied upon to establish religious rulings. First, it has not been established as authentic, and nothing in the verse indicates this meaning. Even if it were established, it would not necessarily constitute a prescribed practice for us. Allah Most High has related the prostration of Joseph’s brothers and parents, and He has related that those who prevailed over the People of the Cave said:

“We will surely build a mosque over them.”

Yet we have been forbidden to build mosques over graves. The wording of the verse states only that they used to seek victory against those who disbelieved, but when there came to them that which they recognized, they disbelieved in it.

This is like the words of Allah Most High:

“If you seek victory, then victory has come to you.”

Istiftāh (seeking victory) means asking for triumph, that is, victory. An example is the transmitted hadith:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, used to seek victory through the poor among the Emigrants.”

That is, he sought victory through them—meaning through their supplications—as he said:

“Are you provided for and granted victory except through the weak among you, through their prayers, supplications, and sincerity?”

This seeking of victory may have consisted of their asking Allah Most High to grant them victory through the Prophet to be sent at the end of time, by hastening his mission to them so that they might prevail over their opponents through him—not of their adjuring Allah by him or petitioning Allah by invoking him. Hence Allah Most High said:

“But when there came to them that which they recognized, they disbelieved in it; so the curse of Allah is upon

---

## Page 303

the disbelievers.”

Even if no transmitted reports had indicated that this is the meaning of the verse, no one would have been entitled to impose that disputed interpretation upon it without evidence, since the verse contains nothing to support it. How much less, then, when reports supporting this meaning have indeed been transmitted?

As for the previously mentioned account that the Jews used to be granted victory, we have explained that it is anomalous and is not among the well-known reports on this subject. The Jews are not known to have defeated the Arabs; rather, they were defeated by them. They used to form alliances with the Arabs, each group allying itself with another: Qurayza were allies of al-Aws, while al-Nadīr were allies of al-Khazraj.

The claim that the Jews used to prevail over the Arabs is therefore unsupported by any known account; indeed, the opposite is what is known. Allah Most High has told us what bears this out. He, Most High, said:

“Humiliation has been imposed upon them wherever they are found, except through a bond from Allah and a bond from people. They have incurred Allah’s wrath, and destitution has been imposed upon them. That is because they used to disbelieve in Allah’s signs and kill the prophets without right. That is because they disobeyed and used to transgress.”

Thus, from the time humiliation was imposed upon the Jews wherever they were found, except through a bond from Allah and a bond from people, they did not prevail on their own over either the Arabs or anyone else. Before Islam, they fought only alongside their allies. Humiliation was imposed upon them from the time the Messiah, peace be upon him, was sent and they rejected him.

He, Most High, said:

“O Jesus, I will take you and raise you to Myself, purify you from those who disbelieve, and place those who follow you above those who disbelieve until the Day of Resurrection.”

And He, Most High, said:

“O you who believe, be supporters of Allah, as Jesus, son of Mary, said

---

## Page 304

to the disciples, ‘Who will be my supporters for Allah?’ The disciples said, ‘We are Allah’s supporters.’ Then a group of the Children of Israel believed, while another group disbelieved. So We supported those who believed against their enemy, and they became victorious.”

They had killed John, son of Zechariah, and other prophets, peace and blessings be upon them. Allah Most High said:

“Humiliation and destitution were imposed upon them, and they incurred Allah’s wrath. That was because they used to disbelieve in Allah’s signs and kill the prophets without right. That was because they disobeyed and used to transgress.”

If, then, the Companions, such as ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb and others, did not adjure Allah by the Prophet’s person, either during his lifetime—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—or after his death, but sought to draw near to Allah only through obedience to him or through his intercession, what, then, is to be said of invoking and petitioning created beings who are absent or dead—prophets, angels, and others?

Allah Most High has said:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim besides Him. They have no power to remove harm from you or to shift it elsewhere.’”

“Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of drawing near to their Lord, seeking to be closest to Him. They hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Indeed, your Lord’s punishment is something to beware of.”

A group of the early generations said:

Some people used to invoke angels and prophets, such as the Messiah, Ezra, and others. Allah forbade this and stated that those being invoked themselves hope for Allah’s mercy, fear His punishment, and seek to draw near to Him. They have no power to remove harm from those invoking them or to shift it away from them.

Allah Most High has also said:

“It is not for any human being to whom Allah has given the Scripture, wisdom, and prophethood to then say to people, ‘Be my worshippers instead of Allah’s.’ Rather, ‘Be devoted scholars of the Lord because you teach the Scripture and because you study it.’”

“Nor would he command you to take angels and prophets as lords. Would he command you to disbelieve after you have become Muslims?”

---

## Page 305

For this reason, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, prohibited taking his grave as a place of prayer or a place of recurring gatherings. During his final illness, he said:

“Allah’s curse be upon the Jews and Christians: they took the graves of their prophets as places of prayer.”

He was warning against what they had done. Al-Bukhārī and Muslim both recorded this in their Sahīhs.

He also said:

“O Allah, do not make my grave an idol that is worshipped. Allah’s wrath was severe against people who took the graves of their prophets as places of prayer.”

Mālik related this in his Muwatta’. He also said:

“Do not exaggerate in praising me as the Christians exaggerated in praising Jesus, son of Mary. I am only a servant, so say: ‘Allah’s servant and Messenger.’”

Related by both al-Bukhārī and Muslim.

He also said:

“Do not say, ‘What Allah wills and Muhammad wills.’ Rather, say, ‘What Allah wills, then Muhammad wills.’”

A Bedouin said to him, “What Allah wills and you will.” He replied, “Have you made me a rival to Allah? Rather, what Allah alone wills.”

Allah Most High said to him:

“Say, ‘I have no power to benefit or harm myself except as Allah wills. Had I known the unseen, I would have acquired much good, and no harm would have touched me.’”

Allah Most High also said:

“Say, ‘I have no power to benefit or harm myself.’”

And He said:

“You do not guide whom you love, but Allah guides whom He wills.”

And He said:

“You have no say in the matter.”

This is the true realization of monotheism, even though he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is the most honoured of all creation before Allah and holds the highest rank with Him.

Al-Tabarānī related in his al-Muʿjam al-Kabīr that a hypocrite was harming the believers. Abū Bakr said, “Come, let us seek the help of Allah’s Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, against this hypocrite.” The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to him, “Help is not to be sought from me; help is to be sought only from Allah.”

Near the end of Sahīh Muslim, it is related that he said five days before his death: “Indeed, those who lived

---

## Page 306

before you used to take graves as places of prayer. Beware: do not take graves as places of prayer, for I forbid you to do so.”

In Sahīh Muslim and elsewhere, it is also related that he said:

“Do not sit on graves or pray facing them.”

In the Sahīhs of al-Bukhārī and Muslim, through Abū Saʿīd and Abū Hurayrah—with numerous chains of transmission through others as well—it is related that he said:

“No journey should be undertaken except to three mosques: this mosque of mine, the Sacred Mosque, and al-Aqsā Mosque.”

Mālik was asked about a man who had vowed to visit the grave of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Mālik said: “If he intended the grave, he should not go to it; but if he intended the mosque, he should go to it.” He then cited the hadith:

“No journey should be undertaken except to three mosques.”

Al-Qādī Ismāʿīl recorded this in his Mabsūt.

If someone were to swear an oath by the right of created beings, his oath would not be binding. There is no distinction in this regard between prophets, angels, and others. Allah, blessed and exalted, has a right in which no one shares—neither the prophets nor anyone else. The prophets have their rights, the believers have theirs, and they have rights over one another.

His right, blessed and exalted, is that they worship Him without associating anything with Him, as stated earlier in the hadith of Muʿādh. Worshipping Him includes devoting their religion sincerely to Him, relying upon Him, turning to Him in hope, and setting up no rival to Allah in their love, fear, supplication, or seeking help. Thus, in the Sahīhs of al-Bukhārī and Muslim, it is related that he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever dies while calling upon a rival besides Allah will enter the Fire.”

He was asked, “Which sin is the gravest?” He replied, “To set up a rival to Allah when He is the One who created you.”

Someone said to him, “What Allah wills and you will.” He replied, “Have you made me a rival to Allah? Rather, what Allah alone wills.”

---

## Page 307

Allah, exalted is He, has said:

“Indeed, Allah does not forgive associating partners with Him, but He forgives anything less than that for whomever He wills.”

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“So do not set up rivals to Allah while you know.”

“And Allah has said, ‘Do not take two gods. He is but One God, so fear Me alone.’”

“So worship Me alone.”

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“So when you have finished, exert yourself.”

“And direct your longing to your Lord.”

And He, exalted is He, has said in the Opening of the Book, which is the Mother of the Quran:

“You alone we worship, and You alone we ask for help.”

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“Among people are those who take rivals besides Allah, loving them as they love Allah. But those who believe are stronger in their love for Allah.”

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“So do not fear people, but fear Me.”

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“Those who convey Allah’s messages, fear Him, and fear no one but Allah.”

Thus, when the polytheists sought to frighten Abraham, Allah’s intimate friend—may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon him—Allah, exalted is He, said:

“His people disputed with him. He said, ‘Do you dispute with me concerning Allah when He has guided me? I do not fear what you associate with Him, unless my Lord wills something. My Lord encompasses all things in knowledge. Will you not then take heed?’”

“‘How could I fear what you associate with Him, when you do not fear having associated with Allah that for which He has sent down no authority to you? Which of the two parties has more right to security, if you know?’”

“Those who believe and do not mix their faith with wrongdoing—it is they who will have security, and they are rightly guided.”

In the Sahīhs of al-Bukhārī and Muslim, Ibn Masʿūd said:

“When this verse was revealed—‘Those who believe and do not mix their faith with wrongdoing’—it distressed the Companions of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. They said, ‘Which of us has not wronged himself?’ The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, told them, ‘That refers only to associating partners with Allah, as the righteous servant said: “My son, do not associate partners with Allah. Indeed, associating partners with Him is a tremendous wrong.”’”

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“Whoever obeys Allah and His Messenger, fears Allah, and is mindful of Him—it is they who are the successful.”

---

## Page 308

Thus He made obedience due to Allah and the Messenger, for whoever obeys the Messenger has obeyed Allah. He reserved reverent fear and mindfulness for Allah alone: one should fear none but Allah and be mindful of none but Him.

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“So do not fear people, but fear Me, and do not exchange My verses for a small price.”

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“So do not fear them, but fear Me, if you are believers.”

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“If only they had been content with what Allah and His Messenger gave them and had said, ‘Allah is sufficient for us. Allah will give us of His bounty, and so will His Messenger. To Allah alone we turn with longing.’”

He, glorified is He, thus attributed giving to both Allah and the Messenger at the beginning and the end of this passage, as in His words, exalted is He:

“Whatever the Messenger gives you, take it; and whatever he forbids you, refrain from it.”

Yet He attributed bounty to Allah alone and directed longing to Allah alone. He, exalted is He, alone is sufficient for them; He has no partner in that.

Al-Bukhārī narrated that Ibn ʿAbbās said concerning the words, “Allah is sufficient for us, and He is the best to entrust with our affairs”:

“Abraham said these words when he was cast into the fire, and Muhammad said them when ‘People said to them, “The people have gathered against you, so fear them.” But this increased them in faith, and they said, “Allah is sufficient for us, and He is the best to entrust with our affairs.”’”

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“O Prophet, Allah is sufficient for you and for those believers who follow you.”

According to the majority of the early and later scholars, this means that Allah alone is sufficient for you and sufficient for those believers who follow you, as has been explained at length with supporting evidence. This is because the messengers, blessings and peace be upon them, are the intermediaries between us and Allah in conveying His commands and prohibitions, His promises and warnings. Thus, what is lawful is what Allah and His Messenger have made lawful; what is forbidden is what Allah and His Messenger have forbidden; and the religion is what Allah and His Messenger have prescribed.

---

## Page 309

We must therefore love Allah and His Messenger, obey Allah and His Messenger, and please Allah and His Messenger. He, exalted is He, has said:

“Allah and His Messenger are more worthy of being pleased, if they are believers.”

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“Obey Allah and obey the Messenger.”

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“Whoever obeys the Messenger has obeyed Allah.”

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“Say, ‘If your fathers, your sons, your brothers, your spouses, your relatives, wealth you have acquired, commerce whose decline you fear, and homes you cherish are dearer to you than Allah and His Messenger and striving in His cause, then wait until Allah brings His command.’”

In the two authentic hadith collections, Anas reported that the Messenger of Allah, blessings and peace be upon him, said:

“There are three qualities through which whoever possesses them tastes the sweetness of faith: that Allah and His Messenger are dearer to him than anyone else; that he loves a person only for Allah’s sake; and that he hates to return to unbelief, after Allah has saved him from it, as he hates to be cast into the Fire.”

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“Indeed, We have sent you as a witness, a bearer of glad tidings, and a warner.”

“So that you may believe in Allah and His Messenger, support and honor him, and glorify Him morning and evening.”

Thus, faith is in Allah and the Messenger; support and honor are due to the Messenger, and supporting him means helping and protecting him. Glorification morning and evening is for Allah alone, for this is an act of worship, and worship belongs to Allah alone.

Prayer must therefore be offered to Allah alone, and fasting must be undertaken for Allah alone. Pilgrimage must be made only to the House of Allah, and journeys must not be undertaken except to the three mosques, because these mosques were built by Allah’s prophets with Allah’s permission. Vows must be made to Allah alone, oaths must be sworn by Allah alone, supplication must be directed to Allah alone, and aid in distress must be sought from Allah alone.

As for what Allah, glorified is He, has created of animals, plants, rain, clouds, and all other

---

## Page 310

created things, He has not appointed any of His servants as an intermediary in that creation, as He has appointed the messengers as intermediaries in conveying His message. Rather, He creates whatever He wills through whatever causes He wills. Nothing among created things can independently bring anything into existence. Every cause requires other causes to assist it, and whatever opposes it must be counteracted. This is something that Allah alone can accomplish. Whatever Allah wills happens, and whatever He does not will does not happen. Conveying His message is different: the Messenger alone served as the intermediary in conveying His message to His servants.

As for placing guidance in the hearts of His servants, that belongs to Allah, exalted is He, not to the Messenger. As Allah, exalted is He, has said:

“Indeed, you do not guide whom you love, but Allah guides whom He wills.”

And He, exalted is He, has said:

“Even if you are eager for their guidance, Allah does not guide those He causes to go astray.”

Likewise, the supplications of the prophets, blessings and peace be upon them, their requests for forgiveness, and their intercession are a cause that brings benefit only if Allah, exalted is He, makes the recipient receptive to it. Otherwise, even if the Prophet were to ask forgiveness for the unbelievers and hypocrites, they would not be forgiven. Allah, exalted is He, has said:

“It is all the same for them whether you ask forgiveness for them or do not ask forgiveness for them: Allah will never forgive them.”

As for the messengers, it has become clear that they are the intermediaries between us and Allah, mighty and majestic, in conveying His commands, prohibitions, promises, warnings, and declarations. We must therefore believe everything they have conveyed and obey them in what they have made obligatory and commanded. We must believe in all the prophets of Allah, mighty and majestic, making no distinction between any of them. Whoever reviles any one of them is an unbeliever and an apostate whose blood may lawfully be shed.

When we discuss the exclusive devotion due to Allah, blessed and exalted is He, we explain that the prophets and all other created beings are not entitled to what belongs exclusively to Allah, blessed and exalted is He. Thus, they must not be associated with Him as partners, nor may one place one’s trust in them or seek their aid in distress as one seeks Allah’s aid, nor may one adjure

---

## Page 311

Allah by invoking them, nor may one seek to draw near to Him through their persons themselves. Rather, one seeks to draw near to Him through faith in them, love for them, obedience to them, allegiance to them, supporting and honoring them, opposing those who oppose them, obeying their commands, believing what they report, and regarding as lawful what they have declared lawful and as forbidden what they have forbidden.

Using these as a means takes two forms:

First: using them to obtain an answer to one’s supplication and the granting of one’s request, as in the hadith of the three men who took refuge in the cave. They invoked their righteous deeds so that Allah would answer their supplication and relieve their distress. This has already been explained.

Second: using them to attain Allah’s reward, His Paradise, and His good pleasure. The righteous deeds commanded by the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, are the complete means to happiness in this world and the Hereafter. An example is the believers’ prayer:

“Our Lord, indeed we have heard a caller calling to faith, ‘Believe in your Lord,’ and we have believed. Our Lord, so forgive us our sins and remove from us our misdeeds and cause us to die with the righteous.”

They mentioned their faith before making their supplication. Another example is what Allah, glorified is He, relates about the believers in His words:

“Indeed, there was a group of My servants who said, ‘Our Lord, we have believed, so forgive us and have mercy upon us, and You are the best of the merciful.’”

There are many such examples.

Likewise, seeking access to Allah through the supplication and intercession of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, takes two forms:

First: asking him to supplicate and intercede, whereupon he supplicates and intercedes, as people used to ask him during his lifetime and as they will ask him on the Day of Resurrection, when they come to Adam and Noah, then to Abraham, Allah’s intimate friend, then to

---

## Page 312

Moses, to whom Allah spoke, then Jesus, and then they come to Muhammad—may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon him and upon them—and ask him to intercede.

Second: alongside this, one seeks access to Allah Most High by asking Him through the Prophet’s intercession and supplication, as in the hadith of the blind man cited and explained earlier. He asked the Messenger to supplicate and intercede for him, so the Messenger supplicated and interceded for him and instructed him to supplicate to Allah, saying:

“O Allah, I ask You and turn to You through him. O Allah, accept his intercession for me.”

Thus, he instructed him to ask Allah Most High to accept his intercession. This differs from someone who seeks access to Allah through the Messenger’s supplication and intercession when the Messenger has neither supplicated nor interceded for him. Such a person is seeking access through something that has not occurred. Only someone for whom he has supplicated and interceded can seek access through his supplication and intercession.

This category also includes the words of the Commander of the Faithful, ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb, when seeking rain, as mentioned earlier. ʿUmar and the Muslims sought access to Allah through the supplication of al-ʿAbbās, and they themselves asked Allah Most High while al-ʿAbbās supplicated. They all sought intercession; al-ʿAbbās was not the only one who supplicated for them. Thus, seeking access through obedience to the Messenger and seeking access through his intercession both accompany the supplication and request of the person seeking access; neither occurs without them. These, then, are four forms, all of which are legitimate, and none is disputed by any person of knowledge and faith.

The religion of Islam rests upon two foundations: truly fulfilling the testimony that none has the right to be worshipped but Allah and that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah. The first requirement is that you not set up another god alongside Allah: you must not love a created being as you love Allah, hope in that being as you hope in Allah, or fear that being as you fear Allah. And whoever equates

---

## Page 313

a created being with the Creator in any of these respects has set up an equal to Allah and is among those who equate others with their Lord. He has taken another god alongside Allah, even if he also believes that Allah alone created the heavens and the earth.

The Arab polytheists acknowledged that Allah alone created the heavens and the earth, as He Most High says:

“If you ask them who created the heavens and the earth, they will surely say, ‘Allah.’”

Yet they were polytheists who took other gods alongside Allah. He Most High says:

“Do you truly testify that there are other gods alongside Allah? Say, ‘I do not testify to that.’”

He Most High also says:

“Among people are those who take others besides Allah as equals, loving them as they love Allah. But those who believe are stronger in their love for Allah.”

They became polytheists because they loved those beings as they loved Him, not because they claimed that their gods created as He creates. As He Most High says:

“Or have they assigned partners to Allah who created as He creates, so that their creations seemed alike to them?”

This is a rhetorical question expressing denial, with the force of a negation: they did not assign partners to Allah who created as He creates, for they acknowledged that their gods did not create as He creates.

Rather, they took them as intercessors and intermediaries. He Most High says:

“They worship besides Allah what can neither harm nor benefit them, and they say, ‘These are our intercessors with Allah.’ Say, ‘Do you inform Allah of something He does not know in the heavens or on earth?’ Glory be to Him, and exalted is He above what they associate with Him.”

The man mentioned in Surah Yā Sīn said:

“Why should I not worship the One who created me, and to whom you will be returned?”

“Should I take other gods besides Him? If the Most Merciful intends harm for me, their intercession will avail me nothing, nor can they rescue me.”

“Then I would indeed be clearly astray.”

“I have believed in your Lord, so listen to me.”

(The second foundation is that we worship Him according to what He has prescribed through His Messengers. We worship Him only through acts that are obligatory or recommended. Permissible acts also fall within this when undertaken with the intention of obeying Him.

---

## Page 314

And supplication is among the acts of worship. Whoever calls upon created beings who are dead or absent and seeks their aid—although neither Allah nor His Messenger has commanded this as obligatory or recommended—is introducing an innovation into religion, associating partners with the Lord of the worlds, and following a path other than that of the believers.

Whoever asks Allah Most High by created beings, or adjures Him by created beings, is introducing an innovation for which Allah has sent down no authority. If he condemns those who disagree with him and seeks to have them punished, he is unjust, ignorant, and transgressive. If he issues a judgment to that effect, he has judged by something other than what Allah has revealed, and his judgment must be overturned by the consensus of the Muslims. He is far more in need of being required to repent of this judgment and being punished for it than of having it enforced on his behalf and receiving assistance in doing so. All of this is agreed upon among the Muslims; there is no disagreement over it, whether among the four imams or others.

These matters have been discussed at length in several volumes, including a work in which we set out principles concerning judges’ rulings and the matters on which they may or may not pass judgment. It is a separate treatise dealing with the rulings pertaining to this subject. It would not be appropriate to include any of its chapters here, since the discussion in this place is devoted specifically to the principles of monotheism and related matters. An abridged portion of that work, with its chapters carefully refined, will be presented later in separate sheets for the thoughtful reader to consult, given the further benefit it offers and the pressing need to understand this important matter. And Allah grants success.

While I was in Egypt in the year 711 AH, I was asked for a legal opinion concerning

---

## Page 315

seeking a means of approach to Allah through the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. I wrote a detailed response on the subject and wished to include it here for the additional benefit it offers. For the more varied the explanations of these principles—concerning the affirmation of monotheism and the elimination of the roots of polytheism and excess—and the clearer their wording, the more this becomes light upon light. And Allah is the One whose help is sought.

The question was worded as follows:

The esteemed scholars, the imams of religion, are asked to explain what is permissible and what is not permissible in seeking intercession and seeking a means of approach to Allah through the prophets and the righteous.

The response was as follows:

All praise is due to Allah, Lord of the worlds. The Muslims unanimously agree that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, will intercede for creation on the Day of Resurrection, after people ask him to do so and after Allah permits him to intercede.

Ahl al-Sunnah wa-l-Jamāʿah likewise agree upon what the Companions—may Allah be pleased with them all—agreed upon and what has been widely transmitted in the prophetic traditions: that he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, will intercede for those of his community who have committed major sins, and will also intercede for creation at large.

He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, has forms of intercession exclusive to him, in which no one else shares, and other forms in which other prophets and righteous people share. Yet his share in these is superior to that of anyone else, for he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is the best of creation and the most honoured by his Lord, Almighty and Majestic. The virtues with which Allah has distinguished him above all other prophets are too numerous to discuss at length here. Among these is “the station

---

## Page 316

of praise,” which people of the earliest and latest generations will envy him for. The hadiths concerning intercession are numerous and transmitted through so many independent chains as to establish certainty. Several appear in the two Sahīh collections, and many more in the Sunan and Musnad collections.

As for the Waʿīdiyyah—those among the Khawārij and Muʿtazilah who hold that divine threats must be fulfilled—they claimed that intercession is exclusively for believers, serving only to raise their ranks. Some of them denied intercession altogether.

The scholars unanimously agree that the Companions used to seek his intercession and seek a means of approach to Allah through him during his lifetime, in his presence. Thus, Sahīh al-Bukhārī records from Anas ibn Mālik that whenever they suffered drought, ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb would seek rain through al-ʿAbbās ibn ʿAbd al-Muttalib, saying:

“O Allah, whenever we suffered drought, we used to seek a means of approach to You through our Prophet, and You would grant us rain. Now we seek a means of approach to You through our Prophet’s uncle, so grant us rain.”

They would then be granted rain.

Al-Bukhārī also records that Ibn ʿUmar said:

“I would sometimes recall the poet’s words while looking at the face of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, as he prayed for rain. He would not come down before every waterspout was gushing:

‘A fair-faced man, through whose countenance the clouds are entreated for rain,
A refuge for orphans, a protector of widows.’”

The seeking of a means of approach through the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, mentioned by ʿUmar ibn al-Khattāb is explained in the other hadiths concerning prayers for rain. It is a form of seeking his intercession: asking him to supplicate and intercede, and asking Allah to accept his supplication and intercession. We put him forward to intercede and petition on our behalf—may my father and mother be sacrificed for him, and may Allah bless him and grant him peace.

Likewise, when the people of Syria suffered drought, Muʿāwiyah ibn Abī Sufyān sought rain through Yazīd ibn al-Aswad al-Jurashī, saying:

“O Allah, we seek intercession—and a means of approach—through the best among us. Yazīd, raise your hands.”

He raised his hands, and the people supplicated until they were granted rain.

---

## Page 317

For this reason, the scholars said:

It is recommended to seek rain through people of religious devotion and righteousness; if they belong to the household of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, that is better still.

The reality of seeking intercession and a means of approach in this way is to seek a means of approach through the person’s supplication. He would supplicate for those seeking a means of approach and intercession through him, and the people would supplicate along with him, as in the following account:

“When the Muslims suffered drought during the lifetime of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, a Bedouin came to him and said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, the livestock have perished and the roads have become impassable. Supplicate to Allah to grant us rain.’

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, raised his hands and said, ‘O Allah, grant us rain! O Allah, grant us rain! O Allah, grant us rain!’ There was not a wisp of cloud in the sky, but a cloud formed in the direction of the sea, and rain fell upon them for a week, during which they did not see the sun. Then the Bedouin—or someone else—came in and said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, the roads have become impassable and the buildings have collapsed. Supplicate to Allah to lift this rain from us.’

“He raised his hands and said, ‘O Allah, around us, not upon us! O Allah, upon the hills and hillocks, the places where trees grow, and the valley beds!’ The clouds drew away from Medina as a garment is drawn aside.”

This hadith is well known in the two Sahīh collections and elsewhere. Another hadith, recorded in Sunan Abī Dāwūd and elsewhere, states:

“A man said to him, ‘We seek your intercession with Allah, and we seek Allah’s intercession with you.’ The Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, glorified Allah until the effect of this could be seen on his Companions’ faces, and said, ‘Woe to you! Do you know who Allah is? Allah is not sought as an intercessor with any of His creation. Allah’s majesty is far greater than that.’”

This makes clear that seeking a person’s intercession—in the words of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and his Companions—means seeking intercession through his supplication and intercession. It does not mean making a request by invoking his person. For if this were

---

## Page 318

a request made by invoking his person, then asking created beings by invoking Allah Most High would be more fitting than asking Allah by invoking created beings. But since it meant the former, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, rejected the man’s statement:

“We seek Allah’s intercession with you.”

He did not reject his statement, “We seek your intercession with Allah,” because an intercessor asks the one with whom he intercedes to fulfil the petitioner’s need. Allah Most High does not ask any of His servants to fulfil the needs of His creation, although a poet spoke of seeking Allah Most High as an intercessor in words such as these:

“My intercessor with you is Allah; there is no Lord besides Him,
And there is no way to turn the intercessor away.”

These are objectionable words that no scholar has uttered. Likewise, some who hold that the Creator and creation are one have mentioned seeking Allah’s intercession—glory be to Him—with the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Both are error and misguidance. Rather, He—glory be to Him—is the One who is asked and invoked, whom everyone in the heavens and on earth asks. He, blessed and exalted, commands His servants, and they obey Him. Whenever obedience to a created being is obligatory, it is obligatory only because it constitutes obedience to Allah Most High. The messengers convey Allah’s commands on His behalf: whoever obeys them has obeyed Allah, and whoever pledges allegiance to them has pledged allegiance to Allah.

Allah Most High says:

“We did not send any messenger except to be obeyed by permission of Allah.”

And He Most High says:

“Whoever obeys the Messenger has obeyed Allah.”

Those in authority, whether scholars or rulers, must be obeyed only when they command obedience to Allah and His Messenger.

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said in an authentic hadith:

“A Muslim must listen and obey, in hardship and ease, whether willing or reluctant, unless he is commanded to disobey Allah. If he is commanded to disobey Allah, then he must neither listen nor obey.”

He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, also said:

“There is no obedience to a created being in disobedience to the Creator.”

---

## Page 319

As for an intercessor, he is a petitioner whose request for intercession need not be obeyed, however great his standing. An authentic hadith relates:

“The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, asked Barīra to remain with her husband and not leave him after she had been freed and the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had given her a choice, whereupon she chose to leave him. Her husband loved her and began to weep, so the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, asked her to remain with him. She said, ‘Are you commanding me?’ He replied, ‘No; I am only interceding.’”

She asked, “Are you commanding me?” and he replied, “I am only interceding,” because Muslims understood that obeying his commands was obligatory, whereas accepting his intercession was not. For this reason, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, did not blame her for declining his intercession. It follows all the more clearly that accepting the intercession of any other created being is not obligatory.

The Creator—exalted is His majesty—is far too exalted and majestic to intercede with a created being. Indeed, He—glory be to Him—is so exalted that no one may intercede with Him except by His permission. Allah Most High says:

“And they say, ‘The Most Merciful has taken a son.’ Exalted is He! Rather, they are honored servants.”

“They cannot precede Him in word, and they act by His command.”

“He knows what is before them and what is behind them, and they cannot intercede except on behalf of one whom He approves. And they, from fear of Him, are apprehensive.”

“And whoever of them should say, ‘Indeed, I am a god besides Him’—that one We would recompense with Hell. Thus do We recompense the wrongdoers.”

The hadith cited earlier shows that the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, may be asked to intercede with Allah, Mighty and Majestic—that is, he is asked to petition his Lord by way of intercession, in this world and the Hereafter. In the Hereafter, people will ask him to intercede so that Allah judges between them and so that they enter Paradise. He will intercede for those of his community who committed major sins. He will also intercede for some who deserve the Fire so that they do not enter it, and for some who have entered it so that they are brought out of it.

---

## Page 320

And there is no dispute among the great majority of the Muslim community that he may intercede for those whose obedience entitles them to reward.

However, many among the adherents of religious innovation, the Khawārij, and the Mu'tazila denied his intercession for those who commit major sins. They said, “He does not intercede for those who commit major sins,” because, according to them, Allah neither forgives such people nor brings them out of the Fire once they have entered it, whether through intercession or otherwise. The position of the Companions, the Successors, the leading Muslim scholars, and all other adherents of the Sunnah and the community is that he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, intercedes for those who commit major sins, and that no believer remains in the Fire forever. Rather, anyone whose heart contains a grain’s weight or an atom’s weight of faith will emerge from the Fire.

But this seeking of rain, requesting intercession, and seeking a means of approach to Allah through him or others took place during their lifetimes: people would ask them to supplicate, and they would supplicate for them. Thus, their means of approach was his supplication; seeking his intercession meant asking him to intercede, and intercession is supplication.

As for seeking a means of approach through his person, whether in his presence, in his absence, or after his death—such as adjuring Allah by his person or by another prophet, or petitioning by their very persons through their supplications—this was not a well-known practice among the Companions and the Successors. Rather, when drought struck, 'Umar ibn al-Khattāb, Mu'āwiya ibn Abī Sufyān, and those present with them from among the Companions of the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and those who followed them in righteousness sought rain, sought a means of approach to Allah, and requested intercession through living people, such as al-'Abbās and Yazīd ibn al-Aswad. In these circumstances, they did not seek a means of approach, request intercession, or seek rain through the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, either at his grave or elsewhere. Instead, they turned to alternatives, such as al-'Abbās

---

## Page 321

and Yazīd. Rather, they would invoke blessings upon him in their supplications. 'Umar said:

“O Allah, we used to seek a means of approach to You through our Prophet, and You would grant us rain. Now we seek a means of approach to You through our Prophet’s uncle, so grant us rain.”

They therefore substituted this for their former practice when it was no longer possible to seek a means of approach through him in the prescribed manner they had previously followed. They could have gone to his grave and sought a means of approach through him, or invoked his standing in their supplications in the open country, using similar expressions that entail adjuring Allah, Almighty and Majestic, by a created being or petitioning Him by that being. They could have said, “We ask You, or adjure You, by Your Prophet, or by Your Prophet’s standing,” and similar expressions used by some people.

Some ignorant people have related that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“When you ask Allah, ask Him by my standing, for my standing with Allah is great.”

This hadith is fabricated. It is not found in any of the Muslim books upon which hadith scholars rely, nor has any scholar of hadith mentioned it—even though his standing with Allah Most High is greater than that of all the other prophets and messengers. Allah, glorified is He, has told us that Moses and Jesus, peace be upon them both, held places of honor with Him. He Most High said:

“O you who believe, do not be like those who offended Moses. Allah cleared him of what they said, and he was held in honor by Allah.”

He Most High also said:

“When the angels said, ‘O Mary, Allah gives you glad tidings of a word from Him, whose name will be the Messiah, Jesus, son of Mary, honored in this world and the Hereafter, and among those brought near to Him.’”

If Moses and Jesus held places of honor with Allah, Almighty and Majestic, then how much more so the leader of the children of Adam, the one granted the Praiseworthy Station for which the earliest and latest generations will regard him with admiring envy, and the one granted al-Kawthar

---

## Page 322

and the Pool to which people will come, whose vessels are as numerous as the stars in the sky, whose water is whiter than milk and sweeter than honey, and from which whoever takes a single drink will never thirst again? He is the one who will intercede on the Day of Resurrection, when Adam and the messengers of firm resolve—Noah, Abraham, Moses, and Jesus, may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon them all—step back from it, while he steps forward to undertake it. He is the bearer of the banner beneath which Adam and all others will stand. He is the leader of the children of Adam and the most honored of them before his Lord, Almighty and Majestic. He is the leader of the prophets when they assemble and their spokesman when they come as a delegation. His standing is immense; may Allah bless him and his family and grant them peace.

Yet a created being’s standing before the Creator Most High is not like one created being’s standing before another, for no one intercedes with Him except by His permission:

“There is no one in the heavens and the earth who will not come to the Most Merciful as a servant.”

“He has certainly counted them and numbered them precisely.”

He Most High also said:

“The Messiah will never disdain to be a servant of Allah, nor will the angels brought near to Him. Whoever disdains His worship and is arrogant—He will gather them all to Himself.”

“As for those who believe and do righteous deeds, He will give them their rewards in full and grant them more from His bounty. But as for those who disdain and are arrogant, He will punish them with a painful punishment, and they will find neither a protector nor a helper besides Allah.”

A created being intercedes with another created being without the latter’s permission, and is thus his partner in securing the desired outcome. Allah Most High, however, has no partner, as He, glorified is He, said:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim to be gods besides Allah. They do not possess even an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth, nor do they have any share in either, nor does He have any helper among them.’”

“Intercession does not benefit with Him except for one whom He permits.”

---

## Page 323

Numerous hadiths have been transmitted from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, establishing that he forbade taking graves as places of prayer, cursed those who did so, and forbade making his grave a place of recurring gatherings. This is because associating partners with Allah first arose among the children of Adam in the people of Noah.

Ibn 'Abbās said:

“Between Adam and Noah were ten generations, all of whom followed Islam.”

It is established in the two Sahīh collections, on the authority of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, that Noah was the first messenger Allah sent to the people of the earth. Allah Most High related that his people said:

“Never abandon your gods, nor abandon Wadd, Suwā', Yaghūth, Ya'ūq, or Nasr.”

“And they have already led many astray.”

Several of the early generations said:

“These were righteous men among the people of Noah. When they died, the people remained devotedly at their graves; then, after a long time had passed, they worshipped them.”

Al-Bukhārī recorded this in his Sahīh on the authority of Ibn 'Abbās. He also mentioned that these gods passed to the Arabs and named the Arab tribes among whom these idols were found.

The Companions, may Allah be pleased with them, knew that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, had cut off the roots of associating partners with Allah by forbidding taking graves as places of prayer—even when the person praying was praying to Allah, Almighty and Majestic. Likewise, he forbade prayer at sunrise so that the person praying would not resemble those who prayed to the sun, even though he was praying only to Allah Most High. They also knew that someone who deliberately supplicated through a deceased person or at his grave was closer to associating partners with Allah than someone who intended only to pray to Allah, Almighty and Majestic. They therefore did not engage in such practices.

The Companions likewise knew that seeking nearness to Allah through him meant only doing so by believing in him and obeying him

---

## Page 324

and loving him and remaining loyal to him, or by means of his supplication and intercession. For this reason, they did not seek nearness to Allah through his person alone, apart from either of these means.

The Companions, may Allah be pleased with them, did none of those things, nor did they offer supplications of this kind. They were more knowledgeable than we are, knew better what Allah and His Messenger love, and understood better which supplications Allah commanded His Messenger to offer and which were more likely to be answered. Instead, they sought nearness to Allah through al-‘Abbās and others who were not equal to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace. Their turning from the more excellent to the less excellent demonstrated that the prescribed means of seeking nearness to Allah through the more excellent was no longer possible.

He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“O Allah, do not make my grave an idol that is worshipped. Allah’s wrath was severe against people who took the graves of their prophets as places of prayer.”

Mālik narrated this in his Muwatta’, as did others. In the Sunan of Abū Dāwūd, it is related that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Do not make my grave a place of recurring gatherings. Send blessings upon me wherever you are, for your blessings reach me.”

In the two Sahīh collections, it is related that he said during his final illness:

“May Allah curse the Jews and Christians: they took the graves of their prophets as places of prayer.”

He was warning against what they had done. ‘Ā’ishah said: “Were it not for that, he would have had his grave left in the open, but he disliked its being made a place of prayer.”

In Sahīh Muslim, Jundub relates that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said five days before his death:

“I disavow before Allah having any of you as an intimate friend. Were I to take an intimate friend from among my community, I would have taken Abū Bakr as an intimate friend, for Allah has taken me as an intimate friend just as He took Abraham as an intimate friend. Those who came before you used to take graves as places of prayer. Beware! Do not take graves as places of prayer, for I forbid you to do so.”

In the Sahīh collection, it is related from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, that he

---

## Page 325

said:

“Do not praise me excessively as the Christians praised Jesus son of Mary. I am only a servant, so say: ‘The servant of Allah and His Messenger.’”

Al-Tirmidhī narrated an authentic hadith from the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, in which he taught a man to supplicate, saying:

“O Allah, I ask You and seek nearness to You through Your Prophet Muhammad, the Prophet of mercy. O Muhammad, O Messenger of Allah, I seek nearness through you to my Lord concerning my need, that He may fulfil it for me. O Allah, accept his intercession for me.”

Al-Nasā’ī narrated a similar supplication. In al-Tirmidhī and Ibn Mājah, it is related from ‘Uthmān ibn Hanīf:

“A blind man came to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and said: ‘Supplicate to Allah to restore my health.’ He replied: ‘If you wish, I will supplicate; and if you wish, you may remain patient, which is better for you.’

“The man said: ‘Then supplicate to Him.’ He instructed him to perform ablution thoroughly and offer this supplication: ‘O Allah, I ask You and turn to You through Your Prophet Muhammad, the Prophet of mercy. O Messenger of Allah, O Muhammad, I have turned through you to my Lord concerning this need of mine, so that it may be fulfilled. O Allah, accept his intercession for me.’”

Al-Tirmidhī said: “This is a sound and authentic hadith.” Al-Nasā’ī also narrated it from ‘Uthmān ibn Hanīf, with the following wording:

“A blind man said: ‘O Messenger of Allah, supplicate to Allah to restore my sight.’ He replied: ‘Go and perform ablution, then pray two units of prayer, then say: “O Allah, I ask You and turn to You through Your Prophet Muhammad, the Prophet of mercy. O Muhammad, I turn through you to my Lord, that He may restore my sight. O Allah, accept his intercession for me.”’ The narrator said: ‘He returned, and Allah had restored his sight.’”

Imam Ahmad said in his Musnad:

“Rūh narrated to us: Shu‘bah narrated to us, from ‘Umayr ibn Yazīd

---

## Page 326

al-Khatmī al-Madīnī, who said:

I heard ‘Umārah ibn Khuzaymah ibn Thābit relate from ‘Uthmān ibn Hanīf that a blind man came to the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, and said: ‘O Prophet of Allah, supplicate to Allah to restore my health.’ He replied: ‘If you wish, I will defer that, which is better for you in the Hereafter; and if you wish, I will supplicate for you.’ The man said: ‘No, rather supplicate to Allah for me.’ He instructed him to perform ablution, pray two units of prayer, and offer this supplication: ‘O Allah, I ask You and turn to You through Your Prophet Muhammad, the Prophet of mercy. O Muhammad, I turn through you to my Lord concerning this need of mine, that it may be fulfilled. O Allah, accept my intercession for him and his intercession for me.’ He said: The man did so and was healed.”

This hadith thus contains seeking nearness to Allah through him in supplication.

Some people say that this entails the permissibility of seeking nearness through him without qualification, whether he is alive or dead. Those who seek nearness through his person after his death or in his absence cite this as evidence. They suppose that the blind man and the Companions sought nearness through him during his lifetime by adjuring Allah by him, or by asking Allah through his person to fulfil their needs. They suppose that seeking nearness through him requires neither that he supplicate for them nor that they obey him. To them, it makes no difference whether the Messenger supplicated for them or not: they regard everyone as having sought nearness through him. Nor does it make any difference whether they obeyed him or not.

They suppose that Allah Most High fulfils the need of someone who, as they claim, sought nearness through him, although the Messenger did not supplicate for him, just as He fulfils the need of someone who sought nearness through his supplication and for whom the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, did supplicate. In their view, both have sought nearness through him. They suppose that everyone who asks Allah Most High through the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, has sought nearness through him just as that blind man did, and that what the blind man was instructed to do is prescribed for them.

Their position is false both in terms of divine law and divine decree: they neither conform to Allah’s law, nor does what they say correspond to Allah’s creation.

---

## Page 327

Others say:

This is a particular case. Its ruling applies to analogous cases that share the grounds on which the ruling rests; it does not establish the same ruling for cases that differ from it rather than resemble it. There is a distinction, both in divine law and in divine decree, between someone for whom the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, supplicated and someone for whom he did not. The two must not be treated as equivalent.

The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, interceded for this blind man. That is why the man said in his supplication:

“O Allah, accept his intercession for me.”

It is therefore clear that he was interceding on the man’s behalf. The wording is:

“If you wish, you may be patient; and if you wish, I will supplicate for you.” The man replied: “Supplicate for me.”

Thus, he asked the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to supplicate for him. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, instructed him to pray and also to supplicate for himself, saying in his supplication:

“O Allah, accept his intercession for me.”

This indicates that his words, “I ask You and turn to You through Your Prophet Muhammad,” mean through his supplication and intercession. Similarly, ‘Umar said:

“O Allah, whenever we suffered drought, we used to seek nearness to You through our Prophet, and You would grant us rain.”

The two hadiths therefore have the same meaning. He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, taught a man to seek nearness through him during his lifetime, just as ‘Umar mentioned that they used to seek nearness through him when they suffered drought. After his death, however, they sought nearness only through others in his place.

If seeking nearness through him were the same whether he was alive or dead, and if someone who sought nearness through him and for whom the Messenger supplicated were like someone for whom the Messenger did not supplicate, they would not have turned away from seeking nearness through him—the best of creation, the most honoured by his Lord, and the nearest to Him—to seeking nearness through others who were not his equals.

---

## Page 328

Likewise, if a blind man who sought nearness through him, but for whom the Messenger did not supplicate, were in the same position as that blind man, the blind Companions, or some of them, would have done as he did. Their turning from one practice to the other is evidence that what is prescribed is the course they followed, not the one they abandoned. They were, after all, the earliest and foremost believers—the Emigrants, the Helpers, and those who followed them in righteousness—and they knew better than we do about Allah and His Messenger, the rights of Allah and His Messenger, which forms of supplication are prescribed and beneficial, which are neither prescribed nor beneficial, and which are more beneficial than others. Moreover, they were suffering distress, famine, and drought, seeking relief from hardships, ease in difficulties, and rainfall by every possible means.

For this reason, in their discussions of prayers for rain, the jurists mentioned what they did, not what they refrained from doing. Seeking nearness through him during his lifetime meant requesting his supplication and intercession. It was a form of asking him to supplicate for them, and this is prescribed. Throughout his lifetime, Muslims would ask the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, to supplicate for them.

After his death, however, the Companions did not ask him to supplicate, whether at his grave or elsewhere, as many people do at the graves of the righteous: one asks the deceased to fulfil his need, or adjures Allah by him, and so forth—even though accounts to this effect have been related from some later figures.

Rather, it is prescribed for any believer to ask another believer to supplicate for him. Indeed, when ‘Umar asked his permission to perform ‘umrah, the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said to him:

“Do not forget us in your supplications, my brother.”

—if authentic

---

## Page 329

as a hadith—and even “the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, instructed that Uways al-Qaranī be asked to seek forgiveness for the person making the request,” even if that person was far superior to Uways. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, also said in an authentic hadith:

“When you hear the caller to prayer, repeat what he says. Then invoke blessings upon me, for whoever invokes blessings upon me once, Allah blesses him ten times. Then ask Allah to grant me al-wasīlah, for it is a rank in Paradise befitting only one of Allah’s servants, and I hope to be that servant. Whoever asks Allah to grant me al-wasīlah will be entitled to my intercession on the Day of Resurrection.”

Yet his asking his community to supplicate for him is not a request for a created being to meet a need. Rather, he is teaching his community what benefits them in their religion. Through that teaching and their acting upon what he has taught them, Allah increases his reward. For when we invoke blessings upon him once, Allah blesses us ten times; and when we ask Allah to grant him al-wasīlah, we become entitled to his intercession on the Day of Resurrection. Whatever reward we receive for our deeds, he receives an equivalent reward, without our reward being diminished in any way. For he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Whoever calls others to guidance will receive a reward equal to the rewards of those who follow him, without their rewards being diminished in any way.”

He is the one who called his community to every good deed. For every good deed his community performs, he receives a reward equal to theirs, without their rewards being diminished in any way. For this reason, the Companions and the early generations did not dedicate the reward of their deeds to him, nor did they perform Hajj on his behalf

---

## Page 330

nor did they give charity or recite the Quran and dedicate the reward to him. For every prayer, fast, Hajj, act of charity, and Quran recitation performed by Muslims, he, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, receives a reward equal to theirs, without their rewards being diminished in any way. Parents, however, are different: not every good deed a Muslim performs brings his parents a reward equal to his. For this reason, he dedicates the reward to his parents and to others.

It is well known that the Messenger, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, obeys his Lord, Almighty and Majestic, in His words:

“So when you are free, strive diligently.”

“And turn to your Lord with longing.”

He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, therefore directs his longing to none but Allah. It is established in an authentic hadith that he said:

“Seventy thousand of my community will enter Paradise without being called to account. They are those who do not ask others to perform ruqyah (healing recitation) for them, do not undergo cauterization, do not believe in omens, and put their trust in their Lord.”

These people belong to his community, and he praised them for not seeking ruqyah. Seeking ruqyah means asking someone else to perform it for oneself, and ruqyah is a form of supplication. He, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, used to perform ruqyah for himself and for others, but he did not ask anyone to perform it for him. The version of this report that reads:

“They do not perform ruqyah”

is weak and erroneous. This helps clarify the true nature of his instruction to his community to supplicate: it is not a case of one created being asking another for something, which is inferior to asking Allah alone. For someone who does not ask people—but asks Allah alone—is superior to someone who asks people, and Muhammad, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is the master of the children of Adam.

A person’s supplication for another in their absence is more likely to be answered than supplication made in their presence, because it is more completely sincere and further removed from associating partners with Allah. How, then, can the supplication of someone who prays for another without being asked be likened to the supplication of

---

## Page 331

someone who supplicates to Allah at another’s request, in that person’s presence?

A hadith states:

“The supplication most likely to be answered is that made by one person for another while they are apart.”

In Sahīh Muslim, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, is reported to have said:

“Whenever a man supplicates for his brother in his absence, Allah appoints an angel over him. Each time he supplicates for his brother, the appointed angel says, ‘Amen, and may you have the same.’”

This is because a created being asks another created being for something the latter is capable of doing. A created being is capable of supplicating to Allah and asking Him. It is therefore permissible to ask someone to supplicate, just as one may ask him for assistance within his capacity or to perform actions he is capable of performing.

As for what only Allah Most High can do, it is permissible to ask only Allah, glorified be He, for it. Such things must not be sought from angels, prophets, or anyone else. It is not permissible to say to anyone other than Allah, “Forgive me,” “Send us rain,” “Grant us victory over the disbelieving people,” “Guide our hearts,” or anything similar.

For this reason, al-Tabarānī narrated in his Mu‘jam:

“During the time of the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, there was a hypocrite who harmed the believers. Al-Siddīq said, ‘Come, let us seek deliverance from this hypocrite through the Messenger of Allah, may Allah bless him and grant him peace.’ They came to him, and he said, ‘Deliverance is not to be sought from me; it is to be sought only from Allah.’”

The same principle applies to seeking assistance.

As for what human beings are capable of doing, it does not fall into this category. Allah, glorified be He, has said:

---

## Page 332

“When you sought help from your Lord, and He answered you.”

In the supplication of Moses, peace be upon him, we find:

“O Allah, to You belongs all praise; to You complaints are brought; Your assistance is sought; from You deliverance is sought; and in You we place our trust. There is no might or power except through You.”

Abū Yazīd al-Bistāmī said: “A created being seeking deliverance from another created being is like a drowning person seeking deliverance from another drowning person.”

Abū ‘Abd Allāh al-Qurashī said: “A created being seeking deliverance from another created being is like a prisoner seeking deliverance from another prisoner.”

Allah Most High has said:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim besides Him. They have no power to remove harm from you or to shift it elsewhere.’”

“Those whom they invoke themselves seek a means of drawing near to their Lord, striving to be the closest. They hope for His mercy and fear His punishment. Indeed, the punishment of your Lord is something to beware of.”

Some of the early generations explained that there were people who invoked angels and prophets, so Allah Most High said: “Those whom you invoke are My servants, just as you are My servants. They hope for My mercy, just as you hope for My mercy; they fear My punishment, just as you fear My punishment; and they seek to draw near to Me, just as you seek to draw near to Me.”

Thus, He, glorified be He, forbade invoking angels and prophets, while informing us that the angels supplicate for us and seek forgiveness for us. Nevertheless, we may not ask them to do so.

The same applies to prophets and righteous people. Even if they are alive in their graves, even if it is supposed that they supplicate for the living, and even if reports to that effect have been transmitted, no one may ask them to do so. None of the early generations did this, because it is a means leading to associating them with Allah and worshipping them instead of Allah Most High. This differs from asking one of them during his lifetime, for that does not lead to associating partners with Allah; and because what the angels do, and what the prophets and righteous people do after death, occurs by Allah’s command governing creation

---

## Page 333

so the requests of those who ask have no effect on it. This differs from asking one of them during his lifetime, when responding to the request is prescribed. After death, they are no longer subject to religious obligations.

Allah Most High has said:

“It is not for a human being to whom Allah has given the Scripture, judgment, and prophethood to then say to people, ‘Be servants of mine rather than Allah.’ Rather, ‘Be devoted scholars of the Lord through your teaching of the Scripture and your study of it.’”

“Nor would he command you to take the angels and prophets as lords. Would he command you to disbelieve after you have become Muslims?”

Thus, He, glorified be He, made clear that whoever takes the angels and prophets as lords is a disbeliever. Allah Most High has said:

“Say, ‘Call upon those whom you claim besides Allah. They do not possess even an atom’s weight in the heavens or on earth. They have no share in either, nor does He have any helper among them.’”

“Intercession before Him is of no benefit except for one to whom He has given permission.”

Allah Most High has said:

“Who can intercede with Him except by His permission?”

Allah Most High has said:

“There is no intercessor except after His permission.”

Allah Most High has said:

“You have no protector or intercessor besides Him.”

Allah Most High has said:

“They worship besides Allah that which neither harms nor benefits them, and say, ‘These are our intercessors with Allah.’ Say, ‘Are you informing Allah of something He does not know in the heavens or on earth?’ Glorified and exalted is He above what they associate with Him.”

Allah Most High has said concerning the man mentioned in Surah Yā Sīn:

“Why should I not worship the One who created me, and to whom you will be returned?”

“Should I take gods besides Him? If the Most Merciful intends harm for me, their intercession will avail me nothing, nor can they rescue me.”

“Then I would surely be in clear error.”

“Indeed, I have believed in your Lord, so listen to me.”

Allah Most High has said:

“Intercession before Him is of no benefit except for one to whom He has given permission.”

Allah Most High has said:

“On that Day, intercession will be of no benefit except for one whom the Most Merciful permits and whose words He approves.”

Allah Most High has said:

“They intercede only for those whom He approves, and they tremble in awe of Him.”

---

## Page 334

Thus, intercession is of two kinds:

The first is the intercession that Allah Most High has denied, such as that affirmed by the polytheists and by those ignorant and misguided people of this community who imitate them. This constitutes associating partners with Allah.

The second is for the intercessor to intercede with Allah’s permission. This is the intercession that Allah Most High has affirmed for His righteous servants. Hence, when people ask the foremost of intercessors to intercede on the Day of Resurrection, he will come forward and prostrate. He said:

“Then I will praise my Lord with praises He inspires me with, which I cannot offer now. Then it will be said: ‘O Muhammad, raise your head. Speak, and you will be heard; ask, and you will be given; intercede, and your intercession will be accepted.’”

Once he is permitted to intercede, he—may Allah bless him and grant him peace—will intercede for whomever Allah wishes him to intercede for.

Those who hold this view say: The permissibility of seeking a means through him and seeking his intercession—in the sense that he supplicates for the person seeking a means through him—does not entail that this is prescribed in his absence or after his death, when he has not supplicated for that person. Rather, the person seeking a means through him has sworn an oath to him or made a request by virtue of his person. Moreover, the Companions distinguished between the two cases.

This is because, during his lifetime, he himself supplicates for whoever seeks a means through him. His supplication to Allah, glorified be He, is the finest supplication offered by any created being, for he is the best of creation and the most honoured of them before Allah. His supplication and intercession for someone are therefore the finest supplication one created being can offer for another. How, then, can this be equated with the case of someone for whom the Messenger has neither supplicated nor interceded? Whoever equates someone for whom the Messenger has supplicated with someone for whom he has not, and treats these two ways of seeking a means as equivalent, is among the most misguided of people.

Furthermore, there is no harm in asking him to supplicate, in his offering that supplication, or in seeking a means through his supplication

---

## Page 335

—rather, it is entirely good, with no evil in it, and involves nothing objectionable or harmful. For none of the prophets, peace be upon them, was worshipped during his lifetime in his own presence: he would forbid anyone from worshipping him or associating him with Allah, even if this amounted only to minor shirk. Thus, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, forbade anyone who prostrated before him from doing so, and said:

“Do not say, ‘As Allah wills and Muhammad wills,’ but say, ‘As Allah wills, then as Muhammad wills.’”

There are other examples of this. After his death, however, there is reason to fear religious temptation and the association of him with Allah, just as the Messiah, Ezra and others were made partners with Allah at their graves. For this reason, the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Do not praise me excessively as the Christians praised Jesus, son of Mary. I am only a servant, so say: ‘Allah’s servant and Messenger.’”

Both compilers reported this in their Sahīh collections. He also said:

“O Allah, do not make my grave an idol that is worshipped.”

And he said:

“May Allah curse the Jews and Christians: they took the graves of their prophets as places of worship.”

He was warning against what they had done.

In short, we hold to two great principles. The first is that we worship none but Allah. The second is that we worship Him only in the ways He has prescribed, not through innovated forms of worship. These two principles fulfil the testimony that “there is no god but Allah and that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah.” As Allah Most High says:

“That He may test you as to which of you is best in deeds.”

Al-Fudayl ibn Iyād said: “The most sincere and the most correct.”

They asked: “Abū Alī, what do you mean by the most sincere and the most correct?”

He replied: “If a deed is sincere but not correct, it will not be accepted; and if it is correct but not sincere, it will not be accepted until it is both sincere and correct. Sincerity means that it is done for Allah, and correctness means that it accords with the Sunnah.”

This fulfils the words of Allah Most High:

“So whoever hopes to meet his Lord, let him do righteous deeds and associate no one in the worship of his Lord.”

---

## Page 336

And the Commander of the Faithful, Umar ibn al-Khattāb, used to say in his supplication to Him:

“O Allah, make all my deeds righteous, make them devoted solely to seeking Your Face, and let no one else have any share in them.”

Allah Most High says:

“Or do they have partners who have prescribed for them a religion that Allah has not authorized?”
